Qassim Internal Medicine OSCE — Summary

Pick a specialty. Each one has the core history, history add-ons, the exam, exam add-ons, then the cases.

Cardiology

How to use this

All 20 cardiology stations in the Qassim 2021–2025 set fall into five presentations: chest pain, palpitations, shortness of breath / HF, syncope, and pre-op assessment. Learn the core history and exam once, then add the block for the presentation in front of you.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • check stability first (ABCD, vitals, SpO2, GCS)
  • note what the patient is connected to (O2, monitor, IV line)
Stations covered
  • chest pain (inferior MI + RV infarct, anterior MI + AV block, type A and type B dissection, post-PCI pericarditis)
  • palpitations (AF ×4, VT post-MI, NSVT in cardiac sarcoid, HOCM ×2)
  • SOB (peripartum CMP, MS ×2, ADHF from NSAIDs, severe AS)
  • pre-op risk after DES
1. Core cardiac history (every case)
Presenting complaint
  • onset
  • duration
  • course
  • frequency
  • severity
  • triggers
  • relieving factors
  • previous similar episodes
Cardiac symptoms
  • chest pain
  • exertional dyspnea (NYHA class)
  • orthopnea (pillows)
  • PND
  • palpitations
  • presyncope / syncope
  • leg swelling
  • weight gain
  • fatigue
Risk factors
  • DM
  • HTN
  • dyslipidemia
  • smoking (pack-years, water pipe)
  • obesity
  • family history of premature CAD / sudden death
Known heart disease
  • IHD (MI, PCI — stent number, site, type DES vs BMS, CABG)
  • valve disease / rheumatic fever
  • cardiomyopathy
  • arrhythmia
  • pacemaker / ICD
  • valve replacement
  • last echo
Other systems
  • thyroid symptoms
  • anemia / bleeding
  • chest (cough, hemoptysis, fever)
  • stroke / TIA
  • CKD
  • OSA
  • CTD (SLE, RA)
  • constitutional symptoms
Drugs
  • antiplatelets (ASA, clopidogrel, ticagrelor)
  • anticoagulants
  • beta blockers
  • ACEi / ARB / ARNI
  • diuretics
  • nitrates
  • antiarrhythmics
  • insulin / hypoglycemics
  • salbutamol
  • thyroxine
  • OCP
  • NSAIDs
  • steroids
  • compliance
Social
  • smoking
  • alcohol
  • caffeine
  • cocaine / amphetamines / illicit drugs
  • functional status (stairs, ADLs)
  • occupation
Family
  • IHD
  • sudden cardiac death
  • cardiomyopathy
  • Marfan
  • bicuspid aortic valve
  • congenital heart disease
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Chest pain
SOCRATES
  • site
  • onset (sudden vs gradual)
  • character (crushing vs tearing vs pleuritic)
  • radiation (jaw / arm vs back)
  • associations
  • timing
  • exacerbating / relieving factors
  • severity /10
ACS
  • crushing retrosternal pain radiating to jaw / left arm
  • sweating, nausea
  • exertional
  • DM patients present atypically (epigastric pain)
  • prior IHD
Aortic dissection
  • sudden severe tearing pain to the back
  • uncontrolled HTN / non-compliance
  • cocaine
  • Marfan or bicuspid valve in family
  • CNS symptoms (stroke, syncope)
  • limb ischemia
  • recent aortic manipulation
Pericarditis
  • sharp, pleuritic, worse lying flat, relieved sitting forward
  • recent MI (days = early pericarditis, weeks = Dressler)
  • viral prodrome
  • CTD, uremia, TB
PE / other
  • long travel, immobilization, leg swelling
  • chest trauma (pneumothorax)
  • GI (PUD, esophagitis, cholecystitis)
  • costochondritis
B. Palpitations
Character
  • onset / offset (sudden = SVT / VT)
  • fast or slow
  • regular or irregular
  • duration
  • frequency
  • at rest or exertion
  • how it terminates
Red flags
  • syncope / presyncope
  • chest pain
  • HF symptoms
  • palpitations on exertion
  • family history of sudden death
Cardiac causes
  • prior MI (VT)
  • valve disease / rheumatic fever (AF with MS)
  • HOCM
  • cardiomyopathy
  • cardiac sarcoid
Non-cardiac causes
  • hyperthyroidism (tremor, heat intolerance, weight loss, diarrhea)
  • anemia / bleeding
  • hypoglycemia (insulin, sulfonylurea)
  • pheochromocytoma
  • anxiety / panic
  • caffeine, alcohol, nicotine
  • menopause
Drugs
  • beta agonists
  • pseudoephedrine
  • thyroxine
  • beta-blocker withdrawal
  • diuretics (low K / Mg)
  • QT-prolonging drugs
AF complications
  • stroke / TIA
  • bleeding on anticoagulation
C. Shortness of breath / heart failure
Dyspnea
  • onset
  • progression
  • NYHA I–IV
  • orthopnea
  • PND
Congestion
  • ankle / leg swelling
  • abdominal distension
  • RUQ discomfort
  • weight gain
  • reduced urine output
Precipitants
  • NSAIDs
  • TZDs
  • steroids
  • diuretic non-adherence
  • salt / fluid excess
  • new AF
  • ACS
  • infection
  • uncontrolled HTN
Cause of cardiomyopathy
  • ischemic (MI, PCI, CABG)
  • hypertensive
  • valvular (rheumatic fever)
  • peripartum (delivery within last months, preeclampsia)
  • viral
  • alcohol
  • thyroid
  • CTD
  • family history
Pregnancy
  • gestation
  • previous pregnancies
  • preeclampsia
Valve symptoms
  • exertional syncope and angina (AS)
  • hemoptysis, palpitations (MS)
D. Syncope / LOC
Event
  • what he was doing (exertion = HOCM / AS)
  • prodrome
  • duration
  • abnormal movements
  • incontinence
  • tongue bite
  • post-ictal confusion
  • injury
  • full recovery
Cardiac clues
  • palpitations before
  • exertional
  • chest pain
  • family history of SCD
Other
  • hypoglycemia
  • seizure
  • stroke / TIA
  • orthostasis
  • vasovagal triggers
E. Pre-operative assessment (after recent MI / stent)
Cardiac event
  • timing of MI
  • type of PCI
  • stent number
  • site
  • type (DES vs BMS)
Current status
  • chest pain
  • dyspnea
  • orthopnea
  • PND
  • palpitations
  • syncope
RCRI items
  • type of surgery
  • IHD
  • HF
  • stroke
  • DM on insulin
  • creatinine > 2 mg/dL
Functional capacity
  • stairs climbed
  • exercise (≥ 4 METs)
Medications
  • ASA
  • clopidogrel
  • beta blocker
  • ACEi
  • statin
  • insulin
3. Core cardiac exam (every case)
General
  • ill / well
  • distress
  • position (orthopneic)
  • sweating
  • pallor
  • cyanosis
  • jaundice
  • connections (O2, monitor)
Vitals
  • BP (both arms in chest pain)
  • HR
  • rhythm
  • RR
  • temperature
  • SpO2
  • glucose
Pulse
  • rate
  • rhythm (irregularly irregular)
  • volume
  • character (slow-rising, bounding)
  • radio-radial and radio-femoral delay
  • pulse deficit
Hands / face / neck
  • IE stigmata (splinters, Osler, Janeway)
  • clubbing
  • mitral facies
  • exophthalmos
  • thyroid
  • JVP (height, cannon a waves, absent a wave in AF)
Precordium inspection
  • sternotomy / thoracotomy scar
  • device (pacemaker / ICD)
  • visible pulsation
  • deformity (pectus)
Palpation
  • apex position and character (tapping, sustained, displaced, trifid)
  • thrill
  • parasternal heave
  • palpable S4
Auscultation
  • S1 (loud in MS, variable in AF)
  • S2 (soft in AS, loud P2 in pulmonary HTN)
  • S3 / S4
  • murmurs (timing, site, radiation, maneuvers)
  • opening snap
  • pericardial rub
Chest
  • basal crackles
  • effusion
Abdomen
  • hepatomegaly
  • ascites
  • pulsatile mass
Legs
  • pitting edema (level)
  • DVT signs
  • peripheral pulses
Neuro
  • focal deficit (dissection, AF embolism)
4. Exam add-ons by diagnosis
ACS / inferior MI
  • bradycardia and heart block
  • hypotension
  • raised JVP with clear chest = RV infarct
  • new murmur (MR, VSR)
  • crackles (Killip class)
Aortic dissection
  • BP differential between arms
  • pulse deficit
  • early diastolic murmur of AR
  • Marfan habitus (tall, pectus, arachnodactyly, high palate)
  • focal neurology
Pericarditis / tamponade
  • friction rub
  • Beck triad (hypotension, raised JVP, muffled heart sounds)
  • pulsus paradoxus
  • tachycardia
AF
  • irregularly irregular pulse, pulse deficit, variable S1, no a wave
  • look for cause (MS murmur, thyroid signs, HF)
Mitral stenosis
  • malar flush
  • tapping apex
  • loud S1
  • opening snap
  • mid-diastolic rumble at apex (left lateral position, bell)
  • loud P2
  • AF
Aortic stenosis
  • slow-rising low-volume pulse
  • narrow pulse pressure
  • sustained apex
  • soft S2
  • ejection systolic murmur right 2nd ICS radiating to carotids
HOCM
  • jerky pulse, double / trifid apex, S4, ejection murmur at LLSB louder with Valsalva / standing and softer with squatting
  • apical MR murmur (SAM)
Heart failure
  • orthopneic, raised JVP, displaced apex, S3 gallop, basal crackles, effusions, hepatomegaly, ascites, pitting edema
  • warm vs cold, wet vs dry
Cardiac sarcoid
  • erythema nodosum
  • lupus pernio
  • facial palsy
  • lymphadenopathy
  • lung crackles
Endocarditis screen (any murmur)
  • fever
  • splinter hemorrhages
  • Osler nodes
  • Janeway lesions
  • Roth spots
5. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

Chest pain
1. Inferior-posterior MI + RV infarct + complete heart block (75M, DM, epigastric pain)
Clues
  • epigastric pain > 3 h radiating to chest, neck, left arm
  • sweating, nausea, dizziness
  • heavy smoker
  • HR 40, BP 90/40, raised JVP, clear chest
DDx
  • MI
  • pericarditis
  • PUD
  • cholecystitis
  • costochondritis
  • esophageal spasm
  • esophagitis
Workup
  • ECG (12-lead + right-sided V4R, posterior V7–V9)
  • cardiac markers (troponin, CK-MB)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Results
  • ECG: ST elevation II, III, aVF + ST depression V1–V3 (posterior) + complete heart block (HR 40) + ST elevation V4R
  • BP 90/40
  • echo: EF ≈ 40%, inferior and posterior hypokinesia, no valve disease
Diagnosis
  • acute inferior-posterior STEMI complicated by RV infarction and complete heart block
Management
  • aspirin load
  • clopidogrel / ticagrelor load
  • heparin
  • IV fluids (RV infarct)
  • cardiology
  • coronary angiography for PCI
  • temporary pacemaker
  • CCU
Examiner Qs
  • nitrates contraindicated in RV infarct (preload dependent)
  • long term = aspirin, clopidogrel, beta blocker, ACEi / ARB, statin, cardiac rehab, education, follow-up
2. Anterior STEMI + AV block (65M, DM, HTN, smoker)
Clues
  • crushing pain radiating to neck, jaw, left arm
  • sweating
  • HR 50, BP 95/60
  • few crackles
DDx
  • ACS
  • aortic dissection
  • PE
  • aortic stenosis
  • pericarditis
  • costochondritis
Workup
  • ECG
  • cardiac markers (troponin, CK-MB)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, aPTT)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
  • coronary angiography
Results
  • troponin positive
  • ECG: ST elevation I, aVL, V1–V6 with RBBB and AV block (HR 45–50, QRS 162 ms)
  • BP 95/60
  • echo: EF 35% with LAD-territory hypokinesia
Management
  • aspirin load
  • clopidogrel / ticagrelor load
  • heparin
  • O2
  • cardiology
  • activate cath lab for primary PCI
  • temporary pacemaker
After PCI
  • CCU
  • DAPT
  • beta blocker
  • ACEi / ARB / ARNI
  • statin
  • permanent pacemaker if AV block persists > 24 h
MI complications
  • post-MI angina
  • HF
  • cardiogenic shock
  • pulmonary edema
  • acute MR (papillary muscle)
  • VSR
  • free wall rupture
  • LV thrombus
  • pericarditis
  • Dressler syndrome
  • arrhythmias
Discharge advice
  • smoking cessation
  • medication compliance
  • clinic follow-up
  • cardiac rehab
  • BP and glucose control
3. Type B aortic dissection (65M, HTN non-compliant, smoker)
Clues
  • sudden severe tearing pain radiating to back
  • BP 170/80
  • no deficit, no murmur
DDx
  • aortic dissection
  • ACS
  • PE
  • pericarditis
  • myocarditis
  • AS
  • costochondritis
Workup
  • ECG
  • cardiac markers (troponin)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN — before contrast)
  • coagulation + group and crossmatch
  • imaging (CXR, CT angiography of the aorta; MRA or TEE if CT not possible)
Results
  • ECG and troponin normal
  • CXR: wide mediastinum
  • CTA: dissection flap in the descending thoracic aorta (Stanford B)
Management
  • IV beta blocker first (esmolol, labetalol)
  • then nitroprusside
  • analgesia (morphine)
  • cardiology and vascular surgery referral
Long term
  • oral beta blocker, ACEi / ARB or CCB
  • surveillance CTA / MRA
4. Type A aortic dissection + Marfan (40–45M)
Clues
  • crushing retrosternal pain to back
  • BP right arm 180/68 vs left 150/55
  • pectus excavatum
  • early diastolic murmur (AR)
DDx
  • aortic dissection
  • ACS
  • PE
  • pneumothorax
Workup
  • ECG
  • cardiac markers (troponin)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • liver profile
  • coagulation + crossmatch
  • toxicology (cocaine)
  • imaging (CXR, CT angiography; echo / TEE if unstable)
Results
  • BP right arm 180/68 vs left arm 150/55, HR 101
  • ECG: inferior ST elevation (flap into the right coronary)
  • echo: dilated aortic root with AR, hyperdynamic EF > 60%
  • CXR: wide mediastinum / double aortic contour
  • CTA: flap in the ascending aorta
Diagnosis
  • Stanford type A (ascending) dissection
Management
  • ICU
  • analgesia
  • urgent cardiac surgery
  • HR ≈ 60 and SBP < 110 with IV beta blocker (esmolol, labetalol) then vasodilator (nitroprusside, nicardipine)
  • screen first-degree relatives
Marfan major features
  • skeletal (pectus, scoliosis, arachnodactyly)
  • ocular (ectopia lentis)
  • cardiovascular (aortic root dilatation, dissection)
Classification
  • Stanford A = ascending ± arch (surgery)
  • Stanford B = distal to left subclavian (medical unless complicated)
  • DeBakey I (ascending + descending), II (ascending only), III (descending)
5. Post-MI acute pericarditis (58M, day 1 after anterior MI + PCI)
Clues
  • sharp retrosternal pain to trapezius, worse lying and inspiration, relieved sitting forward
  • friction rub
  • no JVP rise
DDx
  • early pericarditis
  • stent thrombosis
  • free wall rupture
  • tamponade
Workup
  • ECG
  • cardiac markers (troponin, NT-proBNP)
  • CBC (with differential)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • liver profile
  • metabolic (HbA1c, TFT, iron)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Results
  • ECG: diffuse concave ST elevation + PR depression (PR elevation in aVR)
  • WBC 12,000/µL (12 × 10⁹/L)
  • Hb 13 g/dL (130 g/L)
  • platelets 400,000/µL (400 × 10⁹/L)
  • HbA1c 5.3% (34 mmol/mol)
  • TFT normal
  • CXR: bottle-shaped heart
  • echo: EF 55%, mild–moderate pericardial effusion
Diagnosis (2 of 4)
  • typical pain
  • rub
  • ECG changes
  • new / worsening effusion
ECG stages
  • diffuse STE + PR depression → normalization → diffuse T inversion → T normalization
Management
  • aspirin 600–1200 mg q8h tapered over 2 weeks
  • colchicine 0.5 mg BID (once daily if < 60 kg) for 3 months
  • CCU and pericardiocentesis if tamponade
Complications
  • tamponade
  • constrictive pericarditis
  • myopericarditis
Tamponade signs
  • Beck triad (hypotension, raised JVP, muffled heart sounds)
  • pulsus paradoxus
  • tachycardia
Palpitations and arrhythmia
6. Fast AF in IHD (75M)
Clues
  • exertional palpitations with syncope and sweating
  • HR 140 irregularly irregular, BP 100/60
  • no a wave
  • variable S1
  • crackles
DDx
  • IHD
  • VHD
  • HF
  • hyperthyroidism
  • HTN
  • PE
  • OSA
  • COPD
  • alcohol
  • cardiomyopathy
  • constrictive pericarditis
Workup
  • ECG + Holter
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • coagulation (PT / INR)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH, free T4, glucose)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Results
  • ECG: AF, HR 140, irregularly irregular, no P waves
  • BP 100/60
  • TSH 3 mIU/L, T3 / T4 normal
  • echo: EF 40%, anterior wall hypokinesia, no valve disease
Management
  • treat reversible causes
  • rate vs rhythm control
  • thromboprophylaxis (warfarin vs NOAC)
AF types
  • valvular / non-valvular
  • paroxysmal (< 7 days)
  • persistent (> 7 days)
  • long-standing persistent (> 1 year)
  • permanent (no further rhythm control)
Scores
  • stroke = CHADS2 / CHA2DS2-VASc
  • bleeding = HAS-BLED (or HEMORR2HAGES, ATRIA)
AF complications
  • HF
  • tachycardia-induced CMP
  • ischemia
  • sudden death
  • stroke / TIA
  • mesenteric ischemia
  • bleeding on anticoagulation
7. AF with CKD (67F, DM, HTN)
Clues
  • recurrent sudden onset / offset palpitations
  • HR 120
  • BMI 31
  • on insulin, lisinopril, thiazide
Workup
  • ECG
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN — for DOAC dosing)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • liver profile
  • metabolic / endocrine (glucose, HbA1c, TSH)
  • imaging (echo)
Results
  • ECG: AF, HR 120
  • CBC normal
  • creatinine high with eGFR < 30 mL/min/1.73 m²
  • random glucose high
  • CHA2DS2-VASc 4 (HTN, age, DM, female)
Management
  • admit
  • rate or rhythm control (TEE before cardioversion)
  • beta blocker or CCB (no CCB if EF < 40%)
  • target HR < 110 at rest
  • anticoagulate (apixaban preferred over warfarin)
  • control HTN and DM
  • weight loss
CHA2DS2-VASc
  • CHF 1, HTN 1, Age ≥ 75 = 2, DM 1, Stroke / TIA = 2, Vascular disease 1, Age 65–74 = 1, Sex female 1
  • this patient = 4 (HTN, age, DM, female)
Anticoagulate regardless of score
  • HOCM
  • hyperthyroidism
  • rheumatic MS
  • mechanical valve
8. AF + rheumatic MS — warfarin counseling (50F and 62F)
Clues
  • irregular rapid palpitations, LOC 3 s
  • tapping apex, mid-diastolic rumble at apex
  • crackles
DDx
  • AF
  • valvular disease
  • hyperthyroidism
  • hypoglycemia
  • TIA
  • anxiety
Workup
  • ECG
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • coagulation (INR — baseline for warfarin)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH, HbA1c, iron)
  • imaging (CXR, echo, CT brain if neuro symptoms)
Results
  • ECG: AF, HR 106
  • WBC 7000/µL (7 × 10⁹/L)
  • Hb 13 g/dL (130 g/L)
  • platelets 400,000/µL (400 × 10⁹/L)
  • HbA1c 7% (53 mmol/mol)
  • TFT and iron normal
  • echo: dilated LA, moderate–severe rheumatic MS
  • CT brain normal
Management
  • ABCD
  • rate control
  • anti-failure treatment
  • cardiology
  • anticoagulation (warfarin, as MS = valvular AF)
Counseling steps
  • introduce, private setting, ask what she knows
  • ideas, concerns, expectations
  • explain why (stroke risk cut ≈ 70%)
  • how to take it with heparin bridging at start
  • INR target 2–3 with monitoring
  • side effects (bleeding, GI upset, rash, alopecia)
  • alternatives (NOAC not for MS)
Precautions
  • consistent vitamin K intake (green vegetables, green tea)
  • drug and herb interactions (antibiotics, Bactrim)
  • avoid NSAIDs / aspirin / multivitamins with K
  • acetaminophen is safe
  • tell dentist
  • electric razor
  • avoid pregnancy (teratogenic)
Safety net
  • report bleeding (bruises, melena, epistaxis, severe headache) or overdose
  • carry warfarin card
  • contact numbers, leaflets
  • check understanding
  • thank her
9. Mitral stenosis + AF + pulmonary HTN (45F, DM, HTN)
Clues
  • intermittent palpitations 2 years
  • progressive exertional dyspnea and orthopnea
  • loud S1, diastolic murmur at apex, loud P2, basal crackles
DDx
  • MS
  • AF
  • pulmonary HTN
  • HF
  • hyperthyroidism
  • anemia
Workup
  • ECG
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • coagulation (INR)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH, free T4, HbA1c)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Results
  • ECG: AF
  • Hb 12.5 g/dL (125 g/L)
  • WBC 6 × 10⁹/L
  • platelets 254 × 10⁹/L
  • INR 1.1
  • creatinine 92 µmol/L (1.04 mg/dL)
  • urea 6 mmol/L (BUN ≈ 17 mg/dL)
  • Na 145 mmol/L
  • K 4.0 mmol/L
  • Mg 0.8 mmol/L (1.9 mg/dL)
  • TSH 3.1 mIU/L
  • free T4 20 pmol/L (1.55 ng/dL)
  • CXR: enlarged pulmonary artery
  • echo: rheumatic MS (moderate), RVSP 45 mmHg
Management
  • rate control
  • anticoagulation
  • diuretic
  • rhythm control (cardioversion, ablation) if still symptomatic
Examiner Qs
  • CHA2DS2-VASc not used in valvular AF (anticoagulate anyway)
  • AF types = paroxysmal, persistent, permanent
10. Sustained monomorphic VT after old MI (48M)
Clues
  • episodes 15–30 s for 4 weeks, today 5 min, on exertion
  • thrombolysed MI 3 years ago
  • water-pipe smoker
  • cannon a waves, variable S1
Workup
  • ECG (regular wide-complex tachycardia)
  • CBC (Hb)
  • electrolytes (K, Mg, Ca)
  • cardiac markers (troponin)
  • later — ambulatory ECG, exercise ECG, imaging (echo, coronary angiography), electrophysiology study
Acute (stable VT)
  • DC cardioversion (class I)
  • IV procainamide (IIa)
  • IV amiodarone (IIb)
Long term
  • amiodarone
  • coronary angiography ± revascularization
  • EP study and ablation
  • ICD
  • cardiology
VT vs SVT with aberrancy
  • AV dissociation
  • fusion / capture beats
  • QRS > 140 ms
  • extreme axis
  • precordial concordance favor VT
11. NSVT from cardiac sarcoidosis (39M)
Clues
  • lung and skin sarcoid on prednisolone
  • palpitations with presyncope
  • irregular pulse
DDx
  • cardiac sarcoid NSVT
  • ischemic / dilated CMP
  • ARVC
  • idiopathic NSVT
  • low K / Mg
  • drug-induced
Workup
  • ECG + Holter
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • ACE level
  • cardiac markers (troponin, NT-proBNP)
  • renal profile + electrolytes (Na, K, Mg, Ca)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH)
  • imaging (echo, cardiac MRI with late gadolinium, FDG-PET, CT chest)
  • electrophysiology study
Results
  • Holter: NSVT
  • echo: mildly reduced EF with basal septal thinning
  • MRI: patchy mid-wall / subepicardial LGE
  • PET: active myocardial FDG uptake
Management
  • admit, monitor, correct electrolytes
  • optimize steroids ± methotrexate / azathioprine
  • beta blocker
  • HF therapy if EF < 50%
  • ICD
  • repeat CMR / PET
  • MDT
  • driving restrictions
Poor prognosis
  • syncope
  • VT / NSVT
  • low EF
  • AV block
  • extensive LGE
  • persistent PET uptake
ICD in sarcoid
  • secondary prevention (VT / VF, arrest)
  • primary if NSVT with LV dysfunction or extensive LGE / PET uptake
12. HOCM (20M, syncope while playing football; two stations)
Clues
  • exertional syncope
  • palpitations
  • sustained / trifid apex, S4
  • ejection murmur at LLSB louder with Valsalva / standing
  • apical MR murmur
DDx
  • HOCM
  • AS
  • arrhythmia (AF, VT)
  • myocarditis
  • seizure
  • anemia
  • hyperthyroidism
Workup
  • ECG + Holter
  • CBC
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Mg)
  • cardiac markers (troponin, NT-proBNP)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH, HbA1c)
  • imaging (echo, cardiac MRI, CT brain if syncope with head injury)
  • exercise test
  • genetic testing
Results
  • HR 106 irregular (AF)
  • WBC 7000/µL (7 × 10⁹/L)
  • Hb 13 g/dL (130 g/L)
  • platelets 400,000/µL (400 × 10⁹/L)
  • HbA1c 5.3% (34 mmol/mol)
  • TFT normal
  • ECG: LVH, inferior pseudo-Q waves, deep / giant T inversion
  • Holter: paroxysmal AF
  • echo: asymmetric septal hypertrophy, LVOT gradient 40 mmHg, SAM with moderate MR, dilated LA, EF 55%
Management
  • cardiology
  • avoid dehydration and competitive sport
  • beta blocker
  • alcohol septal ablation or surgical myectomy
  • ICD if indicated
  • anticoagulate AF regardless of CHA2DS2-VASc
  • transplant if end-stage
ICD indications
  • survived SCD / VF / sustained VT
  • family history of SCD
  • unexplained syncope
  • septum ≥ 30 mm
  • apical aneurysm
  • EF ≤ 50%
  • extensive LGE on CMR
Shortness of breath and valve disease
13. Peripartum cardiomyopathy (32F, 2 weeks after delivery)
Clues
  • SOB NYHA 2–3, leg swelling
  • HR 102
  • raised JVP, S3, basal crackles, edema
DDx
  • dilated
  • ischemic
  • hypertrophic
  • hypertensive
  • valvular
  • viral
  • metabolic (thyroid)
  • systemic (SLE, toxins) CMP
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca, Mg)
  • liver profile
  • cardiac markers (troponin, CK-MB, BNP)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH, free T4)
  • ECG
  • imaging (CXR, echo; cardiac MRI / CT coronary / right heart cath / biopsy if unclear)
Results
  • Hb 9 g/dL (90 g/L)
  • electrolytes, renal, LFT, TSH normal
  • troponin 0.02 ng/mL (negative)
  • BNP 900 pg/mL
  • CXR: cardiomegaly with congestion
  • echo: EF 30%, global hypokinesia, dilated LV, normal RV
Management
  • low salt, fluid restriction, exercise training
  • ACEi / ARB (after delivery), ARNI, beta blocker, spironolactone, diuretics, SGLT2i, digoxin, hydralazine + nitrate
  • ICD / CRT-D
  • cardiology
Prognosis
  • about 50% recover fully
  • persistent LV dysfunction or EF < 25% at presentation = high risk in the next pregnancy
14. Acute decompensated HF from NSAIDs (55M, IHD)
Clues
  • 3 days SOB, orthopnea, PND, 2–3 kg weight gain
  • ibuprofen TID for knee OA
  • raised JVP, S3, crackles, hepatomegaly, edema
DDx
  • ADHF
  • ACS
  • new AF
  • PE
  • decompensated liver disease
  • nephrotic / renal failure
  • ARDS
  • venous insufficiency
Workup
  • ECG
  • urine dipstick
  • cardiac markers (troponin, NT-proBNP)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • liver profile
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH)
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Results
  • BP 150/90, HR 95, RR 22
  • ECG: sinus tachycardia
  • NT-proBNP high
  • renal: mild AKI, K 4.2 mmol/L
  • CXR: cardiomegaly, upper-lobe diversion, Kerley B lines, bilateral effusions
  • echo: EF 35%, global hypokinesia
Management
  • stop NSAIDs
  • O2 to 94–98%
  • sit upright
  • strict fluid balance and daily weights
  • IV furosemide
  • GDMT (ACEi / ARNI, beta blocker, MRA, SGLT2i) once euvolemic
  • HF nurse
  • cardiology
Examiner Qs
  • drugs precipitating ADHF = NSAIDs, TZDs, steroids, diuretic non-adherence
  • NSAIDs cause Na / water retention and reduce renal perfusion
  • ICU if cardiogenic shock, severe hypoxia, inotropes, acute renal failure
  • monitor urine output, weight, BP, electrolytes, renal function
15. Severe mitral stenosis in pregnancy (35F, 5 months)
Clues
  • 4 weeks SOB NYHA 2–3, orthopnea, palpitations
  • tapping apex, loud S1, opening snap, mid-diastolic rumble
  • basal crackles
DDx
  • MS
  • tachycardia CMP
  • PE
  • hyperthyroidism
  • anemia
  • pulmonary HTN
  • HF
  • asthma
  • pneumonia
Workup
  • ECG
  • CBC
  • renal profile + electrolytes
  • cardiac markers (troponin, NT-proBNP)
  • metabolic / endocrine (TSH)
  • imaging (echo — avoid CXR / CT in pregnancy unless essential)
Results
  • paroxysmal AF
  • NT-proBNP 600 pg/mL
  • troponin negative
  • echo: rheumatic mitral valve, mean gradient 11 mmHg, valve area 0.9 cm² (severe MS)
Management
  • beta blocker and diuretics
  • anticoagulation
  • balloon mitral commissurotomy if medical therapy fails
PBMC contraindications
  • LA thrombus
  • moderate–severe MR
  • Wilkins score > 8
Anticoagulate in MS if
  • AF
  • prior embolism
  • LA thrombus
  • marked LA enlargement
Rheumatic prophylaxis
  • 10 years or until age 40
  • whichever is longer
16. Severe aortic stenosis (70M)
Clues
  • 3 months exertional SOB, angina-like pain, exertional syncope
  • slow-rising low-volume pulse
  • sustained apex
  • soft S2
  • ejection murmur right 2nd ICS
DDx
  • AS
  • ACS
  • HOCM
  • aortic sclerosis
  • myocarditis
  • pericarditis
  • PE
Workup
  • ECG
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes
  • cardiac markers (NT-proBNP)
  • metabolic (HbA1c, lipids)
  • imaging (CXR, echo; CT / coronary angiography before valve replacement)
Results
  • BP 130/75, HR 80
  • ECG: LVH
  • echo: calcified aortic valve, area 0.8 cm², mean gradient 45 mmHg, peak velocity 5 m/s, EF 45%
Causes of AS
  • senile calcific
  • bicuspid valve
  • congenital (subaortic membrane)
  • rheumatic
  • endocarditis
  • drugs
Management
  • treat risk factors
  • low-dose diuretic
  • avoid strenuous exercise
  • aortic valve replacement (surgical vs TAVI)
Intervene if
  • severe symptomatic AS
  • severe asymptomatic with EF < 50%
  • severe AS having other cardiac surgery
Examiner Qs
  • mechanical AVR INR target 2–3
  • TAVI complications = death, stroke, vascular injury, valve embolization, paravalvular leak, AV block, tamponade, arrhythmia
Pre-operative assessment
17. Cholecystectomy after recent DES (64M, DM, HTN, MI 5 months ago)
Clues
  • STEMI 5 months ago with one DES in LAD
  • asymptomatic, climbs 2 flights, jogs daily
  • on ASA, clopidogrel, bisoprolol, lisinopril, atorvastatin, insulin
Risk tool
  • Revised Cardiac Risk Index (Lee)
RCRI items
  • high-risk surgery
  • IHD
  • HF
  • cerebrovascular disease
  • DM on insulin
  • creatinine > 2 mg/dL
Workup
  • ECG
  • imaging (echo if none within a year, CXR)
  • CBC
  • coagulation (PT / INR, aPTT)
  • renal profile (creatinine — RCRI cut-off > 2 mg/dL = 177 µmol/L)
Results
  • HR 65, BP 110/70
  • CBC, coagulation, CXR normal
  • ECG: sinus rhythm with Q waves V1–V2
  • echo: anteroseptal hypokinesia, EF 55%, normal pulmonary pressure
Anticipated complications
  • stent thrombosis
  • ACS
  • death
  • stroke
  • pulmonary edema
  • arrhythmias
Recommendation
  • elective surgery
  • so postpone until 12 months after DES
Then
  • monitored bed after surgery
  • cardiology
  • stop clopidogrel ≥ 5 days before
  • continue aspirin, beta blocker, ACEi, statin
  • avoid volume overload
  • daily ECG and troponin ± BNP after surgery
Questions asked across several stations
AF scores
  • CHA2DS2-VASc for stroke
  • HAS-BLED for bleeding
  • not used in valvular AF
AF rate control target
  • HR < 110 at rest
  • beta blocker or diltiazem
  • avoid CCB if EF < 40%
Anticoagulation choice
  • NOAC (apixaban) preferred for non-valvular AF
  • warfarin for MS or mechanical valve
Warfarin INR targets
  • 2–3 for AF and mechanical AVR
MI drugs at discharge
  • DAPT, beta blocker, ACEi / ARB / ARNI, statin
  • rehab
  • risk factor control
Tamponade
  • Beck triad and pulsus paradoxus
ICD (HOCM, sarcoid, post-MI VT)
  • secondary prevention after VT / VF
  • primary prevention by disease-specific risk markers
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

ECG — 75M, DM, epigastric pain (case 1)
Findings
  • ST elevation in II, III, aVF (III > II)
  • reciprocal ST depression in I and aVL
  • ST depression with upright T waves in V1–V3 (posterior extension)
  • slow rate
Diagnosis
  • acute inferior-posterior STEMI (RCA occlusion) — do right-sided leads (V4R) for RV infarct
  • watch for complete heart block
ECG — 65M, DM, chest pain (case 2)
Findings
  • machine read: ventricular rate 45, QRS 162 ms, right bundle branch block, anteroseptal infarct
  • ST elevation across the anterior leads
  • slow ventricular rate with AV block
Diagnosis
  • acute anterior STEMI with new RBBB and high-grade AV block (proximal LAD) → primary PCI ± temporary pacing
ECG — 45M, HTN, tearing chest pain to the back (case 4)
Findings
  • ST elevation in II
  • III
  • aVF with reciprocal ST depression in I and aVL
Diagnosis
  • inferior STEMI pattern caused by type A dissection extending into the right coronary ostium — no thrombolysis or heparin until dissection is excluded
CXR — same patient (case 4)
Findings
  • AP erect film
  • widened superior mediastinum
  • prominent aortic contour
Diagnosis
  • suspected aortic dissection → urgent CT angiography
CT angiography of the chest — same patient (case 4)
Findings
  • dilated ascending aorta with an intimal flap separating true and false lumens
  • flap also seen in the descending aorta
Diagnosis
  • Stanford type A (DeBakey I) aortic dissection → emergency surgery
ECG — 58M, chest pain 1 day after anterior MI + PCI (case 5)
Findings
  • sinus rhythm
  • diffuse concave (saddle-shaped) ST elevation in I, II, III, aVF, V2–V6
  • PR depression
  • ST depression and PR elevation in aVR
  • no reciprocal changes
Diagnosis
  • acute (early post-infarction) pericarditis
ECG — palpitations clinic, rheumatic MS (case 8)
Findings
  • irregularly irregular narrow-complex rhythm
  • no discernible P waves
  • ventricular rate ≈ 100–110
Diagnosis
  • atrial fibrillation with rapid ventricular response (valvular AF with MS → warfarin, CHA2DS2-VASc not used)
ECG — 50F, DM, HTN, palpitations and LOC (case 8)
Findings
  • irregularly irregular rhythm
  • no P waves
  • fibrillatory baseline
  • narrow QRS
  • ventricular rate ≈ 80
Diagnosis
  • atrial fibrillation with controlled ventricular rate
ECG — 67F, recurrent palpitations and dizziness (case 7)
Findings
  • irregularly irregular narrow-complex rhythm
  • no P waves
  • fibrillatory waves best in V1
  • rate ≈ 100
Diagnosis
  • atrial fibrillation (CHA2DS2-VASc 4 → anticoagulate; dose-adjust for CKD)
ECG — 48M, palpitations, old MI (case 10)
Findings
  • regular broad-complex tachycardia ≈ 190/min
  • QRS > 140 ms
  • uniform (monomorphic) morphology
  • extreme axis (negative in I, II, III, aVF)
  • no visible P waves
Diagnosis
  • sustained monomorphic ventricular tachycardia (scar-related) → DC cardioversion if unstable
  • procainamide / amiodarone if stable
ECG — 20M, syncope playing football (case 12)
Findings
  • sinus rhythm
  • very tall R waves in V4–V6 (LVH by voltage)
  • deep symmetric T-wave inversion in V4–V6 and I
Diagnosis
  • hypertrophic cardiomyopathy (LVH with giant / deep T inversion — apical pattern) → echo for septal thickness and LVOT gradient
CXR — 55M, IHD, dyspnea and leg swelling (case 14)
Findings
  • sternotomy wires (prior CABG)
  • cardiomegaly
  • upper-lobe venous diversion
  • perihilar haziness
  • Kerley B lines (arrows)
  • fluid in the horizontal fissure
  • blunted costophrenic angles
Diagnosis
  • acute pulmonary edema / decompensated heart failure

Pulmonology

How to use this

The pulmonology stations fall into five presentations: chronic dyspnea (ILD, COPD, pulmonary hypertension), cough (asthma, bronchiectasis, HP), hemoptysis / mass (cancer, TB), pleural effusion, and acute dyspnea / chest pain (PE, CAP, ARDS). Learn the core history and the respiratory exam once, then add the block for the presentation.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • vitals with SpO2
  • note what the patient is connected to (O2, nebulizer, inhalers)
  • check for respiratory distress before anything else
Stations covered
  • ARDS from COVID
  • CTEPH ×2 (one with APS)
  • idiopathic PAH
  • IPF ×2
  • COPD ×2
  • asthma
  • CF bronchiectasis
  • post-TB bronchiectasis
  • hypersensitivity pneumonitis
  • Pancoast adenocarcinoma (EGFR)
  • squamous lung cancer with hypercalcemia
  • pulmonary TB
  • CAP
  • parapneumonic effusion
  • hepatic hydrothorax
  • PE with APS → CAPS
  • respiratory system exam ×2
1. Core respiratory history (every case)
Dyspnea
  • onset
  • duration
  • course
  • continuous vs intermittent
  • triggers
  • relieving factors
  • severity (mMRC / NYHA / WHO class)
  • at rest or exertion
Cough
  • onset
  • duration
  • dry vs productive
  • diurnal variation (night = asthma, morning = bronchiectasis)
  • triggers (exercise, dust, perfumes, cold, work, season)
Sputum and blood
  • sputum amount and color
  • hemoptysis (amount, frequency, fresh vs streaks)
Associated
  • wheeze
  • chest pain (pleuritic or not)
  • orthopnea
  • PND
  • leg swelling
  • palpitations
  • syncope
  • hoarseness
Red flags / constitutional
  • fever
  • night sweats
  • weight loss
  • loss of appetite
  • hemoptysis
  • hoarseness
  • bone pain
Systemic review
  • CTD (joint pain, rash, Raynaud, dry eyes / mouth, muscle weakness, oral ulcers, skin thickening)
  • ENT (rhinitis, postnasal drip, sinusitis)
  • GERD
  • renal (urine color, frothy urine)
  • GI (jaundice, distension)
Past history
  • asthma
  • COPD
  • TB (treated?)
  • recurrent pneumonia / childhood infections
  • VTE
  • cancer
  • heart failure
  • CKD
  • liver disease
  • autoimmune disease
  • previous admissions / ICU / intubation
Drugs
  • inhalers and technique
  • steroids
  • anticoagulants
  • ACEi (cough)
  • beta blockers
  • NSAIDs
  • amiodarone / methotrexate / nitrofurantoin / bleomycin (ILD)
  • OCP / HRT (VTE)
  • allergies
Exposures
  • smoking (pack-years), water pipe, alcohol
  • occupation (dust, asbestos, silica, metal / wood dust, farming)
  • birds / pigeons / animals
  • radiation
  • TB contact
  • sick contacts
  • travel
Family and social
  • lung disease, familial IPF, CF, atopy, TB, cancer, thrombophilia
  • marital status, children / infertility (CF, PCD)
  • vaccination
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Chronic exertional dyspnea (ILD / PH / COPD)
ILD
  • dry cough, progressive dyspnea
  • occupational dust, birds (HP), drugs (amiodarone, MTX, nitrofurantoin, bleomycin), radiation
  • CTD symptoms
  • family history of IPF
  • GERD
Pulmonary hypertension
  • exertional chest tightness, fatigue, presyncope / syncope, ankle swelling
  • previous DVT / PE
  • CTD (scleroderma)
  • liver disease (portopulmonary)
  • HIV
  • appetite suppressants
  • OCP
  • congenital heart disease
Obstetric (young woman)
  • miscarriages, stillbirths, preeclampsia → APS
  • plan for pregnancy (PH = avoid pregnancy)
COPD
  • heavy smoking
  • chronic productive cough
  • exacerbations and admissions
  • inhaler use
  • home O2
B. Chronic cough
Asthma
  • nocturnal, intermittent, triggered by exercise / dust / perfumes
  • wheeze
  • atopy
  • ER visits needing nebulization
Upper airway / GERD
  • rhinitis
  • nasal drip
  • sinusitis
  • heartburn
Drugs
  • ACEi
  • beta blockers
Bronchiectasis
  • copious morning sputum
  • recurrent infections
  • previous TB
  • childhood pertussis / measles
  • recurrent sinusitis
  • infertility (CF, Kartagener)
  • immune deficiency
Hypersensitivity pneumonitis
  • pigeons / birds, farming, molds
  • symptoms improve away from exposure
C. Hemoptysis / lung mass
Bleed
  • amount (tablespoon or more = significant)
  • frequency
  • fresh
  • with sputum
Cancer
  • smoking pack-years, weight loss, hoarseness, bone pain, neck / shoulder pain to the ulnar fingers + ptosis (Pancoast)
  • paraneoplastic (hypercalcemia — thirst, constipation, abdominal pain, confusion; SIADH; Cushing)
TB
  • night sweats
  • weight loss
  • contact
  • origin from an endemic country
  • previous treatment
Other
  • bleeding elsewhere (hematologic)
  • epistaxis / hematuria (GPA, Goodpasture)
  • calf pain (PE)
  • anticoagulants
  • mitral stenosis
D. Pleural effusion
Effusion
  • pleuritic chest pain, dyspnea
  • preceding URTI / fever (parapneumonic)
Transudate causes
  • HF (orthopnea, PND)
  • cirrhosis (jaundice, distension, alcohol, hepatitis)
  • nephrotic / CKD
Exudate causes
  • TB
  • malignancy (weight loss)
  • CTD
  • PE
  • trauma
  • asbestos
E. Acute dyspnea / pleuritic chest pain (PE / CAP / ARDS)
PE
  • calf pain / swelling
  • immobilization
  • long travel
  • surgery
  • trauma
  • cancer
  • previous VTE
  • OCP / HRT
  • pregnancy
  • recent COVID vaccine (VITT)
  • fetal losses (APS)
  • family thrombophilia
Pneumonia
  • fever
  • productive cough
  • pleuritic pain
  • sick contact
  • recent hospitalization
  • travel
  • aspiration risk
Severe respiratory failure
  • COVID contact, inability to talk
  • neuromuscular weakness (GBS, MG), myositis
  • asthma / COPD history
3. Respiratory system exam (dedicated station, 80 marks)
Start
  • introduce, permission, wash hands
  • position at 45° (front) or sitting upright with hands crossed on shoulders (back)
  • ask about pain
  • expose to the waist
From the foot of the bed
  • well or ill, distress, position, O2 / nebulizer, accessory muscles, RR, audible wheeze, cyanosis, GCS
  • legs for edema, DVT, erythema nodosum (sarcoid)
  • sacral edema
Vitals
  • BP
  • HR
  • RR
  • temperature
  • SpO2
Hands
  • clubbing
  • tar stains
  • muscle wasting (Pancoast)
  • tremor / flapping tremor (CO2 retention)
  • cyanosis
  • palmar erythema
  • joint deformity (RA)
Pulse
  • rate
  • AF
  • bounding (CO2 retention)
  • pulsus paradoxus (severe asthma)
Face
  • pallor, jaundice, central cyanosis
  • Horner syndrome (ptosis, miosis)
  • oral candidiasis (inhaled steroids)
Neck
  • JVP
  • trachea position and cricosternal distance
  • cervical, supraclavicular and axillary nodes
Inspection
  • symmetry
  • scars (sternotomy, chest-drain scar in the axilla, thoracotomy / lobectomy)
  • radiotherapy skin changes, telangiectasia
  • deformity (pectus excavatum / carinatum, barrel chest, kyphoscoliosis)
Palpation
  • apex beat
  • chest expansion at three levels (symmetric or not)
  • tactile vocal fremitus
Percussion
  • supraclavicular (apices), infraclavicular, chest wall, axillae, compare side to side
  • tidal percussion for the diaphragm
Auscultation
  • same areas front and back
  • vocal resonance
  • loud P2
  • TR murmur
Close
  • thank the patient and cover
  • offer CVS exam, PEFR, sputum pot
Examiner Qs
  • bronchial breathing with increased vocal resonance = consolidation, cavity / abscess, mass, collapse with patent bronchus, bronchiectasis
  • Consolidation vs effusion: both dull (effusion stony dull)
  • vocal resonance increased in consolidation, reduced / absent in effusion
4. Exam add-ons by presentation
ILD
  • clubbing, bibasal end-inspiratory Velcro crackles, squeaks (HP)
  • CTD signs (arthritis, skin thickening, rash), uveitis, parotids, nodes (sarcoid)
  • signs of PH
Pulmonary hypertension
  • raised JVP with prominent a and v waves
  • RV heave
  • loud / palpable P2
  • pansystolic TR murmur louder on inspiration
  • right S3
  • tender hepatomegaly
  • ascites
  • ankle edema
  • central cyanosis
COPD
  • barrel chest
  • hyperresonance
  • reduced breath sounds
  • expiratory wheeze
  • pursed lips
  • flapping tremor
  • cor pulmonale
Asthma
  • expiratory wheeze
  • check ability to speak, PEFR
Bronchiectasis / CF
  • clubbing, coarse crackles, low BMI, nasal polyps
  • dextrocardia (Kartagener)
Effusion
  • reduced expansion, stony dullness, reduced breath sounds and vocal resonance
  • trachea shifted away if large
  • look for the cause (liver stigmata, ascites, raised JVP, nodes, breast)
Consolidation (CAP)
  • bronchial breathing and crackles, increased vocal resonance
  • confusion, BP, RR for CURB-65
Cancer
  • clubbing
  • cachexia
  • nodes
  • Horner
  • wasting of hand muscles
  • collapse / effusion
  • hepatomegaly
  • bone tenderness
  • neurologic signs (paraneoplastic, brain mets)
PE / APS
  • DVT signs (swollen warm tender calf, dilated superficial veins)
  • livedo reticularis
  • loud P2
  • raised JVP
  • SLE signs (malar rash, joints)
Respiratory failure / ARDS
  • nasal flaring, accessory muscles, tracheal tug, paradoxical abdominal breathing
  • skin and limbs for a source of infection
  • glucose and urine dipstick
5. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → key positives → workup and results → diagnosis → management → examiner's questions.

Pulmonary vascular disease
1. CTEPH (45F, exertional dyspnea 1 year, PE 1 year ago on rivaroxaban)
Clues
  • WHO class II–III, chest pain, fatigue
  • BP 100/55, HR 110, SpO2 91%
  • JVP with prominent a wave
  • loud palpable P2
  • TR murmur louder on inspiration
  • hepatomegaly
  • leg edema
DDx
  • pulmonary hypertension
  • CHF
  • CAP
  • COPD
  • bronchiectasis
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • cardiac (troponin, NT-proBNP, ECG, echo)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • blood gas
  • imaging (CXR, CT pulmonary angiography, V/Q scan)
  • physiology (PFT with DLCO, 6-minute walk test)
  • immunology (autoimmune profile, thrombophilia / APS screen)
  • right heart catheterization
Results
  • WBC 8.55 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 14 g/dL (140 g/L)
  • renal and LFT normal
  • troponin normal
  • pro-BNP 1,200 pg/mL
  • autoimmune negative
  • DLCO low
  • 6MWT 300 m
  • echo EF > 55%, RV dilated, moderate TR, PASP 55 mmHg
  • ECG RA enlargement, RVH, strain
  • CXR prominent main PA, peripheral pruning
  • V/Q multiple mismatched defects
  • RHC mPAP 40 mmHg, PCWP 12 mmHg
Diagnosis
  • chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension (WHO group 4)
Management
  • diuretics
  • continue lifelong anticoagulation
  • pulmonary endarterectomy if operable
  • riociguat if inoperable or persistent PH after surgery
  • refer to a pulmonary hypertension center
2. CTEPH due to APS (46F, 18 months of dyspnea, unprovoked PE, 2 miscarriages)
Clues
  • WHO class III, chest tightness, presyncope, ankle swelling
  • SpO2 90%, central cyanosis
  • JVP with prominent a wave
  • parasternal heave
  • loud P2
  • TR murmur
  • tender hepatomegaly
DDx
  • pulmonary hypertension
  • heart failure
  • ILD
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • coagulation
  • cardiac (ECG, echo, BNP)
  • blood gas
  • physiology (PFT, 6MWT)
  • imaging (CXR, CTPA, V/Q scan)
  • immunology (autoimmune profile, APS — lupus anticoagulant, anticardiolipin, anti-β2-GPI)
  • right heart catheterization
Results
  • CBC, renal, liver, coagulation normal
  • BNP 2,000 pg/mL
  • lupus anticoagulant and anticardiolipin positive
  • ECG right axis, RVH, RBBB
  • CXR enlarged pulmonary arteries
  • V/Q multiple segmental mismatched defects
  • RHC mPAP 48 mmHg, PCWP 12 mmHg, high PVR
  • SpO2 90%
Diagnosis
  • CTEPH secondary to antiphospholipid syndrome (WHO group 4)
Risk factors for CTEPH after PE
  • APS / thrombophilia
  • inadequate anticoagulation
  • large or recurrent PE
  • chronic inflammation or infection

4. PE with APS → catastrophic APS (30F, sudden pleuritic chest pain, 6 hours)
Clues

  • 3 third-trimester fetal losses
  • HR 110, RR 25, SpO2 92%
  • raised JVP
  • loud P2
  • swollen warm tender left calf with dilated veins and livedo reticularis
DDx
  • PE / DVT due to APS
  • HF
  • IHD
  • pericarditis
  • pneumonia
  • pneumothorax
  • aortic dissection
Workup
  • pretest probability (Wells / modified Geneva)
  • CBC
  • coagulation (PT, PTT)
  • renal profile (before contrast)
  • liver profile
  • pregnancy test
  • blood gas
  • D-dimer
  • cardiac (ECG, troponin, BNP, echo)
  • APS screen
  • imaging (CTPA, lower-limb Doppler)
Results
  • pregnancy test negative
  • CBC, renal, PT, LFT normal
  • PTT prolonged (lupus anticoagulant)
  • ABG hypoxemia (SpO2 92% → 85%)
  • D-dimer, BNP and troponin raised
  • APS screen positive
  • ECG sinus tachycardia with nonspecific ST–T changes
  • echo RV strain
  • DVT left common femoral vein
  • CTPA filling defect in the right main pulmonary artery
  • then BP 80/40 mmHg for 30 min
Then
  • hypotension 80/40 for 30 min despite resuscitation = high-risk (massive) PE → IV thrombolysis if no contraindication
Thrombolysis contraindications
  • prior ICH
  • structural cerebrovascular lesion
  • intracranial tumor
  • ischemic stroke within 3 months (unless within 4.5 h)
  • suspected aortic dissection
  • active bleeding
  • bleeding diathesis
  • head / facial trauma within 3 months
APS (revised Sapporo / Sydney)
  • ≥ 1 clinical + ≥ 1 lab
  • clinical = vascular thrombosis or pregnancy morbidity (≥ 1 fetal death ≥ 10 weeks, ≥ 1 premature birth < 34 weeks from preeclampsia / placental insufficiency, ≥ 3 consecutive losses < 10 weeks)
  • lab = anticardiolipin IgG / IgM, anti-β2-GPI IgG / IgM, lupus anticoagulant (repeat after 12 weeks)
Day 4
  • cerebral venous thrombosis + mesenteric ischemia = catastrophic APS
CAPS treatment
  • therapeutic heparin
  • high-dose steroids
  • IVIG and / or plasma exchange
  • rituximab if refractory
Interstitial lung disease

6. IPF (63M smoker, 2 years of dyspnea, now at rest)

  • clubbing

  • blood gas
  • CBC (eosinophils)

  • liver profile

  • SpO2 88%
  • ABG hypoxemia with hypocapnia
  • PFT FVC 1.93 L (56%), FEV1 2.03 L (60%), FEV1/FVC 93%, TLC 3.21 L (53%), DLCO 42% → restrictive with low DLCO

HRCT hallmarks (UIP)
  • subpleural, basal predominance
  • reticulation
  • honeycombing
  • traction bronchiectasis
  • no features of another diagnosis (extensive ground glass, nodules, consolidation)
Risk factors
  • age > 50
  • male

  • dust (wood, metal, silica)

Steroids

  • short course only in acute exacerbation
Antifibrotics
  • slow FVC decline by about 50% and reduce exacerbations but do not reverse fibrosis
Monitoring
  • PFT (FVC, DLCO), SpO2, 6MWT, symptoms, imaging if needed
  • cancer screening, vaccines
Prognosis
  • median survival 3–5 years
  • most die of respiratory failure

  • coagulation (PT / PTT — before biopsy)

Horner syndrome
  • ipsilateral ptosis
  • miosis
  • anhidrosis
  • enophthalmos (sympathetic chain invasion)

  • adenocarcinoma → young non-smoker → request molecular testing (EGFR, ALK, ROS1; also PD-L1, KRAS, BRAF)

  • MDT. The checklist says neoadjuvant chemo → surgery → radiotherapy with no adjuvant role

  • DVT prophylaxis
  • broad-spectrum antibiotics
  • chest tube if large, pH < 7.2, pus or positive Gram stain / culture
  • intrapleural tPA / DNase or VATS if loculated
18. Hepatic hydrothorax (60M retired soldier, dyspnea 3 weeks)
Clues
  • smoker, BMI 35
  • dry cough
  • clubbing, palmar erythema, jaundice
  • reduced right expansion, stony dullness, reduced breath sounds right mid-lower zone
  • distended abdomen with ascites and dilated abdominal veins
DDx
  • pleural effusion
  • lung cancer
  • liver cirrhosis
  • HF
  • CAP
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile + albumin + total protein
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT)
  • cardiac (enzymes, pro-BNP, echo)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • serum LDH
  • autoimmune profile
  • TB (PPD / QuantiFERON)
  • viral and autoimmune hepatitis screen
  • urinalysis
  • imaging (CXR, chest US, CT chest, abdominal US with Doppler)
  • diagnostic thoracentesis (cell count, protein, LDH, glucose, pH, culture, cytology, ADA)
Results
  • WBC 9 ×10⁹/L
  • cardiac enzymes and pro-BNP normal
  • LFT and bilirubin high
  • INR 2
  • albumin 3 g/dL (30 g/L)
  • serum total protein 5 g/dL (50 g/L)
  • LDH 70 U/L
  • ESR 30 mm/h
  • CRP 10 mg/L
  • PPD / QuantiFERON positive (latent only)
  • autoimmune and hepatitis screen negative
  • US shrunken nodular liver with large ascites
  • fluid clear straw-colored, WBC 150, normal pH and glucose, fluid / serum protein < 0.4, LDH ratio 0.3 → transudate
Fluid
  • clear straw-colored, WBC 150, normal pH and glucose
  • protein ratio < 0.5, LDH ratio 0.3 = transudate
Diagnosis
  • transudative effusion from cirrhosis = hepatic hydrothorax
Management
  • hepatology referral and work up the cause of cirrhosis
  • treat ascites (salt restriction + diuretics)
  • education, nutrition, vaccines
  • therapeutic thoracentesis for symptoms
  • TIPS if refractory
  • liver transplant
  • avoid chest tube (renal failure, protein loss, infection, circulatory collapse)
Complications
  • spontaneous bacterial empyema
  • fibrothorax / trapped lung
  • respiratory failure
Critical care
19. ARDS from COVID-19 pneumonia (32M, cough 2 days, now dyspneic)
History asks
  • cough and sputum
  • fever, chest pain
  • NYHA
  • recent flu-like illness, sick or COVID contact
  • CVS, renal, autoimmune (rash, arthralgia, conjunctivitis)
  • asthma, DM, HTN
  • previous ICU
  • drugs, smoking
Exam
  • GCS, vitals, distress signs (nasal flaring, accessory muscles, tracheal tug, abdominal breathing)
  • full chest, CVS, CNS, volume and abdomen
  • skin / MSK for a source
  • glucose, urine dipstick
DDx
  • type 1 respiratory failure / ARDS from COVID
  • asthma or COPD exacerbation
  • neuromuscular disease (GBS, MG)
  • inflammatory myositis
Workup
  • blood gas + lactate
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • electrolytes (K, Mg, Ca, PO4)
  • cardiac (ECG, CK, troponin, D-dimer)
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, LDH, ferritin)
  • infection (blood / urine / sputum cultures, COVID and respiratory viral PCR)
  • imaging (CXR)
Results
  • accessory-muscle use
  • PaO2 52 mmHg (6.9 kPa)
  • lactate 4 mmol/L (36 mg/dL)
  • CXR bilateral alveolar-interstitial infiltrates
  • COVID PCR positive
  • after 1 h on NIV SpO2 82% with paradoxical breathing → intubated
Diagnosis
  • type 1 respiratory failure / ARDS due to COVID-19 pneumonia
Management
  • ABC
  • ICU
  • NIV / HFNC
  • dexamethasone
  • antibiotics if bacterial infection suspected
  • antiviral (remdesivir / favipiravir per MOH)
  • electrolytes
  • DVT and GI prophylaxis
  • ID consult
After NIV failure (SpO2 82%, paradoxical breathing)
  • intubate and ventilate
Lung-protective ventilation
  • tidal volume 4–6 (up to 8) mL/kg ideal body weight
  • plateau pressure ≤ 30 cmH2O
  • SpO2 target 88–94%
  • PEEP ≥ 5 to prevent atelectrauma
Non-ventilatory measures
  • treat the cause
  • permissive hypercapnia (pH ≥ 7.25)
  • neuromuscular blockade in severe ARDS
  • prone positioning (PaO2/FiO2 < 150)
  • conservative fluids
Tocilizumab
  • within 24 h of ICU admission when O2 need escalates to HFNC
  • NIV or MV
  • and CRP ≥ 75 mg/L
Questions asked across several stations
Bronchial breathing + increased vocal resonance
  • consolidation
  • cavity / abscess
  • mass
  • collapse with patent bronchus
  • bronchiectasis
Effusion vs consolidation
  • both dull
  • effusion stony dull with absent vocal resonance and breath sounds
  • consolidation increased resonance and bronchial breathing
PH workup sequence
  • echo (PASP) → ECG / CXR → PFT with DLCO → V/Q (exclude CTEPH) → CTD / HIV / liver → right heart cath (mPAP > 20, PCWP ≤ 15 = precapillary)
Light's criteria (exudate if any)
  • fluid / serum protein > 0.5
  • fluid / serum LDH > 0.6
  • fluid LDH > two-thirds of the upper limit of normal serum LDH
Smoking-related, high-yield
  • LDCT screening criteria
  • COPD mortality measures
  • squamous cancer → hypercalcemia
  • small cell → SIADH / Cushing / LEMS
Vaccines in chronic lung disease
  • influenza
  • pneumococcal
  • COVID
  • RSV (≥ 60)
  • pertussis
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

CXR + V/Q scan — 45F, exertional dyspnea, PE 1 year ago (case 1)
Findings
  • CXR: prominent main pulmonary artery and right heart border, peripheral oligemia (pruning)
  • V/Q: multiple segmental perfusion defects with normal ventilation (mismatched)
Diagnosis
  • chronic thromboembolic disease → right heart catheterization confirmed CTEPH (mPAP 40 mmHg, PCWP 12 mmHg)
ECG — same patient (case 1)
Findings
  • sinus tachycardia
  • peaked P waves (right atrial enlargement)
  • right axis deviation
  • dominant R in V1 (RV hypertrophy)
  • T-wave inversion V1–V3 (RV strain)
Diagnosis
  • right ventricular hypertrophy with strain → pulmonary hypertension
ECG — 46F, progressive dyspnea, PE and 2 miscarriages (case 2)
Findings
  • right axis deviation
  • RV hypertrophy
  • RBBB pattern with T inversion in V1–V3
Diagnosis
  • right heart strain from pulmonary hypertension
CXR — same patient (case 2)
Findings
  • enlarged central pulmonary arteries
  • prominent right heart border
  • clear lung fields
Diagnosis
  • pulmonary hypertension → V/Q (mismatched defects) + RHC (mPAP 48 mmHg, PCWP 12 mmHg) = CTEPH due to APS
CXR — 65M smoker, dry cough 3 years, SpO2 87% (case 5)
Findings
  • bilateral diffuse reticular (interstitial) opacities
  • basal and peripheral predominant
  • reduced lung volumes
Diagnosis
  • interstitial lung disease → confirm with HRCT
HRCT — same patient (case 5)
Findings
  • subpleural, basal-predominant honeycombing, reticulation and traction bronchiectasis
  • no ground glass or nodules
Diagnosis
  • usual interstitial pneumonia (UIP) pattern → idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis
PFT + HRCT — 63M smoker, dyspnea now at rest, SpO2 88% (case 6)
Findings
  • FVC 1.93 L (56%), FEV1 2.03 L (60%), FEV1/FVC 93%, TLC 3.21 L (53%), DLCO 42% → restrictive defect with low diffusion
  • HRCT: extensive peripheral honeycombing and traction bronchiectasis
Diagnosis
  • IPF (UIP) → antifibrotic (nintedanib / pirfenidone)
  • O2
  • rehab
  • transplant referral
CXR + HRCT — 55M pigeon breeder, dry cough 10 months (case 7)
Findings
  • CXR: bilateral fine reticular / reticulonodular opacities
  • HRCT: diffuse centrilobular micronodules, ground glass and mosaic attenuation (air trapping)
  • PFT restrictive with low DLCO
Diagnosis
  • hypersensitivity pneumonitis (bird fancier's lung) → antigen avoidance ± steroids
HRCT + CXR — 66M, 40 pack-years, dyspnea and cough (case 8)
Findings
  • CT: upper-zone centrilobular and paraseptal emphysema with thin-walled lucencies
  • CXR: hyperinflated lungs (> 6 anterior ribs), flattened diaphragms, narrow heart
  • spirometry obstructive, not reversible
Diagnosis
  • COPD (emphysema)
  • GOLD 3 group B
HRCT — 57M, 40 pack-years, mMRC 4 (case 9)
Findings
  • bilateral upper-lobe centrilobular emphysema (areas of low attenuation without walls) with paraseptal changes
Diagnosis
  • emphysema (COPD)
CXR — same patient (case 9)
Findings
  • hyperinflation
  • flat hemidiaphragms
  • increased retrosternal space
  • long narrow heart
  • attenuated peripheral vessels
Diagnosis
  • COPD (emphysema) → LAMA + LABA
  • rehab
  • LVRS / home O2 assessment
Spirometry, flow-volume loop and CXR — 18M, nocturnal cough and exercise dyspnea (case 10)
Findings
  • FVC 3.15 L (89%)
  • FEV1 2.07 L (66%)
  • FEV1/FVC 65% (obstruction)
  • after bronchodilator FEV1 2.52 L = +14% and +450 mL (significant: ≥ 12% and ≥ 200 mL)
  • scooped expiratory loop that improves after bronchodilator
  • CXR normal
Diagnosis
  • reversible airflow obstruction → bronchial asthma
CXR + HRCT — 24M, productive cough > 2 years, infertile (case 11)
Findings
  • CXR: hyperinflation, bronchial wall thickening, ring and tram-track shadows
  • HRCT: bilateral cystic bronchiectasis with thick-walled dilated bronchi (signet-ring sign) and mucus plugging
  • sweat chloride 70 mmol/L (≥ 60 = diagnostic)
Diagnosis
  • cystic fibrosis bronchiectasis
HRCT + spirometry — 55M, treated TB, copious morning sputum (case 12)
Findings
  • right upper lobe cystic / varicose bronchiectasis with volume loss and fibrosis
  • FEV1 1.36 L (51%), FEV1/FVC 45%, post-bronchodilator change 3% (not reversible)
Diagnosis
  • post-TB bronchiectasis with fixed airflow obstruction
CXR + CT chest — 35F non-smoker, arm pain to ring / little fingers, ptosis (case 13)
Findings
  • CXR: right apical opacity
  • CT: right apical (superior sulcus) soft-tissue mass invading the posterior chest wall and paravertebral region
Diagnosis
  • Pancoast (superior sulcus) tumor with Horner syndrome → biopsy: EGFR-mutant adenocarcinoma
CXR — 66M, 35 pack-years, hemoptysis, Ca 3.0 mmol/L (case 14)
Findings
  • large well-circumscribed rounded mass in the left mid / lower zone projecting through the heart
Diagnosis
  • lung mass → CT
CT chest (mediastinal and lung windows) — same patient (case 14)
Findings
  • large lobulated soft-tissue mass in the left lower lobe abutting the descending aorta / posterior pleura
Diagnosis
  • primary lung cancer
  • likely squamous cell (smoker + PTHrP hypercalcemia)
CXR + CT — 45M from India, hemoptysis, AFB positive (case 15)
Findings
  • CXR: patchy multifocal consolidation in the right upper zone
  • CT: right upper lobe consolidation with nodules / tree-in-bud and early cavitation
Diagnosis
  • active pulmonary tuberculosis → airborne isolation
  • RIPE + pyridoxine
CXR — 62M, fever, pleuritic pain, confusion, BP 88/52 (case 16)
Findings
  • right lower zone air-space consolidation with air bronchograms
Diagnosis
  • right lower lobe community-acquired pneumonia (CURB-65 = 4 → ICU)
CXR — 42M, dyspnea, pleuritic pain after URTI (case 17)
Findings
  • right-sided pleural effusion — homogeneous opacity of the right lower and mid zone with a meniscus (arrow)
  • blunted costophrenic angle
Diagnosis
  • pleural effusion → tap: exudate
  • pH 7.27
  • glucose 55 mg/dL (≈ 3.1 mmol/L) = complicated parapneumonic effusion
CXR — 60M with cirrhosis and ascites, dyspnea 3 weeks (case 18)
Findings
  • large right pleural effusion with meniscus, displacing the mediastinum slightly to the left
  • left lung clear
Diagnosis
  • right-sided transudate in cirrhosis = hepatic hydrothorax
CXR — 32M, COVID-19, intubated (case 19)
Findings
  • endotracheal tube and lines in place
  • bilateral diffuse patchy alveolar / ground-glass opacities, peripheral and lower-zone predominant
  • no effusion, normal heart size
Diagnosis
  • ARDS from COVID-19 pneumonia (bilateral infiltrates, PaO2 52 mmHg, not cardiogenic) → lung-protective ventilation
  • prone

Gastroenterology & Hepatology

How to use this

The 20 GI stations cluster around five presentations: jaundice / abnormal LFTs, chronic diarrhea (IBD vs celiac), GI bleeding, upper abdominal pain, and dysphagia. The abdominal exam is examined on its own three times, and the ascites exam once.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • in bleeding or confused patients secure ABC, two large-bore lines and vitals with postural BP first
Stations covered
  • jaundice (cholangitis, acetaminophen ALF, PBC, chronic HBV, hemochromatosis, cirrhotic ascites)
  • diarrhea (Crohn's ×3, UC ×2, celiac ×4 incl. abdominal exam)
  • bleeding (massive UGIB from PUD ×2)
  • pain (hypertriglyceridemic pancreatitis)
  • cancer (esophageal cancer with breaking bad news, right colon cancer)
1. Core GI history (every case)
Presenting complaint
  • onset
  • duration
  • course (intermittent, continuous, progressive)
  • severity
  • previous similar episodes
Pain
  • SOCRATES
  • relation to food
  • radiation to back (pancreas) or shoulder (biliary)
Upper GI
  • nausea
  • vomiting (content, blood, coffee-ground)
  • heartburn
  • dyspepsia
  • dysphagia
  • odynophagia
  • early satiety
Lower GI
  • bowel habit change
  • diarrhea
  • constipation
  • blood / mucus
  • melena
  • tenesmus
  • incontinence
Liver
  • jaundice
  • dark urine
  • pale stool
  • pruritus
  • abdominal distension
  • leg swelling
  • confusion
  • bleeding / bruising
Constitutional
  • fever
  • weight loss (how much, how fast)
  • anorexia
  • night sweats
  • fatigue
Extraintestinal
  • mouth ulcers
  • red eyes
  • joint / back pain
  • skin rash (erythema nodosum, pyoderma, dermatitis herpetiformis)
Past history
  • GERD
  • PUD
  • gallstones
  • IBD
  • celiac
  • CLD
  • DM
  • autoimmune disease
  • radiation
  • previous abdominal surgery / ERCP / cholecystectomy
  • blood transfusion
Drugs
  • NSAIDs
  • aspirin
  • anticoagulants
  • steroids
  • bisphosphonates
  • PPI
  • antibiotics
  • metformin
  • iron
  • laxatives
  • hepatotoxins (acetaminophen, anti-TB, antiepileptics, methotrexate, statins, amiodarone, herbals)
Social
  • alcohol (units, CAGE)
  • smoking
  • IV drugs
  • tattoos
  • sexual history
  • travel
  • sick contacts
  • eating outside
  • diet
  • occupation
Family
  • colon cancer
  • polyps
  • IBD
  • celiac
  • viral hepatitis
  • Wilson
  • hemochromatosis
  • GI malignancy
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Jaundice / abnormal LFTs
Pattern
  • prehepatic (hemolysis — anemia, dark urine, SCD, thalassemia, Gilbert) vs hepatocellular vs cholestatic (pruritus, pale stool, dark urine)
Biliary / cholangitis
  • RUQ pain
  • fever with rigors (Charcot triad)
  • vomiting
  • gallstones
  • previous ERCP
Viral hepatitis risk
  • transfusion
  • IV drugs
  • tattoos
  • dialysis
  • unprotected sex
  • needle-stick
  • family member with HBV
  • travel
  • vaccination status
Drug / toxin
  • acetaminophen overdose (time, amount, number of tablets)
  • depression or suicidal intent
  • herbal remedies
  • mushrooms
  • ecstasy
Autoimmune
  • arthralgia, rash, vitiligo, other autoimmune disease (RA, thyroid, Sjögren, celiac)
  • IBD (PSC)
Metabolic / genetic
  • obesity, DM, dyslipidemia (NAFLD)
  • family history of cirrhosis (hemochromatosis — skin darkening, DM, arthropathy of 2nd–3rd MCPs, impotence)
  • Wilson (young, neuro / psychiatric)
Decompensation
  • distension
  • edema
  • GI bleed
  • confusion
  • sleep reversal
Other
  • pregnancy
  • HF (congestive hepatopathy)
  • immunosuppression / HIV
B. Chronic diarrhea
Type
  • watery (large volume)
  • fatty (bulky, greasy, hard to flush)
  • inflammatory (blood, mucus, small volume, fever, tenesmus)
Red flags
  • nocturnal diarrhea
  • blood
  • weight loss
  • anemia
  • age > 50
  • family history of CRC / IBD
Triggers
  • gluten
  • dairy
  • fasting response (osmotic stops with fasting)
  • stress
IBD clues
  • RLQ pain
  • perianal pain / discharge / fistula
  • mouth ulcers
  • back pain
  • arthritis
  • red eyes
  • skin rash
  • smoking (Crohn)
Celiac clues
  • bloating
  • weight loss
  • iron deficiency
  • itchy blistering rash on knees / elbows / scalp
  • other autoimmune disease
Infection
  • travel
  • sick contacts
  • recent antibiotics (C. diff)
  • amoebiasis
  • TB contact
  • HIV risk
Other causes
  • thyroid
  • DM
  • radiation
  • bowel surgery
  • chronic pancreatitis
  • microscopic colitis
  • bile acid diarrhea after cholecystectomy
  • drugs
C. GI bleeding
Upper vs lower
  • hematemesis / coffee-ground / melena vs fresh blood / maroon stool (maroon + shock can still be upper)
Amount and effect
  • number of episodes
  • volume
  • dizziness
  • syncope
  • confusion
Causes
  • NSAIDs
  • aspirin
  • steroids
  • H. pylori
  • alcohol / CLD (varices)
  • retching (Mallory-Weiss)
  • weight loss (malignancy)
  • anticoagulants
  • bleeding disorder
Exclude
  • ENT or mouth bleeding swallowed
D. Upper abdominal pain / pancreatitis
Pain
  • sudden epigastric pain radiating to back, relieved leaning forward
  • vomiting
Causes
  • gallstones
  • alcohol (binge, CAGE)
  • hypertriglyceridemia (DM, obesity, family history)
  • recent ERCP
  • drugs (azathioprine, valproate, thiazides, furosemide, 5-ASA, metronidazole, tetracycline)
  • trauma
  • hypercalcemia
Mimics
  • inferior MI
  • perforated ulcer
  • dissection
  • DKA
  • cholangitis
E. Dysphagia
Level
  • oropharyngeal (initiating, choking, nasal regurgitation) vs esophageal (food sticks after swallowing)
Pattern
  • solids then liquids = mechanical (cancer, stricture)
  • solids and liquids from start = motility (achalasia, scleroderma)
Progression
  • progressive vs intermittent (ring, EoE)
  • food impaction
Associated
  • weight loss
  • heartburn
  • odynophagia
  • hematemesis
  • regurgitation
  • chest pain
Risk factors
  • smoking
  • alcohol
  • long GERD (Barrett)
  • corrosive ingestion
  • atopy (EoE)
  • stroke
  • scleroderma
  • pill esophagitis (NSAIDs, bisphosphonates, KCl, doxycycline)
3. Abdominal examination (examined as its own station ×3)
Preparation
  • wash hands
  • introduce
  • explain
  • consent
  • privacy
  • expose nipples to symphysis
  • lie flat with one pillow
  • ask about pain
General
  • look
  • mental state
  • BMI
  • vitals
Hands
  • clubbing
  • leukonychia
  • koilonychia
  • palmar erythema
  • Dupuytren
  • arthropathy
  • flapping tremor
  • bruising
  • scratch marks
  • wasting
Face
  • jaundice, pallor, Kayser-Fleischer rings, parotid enlargement
  • mouth for fetor, angular stomatitis, glossitis, ulcers, thrush, leukoplakia
Neck and chest
  • cervical nodes (Virchow node)
  • spider nevi, gynecomastia, loss of body hair
Inspection
  • shape
  • distension
  • scars
  • fistulae
  • stoma
  • striae
  • pigmentation
  • dilated veins (caput medusae)
  • visible peristalsis
  • umbilicus
  • pulsation
  • hernia on coughing
  • divarication
Palpation
  • light then deep in 9 regions
  • liver and spleen from right iliac fossa (roll to right for spleen)
  • kidneys bimanually and ballotted
Percussion
  • liver span
  • Traube space
  • shifting dullness
  • fluid thrill
Auscultation
  • bowel sounds
  • rubs over organs
  • venous hum
  • renal bruits
Complete by mentioning
  • hernial orifices, genitalia, rectal exam, leg edema, neuro exam for alcohol, thyroid
  • thank patient, cover, wash hands, present findings
4. Exam add-ons by diagnosis
Chronic liver disease
  • jaundice
  • clubbing
  • leukonychia
  • palmar erythema
  • Dupuytren
  • spider nevi
  • gynecomastia
  • testicular atrophy
  • parotids
  • caput medusae
  • splenomegaly
  • ascites
  • edema
  • asterixis
  • fetor
Acute liver failure
  • GCS and encephalopathy grade
  • asterixis
  • rigidity
  • clonus / hyporeflexia
  • papilledema
  • tender hepatomegaly
  • bruising
  • petechiae
  • drug rash
  • herpetic lesions
Cholangitis / cholecystitis
  • fever
  • jaundice
  • RUQ tenderness
  • Murphy sign
  • peritonism
  • hypotension
IBD
  • low BMI
  • pallor
  • mouth ulcers
  • uveitis / episcleritis
  • clubbing
  • erythema nodosum
  • pyoderma
  • arthritis
  • spinal stiffness (AS)
  • RLQ mass
  • perianal fistula / skin tags / abscess on PR
Celiac
  • low BMI
  • pallor
  • dermatitis herpetiformis (scalp, knees, elbows)
  • angular stomatitis
  • glossitis
GI bleeding
  • postural drop
  • tachycardia
  • capillary refill
  • pallor
  • epigastric tenderness
  • PR for melena / fresh blood
  • CLD stigmata
Pancreatitis
  • epigastric tenderness
  • Cullen / Grey-Turner bruising
  • jaundice
  • eruptive xanthomas (high TG)
  • dehydration
Dysphagia / cancer
  • cachexia
  • Virchow node
  • hepatomegaly
  • mouth
  • neuro exam
Hemochromatosis
  • bronze pigmentation (face, forearms)
  • 2nd–3rd MCP arthropathy
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • testicular atrophy
  • cardiomyopathy signs
Ascites (own station)
  • inspect distension, flanks, umbilicus, veins
  • fluid thrill
  • shifting dullness
  • liver and spleen
  • JVP and heart (cardiac cause)
  • lung bases (effusion)
  • leg and sacral edema
  • abdominal girth
  • peritonism if SBP suspected
5. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

Jaundice and liver
1. Ascending cholangitis (48F, RUQ pain and jaundice)
Clues
  • 1 week jaundice, fever, rigors, recurrent RUQ pain
  • T 38.5, HR 110, BP 102/58
  • Murphy positive
  • BMI 32
DDx
  • choledocholithiasis
  • cholangitis
  • cholecystitis
  • viral hepatitis
  • PUD / perforation
  • PBC
  • PSC
  • IgG4 cholangitis
  • liver abscess
  • pyelonephritis
Workup
  • CBC (with differential)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • liver profile (ALT, AST, ALP, GGT, total / direct bilirubin, albumin)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT)
  • pancreatic (amylase, lipase)
  • lactate
  • hemolysis screen
  • infection (blood and urine cultures, viral hepatitis serology, HIV)
  • imaging (US abdomen, then MRCP or ERCP)
Results
  • WBC 19 ×10⁹/L (19,000/µL)
  • Hb 12 g/dL (120 g/L)
  • platelets 460 ×10⁹/L
  • creatinine 96 µmol/L (1.1 mg/dL)
  • urea 5 mmol/L (BUN 14 mg/dL)
  • Na 139, K 3.8 mmol/L
  • ALT 250, AST 170, ALP 420 IU/L (cholestatic > hepatocellular)
  • total bilirubin 80 µmol/L (4.7 mg/dL), direct 70 µmol/L (4.1 mg/dL)
  • INR 2.3
  • PTT 30 s
  • US distended gallbladder with multiple stones and dilated duct
Diagnosis
  • ascending cholangitis (Charcot triad: fever, jaundice, RUQ pain)
Management
  • ICU / monitored bed
  • IV fluids
  • IV piperacillin-tazobactam
  • correct coagulopathy (vitamin K, FFP)
  • GI for urgent ERCP
  • surgical consult (cholecystectomy later)
2. Acetaminophen-induced acute liver failure (24F, depression)
Clues
  • found jaundiced, confused, vomiting
  • 4 empty strips of acetaminophen
  • GCS 13, grade III encephalopathy, rigidity, hyporeflexia
  • tender hepatomegaly
  • BP 98/52
DDx
  • DILI
  • viral (HAV, HBV, HSV)
  • autoimmune hepatitis
  • Wilson
  • Budd-Chiari / ischemic
  • mushroom
  • acute fatty liver of pregnancy
  • cholangitis
Workup
  • toxicology (acetaminophen level at ≥ 4 h, salicylate, alcohol, urine drug screen)
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, PO4, glucose)
  • liver profile (ALT, AST, ALP, bilirubin, albumin)
  • coagulation (PT / INR)
  • blood gas + lactate
  • acute liver failure screen (viral serology and PCR — HAV, HBV, HEV, HSV, CMV, EBV; autoimmune — ANA, ASMA, LKM1, IgG; ceruloplasmin; pregnancy test)
  • cultures
  • imaging (Doppler US liver, CT head if encephalopathic)
Results
  • WBC 8 ×10⁹/L

4. Chronic hepatitis B (30M, raised ALT)
Clues
  • HBsAg positive for > 6 months
  • anti-HBs < 10
  • raised ALT / AST
  • no risk factors
Workup
  • HBV serology (HBsAg — repeat at 6 months, anti-HBs, anti-HBc IgM / IgG, HBeAg / anti-HBe)

  • coagulation (INR)
  • CBC (platelets)
  • co-infection screen (anti-HCV, anti-HDV, HIV, HAV IgG for vaccination)
  • HCC surveillance (AFP + US)
  • fibrosis (FibroScan, APRI / FIB-4)
Results
  • HBsAg positive
  • anti-HBs < 10 IU/L
  • ALT and AST elevated → chronic hepatitis B
  • treat if HBV DNA > 2,000 IU/mL with ALT > ULN, or significant fibrosis (FibroScan > 7 kPa, APRI > 0.5), or cirrhosis
Treat if
  • HBV DNA > 2000 IU/mL with ALT above normal
  • fibrosis ≥ F2 (FibroScan > 7 kPa, APRI > 0.5)
  • cirrhosis
  • or regardless with HIV / HDV / HCV coinfection, family history of HCC, immunosuppression, extrahepatic disease, pregnancy with high viral load
Treatment
  • entecavir or tenofovir
  • GI clinic
  • screen and vaccinate family
Goals
  • undetectable DNA
  • HBsAg loss
  • normal ALT
HCC surveillance
  • cirrhosis, advanced fibrosis, family history of HCC, men > 40 / women > 50 from endemic areas
  • US every 6 months ± AFP
