  • Lynch (HNPCC)
  • FAP
  • MUTYH-associated polyposis
Staging
  • limited to submucosa
  • node-negative
  • no metastasis = stage I
  • 5-year survival ≈ 90%
Family screening
  • wife at 45 (average risk)
  • sons by colonoscopy at 36 (10 years before father's age at diagnosis) or 40, whichever earlier, every 5 years
Questions asked across several stations
SAAG
  • ≥ 1.1 = portal hypertension (cirrhosis, HF, Budd-Chiari)
  • < 1.1 = peritoneal (TB, cancer), nephrotic, pancreatic
Bleeding management bundle
  • two large-bore lines, crossmatch, PPI infusion, restrictive transfusion (Hb > 7–8), endoscopy within 24 h
  • add octreotide + ceftriaxone if cirrhotic
Before biologics
  • TB
  • hepatitis B
  • HIV screening and vaccination
IBD extraintestinal
  • joints
  • eyes
  • skin
  • liver (PSC)
Celiac workup
  • TTG-IgA + total IgA on a gluten diet
  • then duodenal biopsy
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

Rumack-Matthew nomogram — 24F, depression, jaundice and confusion (case 2)
Findings
  • acetaminophen level 250 mcg/mL (mg/L)
  • well above the treatment line (≈ 150 mg/L at 4 h on the US line; 100 mg/L on the UK line shown)
Diagnosis
  • toxic acetaminophen ingestion → IV N-acetylcysteine now (nomogram only valid for a single acute ingestion with known time; give NAC regardless if time unknown or liver injury present)
Colonoscopy — 24M, bloody diarrhea 6 weeks (case 11)
Findings
  • distal segments show diffuse erythema, granular friable mucosa and loss of vascular pattern
  • proximal segments show a normal vascular pattern
  • biopsies: cryptitis, crypt abscesses and architectural distortion in rectum, sigmoid and descending colon
  • transverse, right colon and terminal ileum normal
Diagnosis
  • left-sided ulcerative colitis (continuous from the rectum, chronic architectural distortion)
Colonoscopy, segments C and D — same patient (case 11)
Findings
  • preserved fine vascular pattern
  • no ulcers
Diagnosis
  • normal proximal colon — confirms disease limited to the left colon (E2, left-sided UC)
Knees, before treatment — 21F, diarrhea and rash (case 12)
Findings
  • symmetric grouped erythematous papules / excoriations on the extensor surfaces of both knees
Diagnosis
  • dermatitis herpetiformis → celiac disease (check TTG-IgA with total IgA, duodenal biopsies)
Knees, after treatment — same patient (case 12)
Findings
  • rash cleared
Diagnosis
  • response to a strict gluten-free diet (± dapsone)
Colonoscopy — 26M, bloody diarrhea, weight loss, arthralgia (case 8)
Findings
  • deep longitudinal / serpiginous ulcers
  • cobblestone mucosa and patchy inflammation with normal areas between (skip lesions)
Diagnosis
  • Crohn's disease
CT abdomen and pelvis — same patient (case 8)
Findings
  • right lower quadrant fluid collection with gas bubbles (arrows) tracking into the right psoas
  • thickened adjacent bowel
Diagnosis
  • Crohn's disease complicated by ileopsoas abscess → drainage + antibiotics before biologics
Lower legs — 20M, chronic diarrhea, weight loss, perianal pain (case 9)
Findings
  • tender
  • ill-defined erythematous nodules on the shins (outlined)
Diagnosis
  • erythema nodosum — extra-intestinal manifestation of Crohn's disease
Colonoscopy, terminal ileum — same patient (case 9)
Findings
  • edematous ileal mucosa with ulceration
  • nodularity and narrowing
Diagnosis
  • Crohn's ileitis
CT enterography — same patient (case 9)
Findings
  • thickened
  • hyper-enhancing terminal ileum with engorged vasa recta ("comb sign") and mesenteric fat stranding
Diagnosis
  • active ileal Crohn's disease
Upper endoscopy — 45M, maroon rectal bleeding (case 15)
Findings
  • ulcer at the pylorus / duodenal bulb with a clean whitish fibrin base and a small red spot at its edge (stigma of recent hemorrhage)
Diagnosis
  • bleeding peptic ulcer (upper source for hematochezia; Forrest IIa–IIb if a visible vessel or clot → endoscopic therapy + high-dose PPI)
Upper endoscopy — 65M, dysphagia and weight loss (case 17)
Findings
  • irregular
  • friable
  • ulcerated mass narrowing the esophageal lumen
Diagnosis
  • esophageal carcinoma → biopsy
  • CT / PET staging
  • EUS
Colonoscopy — 46M, fatigue, bloating, constipation (case 18)
Findings
  • large lobulated
  • fungating polypoid mass with irregular surface
Diagnosis
  • colorectal adenocarcinoma (right colon) → biopsy, CEA, CT staging
  • screen first-degree relatives at 40 or 10 years before the index age

Nephrology

How to use this

All 18 nephrology stations in the Qassim 2021–2025 set run on one skeleton: core history, then the add-on block for your top differentials, then the general exam plus its add-on signs. Learn the core once; the marks that separate candidates sit in the add-ons and the examiner's follow-up questions.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • confirm name and age
  • in an unstable patient say ABC / MOVE first (Monitor, O2, IV line + labs, ECG)
  • ask for vitals and a glucose
Rest of history
  • PAM-FOSS
  • Past medical/surgical incl. blood transfusion
  • Allergies
  • Medications
  • Family
  • Occupation
  • Social
  • Sexual
Close every management answer
  • ANERVVES
  • Admission
  • Nutrition
  • Education
  • Referral
  • Vaccination
  • VTE prophylaxis
  • Exercise
  • Smoking
  • plus nephrology referral
Stations covered
  • AKI (AIN ×2, gentamicin ATN, rhabdomyolysis)
  • GN (IgA ×2, infection-related GN, lupus nephritis IV)
  • nephrotic (MCD)
  • CKD (ESKD counseling, CKD + HFrEF problem list)
  • dialysis disequilibrium
  • Na disorders (thiazide hyponatremia, SIADH, hypernatremia)
  • secondary HTN (Conn's, FMD/RAS, ADPKD)
1. Core nephrology history (ask in every case)
Presenting complaint
  • onset
  • duration
  • course
  • previous similar episodes
Urine
  • output (oliguria; last 24 h volume if inpatient)
  • color (red vs cola)
  • clots
  • frothy urine
  • dysuria
  • frequency
  • flank pain
  • weak stream / dribbling
Nephrotic / overload
  • puffy face
  • leg swelling
  • weight gain
  • abdominal distension
  • exertional dyspnea
  • orthopnea
  • PND
Nephritic / BP
  • headache
  • dizziness
  • blurred vision
  • SOB
  • known high BP
Uremia
  • nausea
  • vomiting
  • anorexia
  • itching
  • fatigue
  • confusion / reduced LOC
  • pleuritic chest pain (pericarditis)
  • peripheral neuropathy
  • bleeding
Volume depletion
  • poor oral intake
  • vomiting
  • diarrhea
  • bleeding
  • hypotension episodes
  • postural dizziness
Infection
  • fever
  • recent sore throat / URTI
  • skin infection
  • cough + sputum
  • night sweats
  • weight loss
Autoimmune screen
  • joint pain/swelling
  • malar or other rash
  • photosensitivity
  • oral ulcers
  • hair loss
  • Raynaud's
  • red eyes
  • ENT/sinus problems
  • hemoptysis
  • pleurisy
  • purpura
Malignancy screen
  • weight loss
  • anorexia
  • fatigue
  • neck masses / lymph nodes
  • breast lump
Comorbidities
  • DM (duration, control, retinopathy, neuropathy)
  • HTN
  • CKD (baseline creatinine)
  • HF
  • liver cirrhosis
  • hepatitis B/C
  • HIV
  • stones
  • BPH
  • SCD
Drugs (the big list)
  • NSAIDs
  • ACEi/ARB
  • diuretics
  • SGLT2i
  • PPI
  • antibiotics (aminoglycosides, penicillins, cephalosporins, vancomycin)
  • recent IV contrast
  • lithium
  • herbal / gum arabic
  • illicit drugs (cocaine, amphetamines, heroin)
Past history
  • previous admissions
  • surgery
  • procedures / arterial catheterization
  • blood transfusion
  • tattoos
Family
  • kidney disease
  • PKD
  • autoimmune disease (lupus)
  • HTN
  • malignancy
Social
  • smoking
  • alcohol
  • IV drugs
  • sexual history
  • travel
  • occupation (chemical / benzene exposure)
2. History add-ons by differential
A. AKI / rising creatinine
Always get
  • baseline creatinine
  • urine output over 24 h
  • weight
Pre-renal
  • vomiting
  • diarrhea
  • poor intake
  • bleeding
  • hypotension overnight / fluid boluses
  • sepsis or septic shock
  • HF
  • cirrhosis
  • diuretics
  • ACEi/ARB
  • NSAIDs
ATN — ischemic
  • shock
  • sepsis
  • prolonged hypotension
  • recent surgery
ATN — nephrotoxic
  • aminoglycosides (gentamicin: dose, duration, levels)
  • new hearing loss or tinnitus = ototoxicity
  • vancomycin
  • amphotericin
  • cisplatin
  • IV contrast
AIN
  • new drug days to weeks before (penicillins incl. Augmentin, cephalosporins, sulfonamides, PPI, NSAIDs)
  • triad of fever, rash, arthralgia
  • also infection (UTI)
Rhabdomyolysis
  • extreme exertion in heat
  • trauma / crush
  • prolonged immobilization or fall
  • seizure
  • muscle pain, cramps, weakness, dark (cola) urine
  • statins, cocaine, amphetamines, alcohol
  • heat stroke
  • viral illness
  • NMS / serotonin syndrome / malignant hyperthermia (antipsychotics, SSRIs, anesthesia)
GN / RPGN
  • hematuria
  • puffy face
  • recent sore throat or skin infection
  • hemoptysis (pulmonary-renal)
  • rash
  • joint pain
  • ENT symptoms
Post-renal / obstruction
  • BPH symptoms (weak stream, dribbling, retention)
  • stones
  • pelvic or prostatic malignancy
  • blocked catheter
  • anuria
Atheroembolic
  • recent angiography / arterial catheterization
  • livedo
  • blue toes
Abdominal compartment (ICU)
  • tense abdomen
  • massive ascites
B. Hematuria / nephritic
Is it true blood?
  • food
  • drugs (rifampicin)
  • hemolysis or SCD (dark urine)
  • myoglobin (muscle pain, exertion)
  • menstruation
  • bleeding from other orifices
  • anticoagulants
Timing after infection
  • within 1–3 days of URTI = IgA (synpharyngitic)
  • 1–3 weeks after sore throat or skin infection = post-infectious GN
IgA
  • recurrent episodes of gross (cola) hematuria with URTIs
  • flank discomfort
  • young male
Infection-related GN
  • current fever
  • sore throat
  • productive cough
  • oliguria
Lupus nephritis
  • young female
  • arthralgia
  • alopecia
  • photosensitive rash
  • oral ulcers
  • Raynaud's
  • serositis
  • seizures
  • miscarriages (APS)
ANCA vasculitis / anti-GBM
  • hemoptysis
  • sinusitis
  • epistaxis
  • red eyes
  • hearing loss
  • purpura
  • neuropathy
  • smoking (anti-GBM)
TMA (TTP/HUS)
  • bloody diarrhea
  • bruising / petechiae
  • fever
  • confusion
Cryoglobulinemia / MPGN
  • hepatitis C
  • purpura
  • arthralgia
Non-glomerular
  • stones (renal colic)
  • UTI (fever, dysuria)
  • bladder or renal cancer (age > 40, painless gross hematuria with clots, smoking, benzene / aniline dyes, cyclophosphamide or chemotherapy)
  • PKD
  • trauma
C. Nephrotic syndrome
Analysis of edema
  • onset
  • uni- vs bilateral
  • puffy face in the morning
  • extent (ascites, scrotal, pleural)
Exclude other edema
  • HF symptoms
  • liver disease
  • DVT
  • drugs (amlodipine)
Complications
  • thrombosis (DVT, PE, renal vein, cerebral venous thrombosis)
  • infections
  • hyperlipidemia
MCD triggers
  • NSAIDs
  • ampicillin
  • recent URTI / allergy / immunization
  • Hodgkin or non-Hodgkin lymphoma
  • leukemia
Membranous triggers
  • solid cancers (age-appropriate screening)
  • hepatitis B
  • SLE
  • drugs (penicillamine, gold, NSAIDs)
FSGS triggers
  • obesity
  • HIV
  • heroin
  • sickle cell
  • reflux
  • reduced nephron mass
Diabetic kidney disease
  • long DM
  • retinopathy (DKD without retinopathy is unusual)
  • neuropathy
Others
  • amyloid / myeloma (bone pain, age > 50)
  • syphilis
  • HCV
D. CKD / ESKD
CKD story
  • cause
  • duration
  • how diagnosed (microalbuminuria → proteinuria → falling GFR)
  • last creatinine / eGFR
  • follow-up
  • compliance
  • missed appointments
Cause hunt
  • DM
  • HTN
  • GN
  • PKD (family history)
  • obstruction (stones, BPH, recurrent UTI)
  • autoimmune disease
  • drugs
Complications
  • uremia (nausea, anorexia, itching, pericarditis, encephalopathy)
  • overload
  • anemia (fatigue, SOB, bleeding, melena)
  • bone disease (bone pain, fractures)
  • acidosis
  • hyperkalemia (weakness, palpitations)
Comorbidity control
  • DM (HbA1c, eye, nerve, foot ulcers, gastroparesis)
  • HTN
  • HF (NYHA, last echo, ACS / PCI / CABG)
Meds review
  • metformin
  • sitagliptin dose
  • NSAIDs
  • SGLT2i
  • ACEi/ARB
  • calcitriol
  • insulin
  • herbal (gum arabic)
RRT readiness
  • previous dialysis catheter
  • AV fistula
  • transplant
  • PD
  • patient preference
E. Dialysis patient with neuro symptoms (seizure / confusion)
Timing
  • first or early dialysis session
  • symptoms during or hours after HD = dialysis disequilibrium
DDS prodrome
  • headache
  • nausea
  • vomiting
  • dizziness
  • muscle cramps
  • tremor
  • blurred vision
  • confusion
Seizure description
  • aura
  • LOC
  • eye rolling
  • frothing
  • tongue bite
  • incontinence
  • post-ictal confusion
  • Todd's paralysis
  • time to recover
Other causes
  • uremic encephalopathy (missed dialysis)
  • hypertensive encephalopathy (very high BP)
  • stroke (focal deficit)
  • CNS infection (catheter, fever, neck stiffness)
  • hypoglycemia (insulin)
  • electrolytes (Na, Ca — hyperparathyroidism)
  • drugs
F. Secondary hypertension (young, severe or resistant HTN)
HTN story
  • onset
  • previous readings
  • home BP
  • compliance
  • target-organ damage (headache, chest pain, SOB, visual changes, focal deficit, hematuria, oliguria)
  • hypertensive emergencies
Renal parenchymal / PKD
  • family history of kidney disease
  • hematuria
  • flank pain
  • UTIs
  • stones
  • cramps
  • itching
Renovascular (RAS / FMD)
  • young woman (FMD) or older vasculopath (atherosclerotic)
  • flash pulmonary edema
  • AKI on ACEi/ARB
  • abdominal bruit
Primary aldosteronism
  • hypokalemia symptoms (muscle weakness, cramps, paresthesia, polyuria)
  • family history of early HTN or stroke
Pheochromocytoma
  • episodic headache
  • sweating
  • palpitations
  • pallor
  • panic attacks
Cushing's
  • weight gain
  • striae
  • easy bruising
  • proximal weakness
  • bone pain
Thyroid
  • weight loss
  • heat intolerance
  • diarrhea
  • tremor
  • eye protrusion
Acromegaly
  • headaches
  • sweating
  • ring / shoe size change
  • visual field loss
Coarctation
  • HF symptoms
  • leg claudication
OSA
  • snoring
  • witnessed apnea
  • daytime sleepiness
Drugs / substances
  • steroids
  • NSAIDs
  • OCP
  • CNIs (cyclosporine)
  • TCAs
  • decongestants
  • weight-loss pills
  • cocaine
  • amphetamines
  • alcohol
  • caffeine
  • salt
Women
  • menstrual cycle
  • pregnancy
  • pre-eclampsia
G. Hyponatremia
Symptoms / severity
  • headache
  • nausea
  • vomiting
  • confusion
  • sleepiness
  • seizures
  • falls
Hypovolemic
  • vomiting
  • diarrhea
  • poor intake
  • diuretics (thiazide just started = classic)
  • adrenal insufficiency (stopping steroids)
  • salt-wasting
Euvolemic (SIADH)
  • drugs (SSRIs, TCAs, carbamazepine, valproate, antipsychotics, cyclophosphamide)
  • CNS disease (stroke, bleed, infection, trauma)
  • lung disease (pneumonia, mass — smoker with weight loss = small cell)
  • malignancy
  • pain / nausea / surgery
  • hypothyroidism (cold intolerance, weight gain)
  • cortisol deficiency
Hypervolemic
  • HF
  • cirrhosis
  • nephrotic syndrome
  • advanced CKD
Water intake
  • psychogenic polydipsia
  • beer potomania
  • tea-and-toast diet
ODS risk
  • alcoholism
  • malnutrition
  • liver disease
  • hypokalemia
  • Na < 105
H. Hypernatremia
Access to water
  • elderly
  • bedbound
  • dependent
  • impaired thirst
  • dementia (commonest cause in elderly)
Extrarenal loss
  • vomiting
  • diarrhea
  • sweating
  • fever
  • burns
Renal loss
  • loop diuretics
  • osmotic diuresis (hyperglycemia, mannitol)
  • central DI (head trauma, surgery)
  • nephrogenic DI (lithium, amphotericin B, demeclocycline, hypercalcemia, hypokalemia)
Iatrogenic
  • hypertonic saline
  • sodium bicarbonate
I. Altered mental status in a renal / electrolyte patient
CNS
  • headache
  • focal deficit
  • slurred speech
  • seizure
  • neck stiffness
  • photophobia
Metabolic
  • glucose (sulfonylurea, insulin, polyuria / polydipsia)
  • Na
  • Ca (tetany, perioral numbness, malignancy, hyperparathyroidism)
  • uremia
  • liver failure (acetaminophen, jaundice)
  • hypothyroidism
Drugs
  • sedatives
  • SSRIs
  • antihistamines
  • anticholinergics
  • quinolones
  • steroids
  • alcohol
Infection source
  • chest
  • urine
  • abdomen
  • skin
  • bed sores
3. General nephrology exam (every case)
HMF + general look
  • ill / well
  • distress
  • GCS and orientation
  • body build and BMI
  • pallor
  • jaundice
  • cyanosis
  • uremic tinge
  • scratch marks
Vitals
  • BP (both arms in HTN, lying and standing for postural drop)
  • HR and rhythm
  • RR
  • temperature
  • SpO2
  • blood glucose
Volume status
  • JVP
  • mucous membranes
  • skin turgor
  • capillary refill
  • postural BP
  • peripheral and sacral edema
  • daily weight and urine output
Face / hands
  • periorbital puffiness
  • malar rash
  • oral ulcers
  • alopecia
  • asterixis
  • AV fistula / graft (thrill, bruit)
  • dialysis catheter scar
CVS
  • apex (displaced = LVH / HF)
  • S3
  • S4
  • murmurs
  • pericardial rub
Chest
  • basal crackles (overload)
  • coarse crackles / bronchial breathing (pneumonia, pulmonary hemorrhage)
  • effusion
Abdomen
  • ballotable / polycystic kidneys
  • transplant scar (iliac fossa)
  • renal or aortic bruit
  • palpable bladder
  • flank and suprapubic tenderness
  • liver and spleen
  • ascites (shifting dullness, fluid thrill)
  • PD catheter
Lower limbs
  • pitting edema (level)
  • DVT signs
  • livedo reticularis
  • rash / purpura
Skin and joints
  • rash (maculopapular, purpuric, malar)
  • active synovitis
Fundoscopy
  • hypertensive and diabetic retinopathy
Bedside
  • urine dipstick (blood, protein, leukocytes, nitrites)
  • ECG if K+ is a concern
4. Exam add-ons by differential
AKI in ICU
  • mental status / GCS
  • nose and chest for pulmonary-renal syndrome
  • tense abdomen or massive ascites → measure intra-abdominal pressure
  • skin rash (AIN, vasculitis)
  • palpable bladder (obstruction)
  • dipstick
Pre-renal / hypovolemia
  • tachycardia
  • postural hypotension
  • low JVP
  • dry mucosa
  • reduced turgor
AIN
  • fever
  • maculopapular rash over trunk / limbs
Rhabdomyolysis
  • muscle tenderness and swelling
  • power and reflexes (weakness, hyporeflexia)
  • compartment syndrome
  • temperature (heat stroke)
  • skin hot / dry / sweaty
  • signs of trauma
  • ECG for peaked T
Atheroembolic
  • livedo reticularis
  • blue toes
  • retinal plaques
Nephritic / GN
  • BP high
  • periorbital puffiness, edema
  • throat (tonsils, exudate) and skin for infection
  • ENT (saddle nose, sinus tenderness)
  • chest crackles or hemoptysis signs
  • purpura
  • arthritis
  • murmur (endocarditis-related GN)
Lupus
  • malar / discoid rash
  • oral ulcers
  • alopecia
  • synovitis
  • serositis (rub, effusion)
  • lymphadenopathy
Nephrotic
  • periorbital and pitting edema (non-pitting = other cause)
  • ascites
  • pleural effusion
  • DVT
  • xanthelasma
  • lymph nodes and organomegaly (lymphoma)
  • breast / thyroid / prostate for cancer (membranous)
CKD / ESKD
  • uremic look
  • pallor
  • scratch marks
  • asterixis
  • AV fistula / graft
  • catheter scars
  • transplant scar
  • polycystic kidneys
  • pericardial rub
  • S3
  • crackles
  • edema
  • livedo
  • bone tenderness / renal osteodystrophy
  • fundi
Dialysis patient with seizure
  • GCS
  • focal neurology
  • neck stiffness
  • fundi (papilledema)
  • BP
  • catheter / access site for infection
  • tongue bite
Secondary HTN
  • both-arm BP, radio-radial and radio-femoral delay, weak femoral pulses, murmur (coarctation)
  • renal / abdominal bruit (RAS, FMD)
  • ballotable kidneys (ADPKD)
  • abdominal mass (pheo)
  • Cushingoid look (moon face, buffalo hump, supraclavicular fat, purple striae, bruising, proximal myopathy)
  • thyroid signs (goiter, tremor, exophthalmos)
  • acromegaly (large hands / feet, prognathism, widely spaced teeth)
  • short neck / retrognathia (OSA), BMI
  • displaced apex / S4 (LVH)
Fundoscopy grades
  • I arteriolar thickening
  • II AV nipping
  • III hemorrhages / cotton wool / exudates
  • IV papilledema
Hyponatremia
  • volume status decides the category (postural BP, JVP, edema, ascites)
  • CNS exam (focal deficit, reflexes)
  • chest for mass / effusion
  • thyroid and adrenal signs (pigmentation)
  • PR if bleeding suspected
Hypernatremia
  • GCS
  • dehydration (dry mucosa, turgor)
  • postural BP
  • CNS (tone, reflexes)
  • HF signs if on diuretics
  • CLD stigmata
5. Case scenarios (all 18 stations)

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

AKI
1. Drug-induced AIN in ICU (two stations: 62M and 67F)
Stem
  • respiratory failure from Gram-negative pneumonia
  • Augmentin failed → cephalosporin + gentamicin
  • creatinine 90 → 430 µmol/L
Clues
  • maculopapular rash on legs and arms
  • one overnight hypotension
  • UOP 690 mL/24 h (oliguric for 80 kg)
  • high JVP
  • crackles
Workup
  • CBC (eosinophils)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN, urine output)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Cl)
  • blood gas (VBG — pH, HCO3, PCO2, anion gap)
  • liver profile
  • urine (urinalysis + microscopy, urine eosinophils, urine Na, urine osmolality, FeNa)
  • CK
  • drug levels (gentamicin / vancomycin)
  • immunology (ANA, C3 / C4, ANCA)
  • imaging (renal US, CXR)
Results
  • station 1 (62M): creatinine 430 µmol/L (4.8 mg/dL) from baseline 90 µmol/L (≈ 1.0 mg/dL)
  • Na 131, K 5.1, Cl 95 mmol/L
  • VBG pH 7.29, HCO3 15 mmol/L, PCO2 42 mmHg (5.6 kPa) — high anion gap metabolic acidosis (AG 21) with inadequate respiratory compensation
  • urine Na 20 mmol/L, osm 300 mOsm/kg, FeNa < 1%
  • CK normal
  • urine +1 protein, no RBCs, WBC 40/hpf, WBC casts, eosinophiluria, no bacteriuria
  • ANA positive with normal C3 / C4
  • gentamicin level in range
  • kidneys 12 cm
  • Station 2 (67F): urine output 690 mL/24 h at 80 kg (≈ 0.36 mL/kg/h — oliguria)
  • metabolic acidosis with K 5.1 mmol/L
  • WBC 14 ×10⁹/L
  • urine Na 15 mmol/L, osm 300 mOsm/kg, low FeNa
  • urine WBC with eosinophils
  • CXR congested
  • autoimmune normal
DDx (5)
  • pre-renal (hypotension, sepsis)
  • ATN (sepsis, gentamicin, rhabdo)
  • AIN (Augmentin)
  • GN
  • obstruction
Diagnosis
  • AKI from AIN (beta-lactam)
  • confirm with renal biopsy (interstitial inflammatory infiltrate, normal glomeruli and vessels)
Management
  • nephrology
  • stop culprit and nephrotoxins
  • treat sepsis
  • avoid hypotension
  • diuretics for overload
  • strict UOP and daily weight
  • low-K diet
  • correct K, PO4, Ca, Mg, acidosis
  • avoid hypo/hyperglycemia
  • assess dialysis need
  • add steroids once biopsy confirms AIN
Examiner Qs
  • urgent dialysis = refractory hyperkalemia
  • refractory acidosis
  • refractory volume overload
  • uremic symptoms
  • toxins (methanol, lithium)
2. Gentamicin ATN + angry patient (62M)
Stem
  • BPH, acute prostatitis on IV gentamicin
  • becomes oliguric with hearing trouble, now needs HD
  • angry
Communication marks (50%)
  • ABC first
  • safety (door open, distance)
  • let him vent
  • acknowledge anger empathically
  • apologize without blaming anyone or being defensive
  • explain plan
  • promise transparent investigation
  • explain the complaint process and that it won't affect his care
  • follow-up plan
  • resources
  • check understanding
  • thank him
DDx for oliguria
  • pre-renal (hypovolemia, sepsis, HF)
  • intrinsic (aminoglycoside ATN, AIN from antibiotics, ischemic ATN, post-infectious GN / RPGN)
  • post-renal (prostate, stones, clot, blocked catheter)
Pre-renal vs intrinsic
  • pre-renal = urine Na < 20, FeNa < 1%, urine osm > 500
  • intrinsic = urine Na > 40, FeNa > 2%, urine osm < 350
  • casts = muddy brown (ATN), WBC (AIN), RBC (GN)
Workup
  • renal profile (creatinine, urea / BUN, eGFR, urine output)
  • electrolytes (K, Na, Ca, PO4, Mg)
  • blood gas (HCO3, pH)
  • urine (urinalysis + microscopy, urine Na, FeNa, urine osmolality)
  • gentamicin trough level
  • CBC
  • ECG (hyperkalemia)
  • imaging (renal US)
Results
  • urea 22 mmol/L (BUN ≈ 62 mg/dL)
  • creatinine 340 µmol/L (≈ 3.8 mg/dL)
  • K 6.6 mmol/L (6.6 mEq/L)
  • HCO3 14 mmol/L
  • urine Na 65 mmol/L
  • FeNa 3% (> 2% = intrinsic ATN)
  • urine osmolality 280 mOsm/kg (isosthenuric)
  • muddy brown granular casts
  • renal US normal
Diagnosis
  • AKI from gentamicin ATN
Management
  • admit
  • stop gentamicin
  • alternative non-nephrotoxic antibiotic
  • nephrology
  • volume status and fluid balance
  • treat K
  • HD (hyperkalemia + acidosis here)
  • counsel on reversibility
  • DVT / GI prophylaxis
3. Rhabdomyolysis (22M, charity run in heat)
Stem
  • 15-mile run
  • dizziness
  • cramps
  • back pain
  • leg weakness
Clues
  • HR 114
  • BP 95/50
  • dry mucosa
  • LL power 3/5 with hyporeflexia
  • normal sensation
DDx
  • rhabdomyolysis
  • trauma
  • viral myositis
  • heat stroke
  • malignant hyperthermia
  • NMS
  • serotonin syndrome
  • inflammatory myositis
  • pheo / thyroid storm
  • drugs (statins, cocaine, amphetamines)
Workup
  • muscle injury (CK, urine myoglobin — dipstick blood positive without RBCs, LDH, AST)
  • renal profile (creatinine, BUN, urine output)
  • electrolytes (K, Ca, PO4, Mg, uric acid)
  • blood gas (pH, HCO3, lactate)
  • CBC
  • coagulation (DIC)
  • liver profile
  • endocrine (TSH, cortisol)
  • ECG (hyperkalemia)
  • imaging (CXR; CT / MRI spine if back pain with neuro signs)
  • EMG / NCS if weakness persists
Management
  • ICU
  • ABC
  • two large-bore cannulae
  • NS 200–250 mL/h targeting high UOP
  • correct electrolytes
  • consider urinary alkalinization (pH > 6.5, weak evidence)
  • RRT and nephrology
  • DVT and GI prophylaxis
ECG = peaked T (hyperkalemia)
  • IV calcium gluconate / chloride
  • insulin 10 units + dextrose
  • salbutamol nebs
  • RRT if refractory
RRT indications here
  • oliguric AKI
  • volume overload / pulmonary edema
  • refractory electrolytes
  • metabolic acidosis
  • symptomatic uremia
Complications
  • AKI
  • hyperkalemia
  • hyperphosphatemia
  • hypocalcemia (early)
  • hypermagnesemia
  • DIC
  • compartment syndrome
  • DVT / PE
Glomerular disease
4. IgA nephropathy (two stations: 26M gross hematuria; 25M red urine 2 days after URTI)

  • Na 139, K 4.9 mmol/L

Management

HD counseling
  • why he needs it
  • what HD is
  • access (AVF vs catheter)
  • risks and benefits
  • risk of refusing
  • alternatives (PD, transplant)
Anemia
  • check ferritin / TSAT
  • IV iron if ferritin < 200 and TSAT < 20%

Hyperphosphatemia

10. Dialysis disequilibrium syndrome (52M, first HD session, then seizure)
Clues
  • DM, HTN, CKD
  • headache and vomiting then first tonic-clonic seizure on the day of first HD
  • no fever, focal weakness or neck stiffness
DDx
  • DDS
  • uremic encephalopathy
  • CNS infection (catheter)
  • hemorrhagic or ischemic stroke
  • hypertensive encephalopathy
  • hypoglycemia
  • hypercalcemia (hyperparathyroidism)
  • idiopathic
DDS features
  • nausea, vomiting, headache, dizziness, cramps, confusion, tremor, visual change, asterixis, seizure, coma, death
  • during or hours after HD
Workup
  • bedside glucose
  • electrolytes (Na, Ca, Mg, PO4)
  • renal profile (pre- and post-dialysis urea / BUN)
  • blood gas
  • CBC
  • PTH
  • imaging (CT brain, MRI)
  • ECG
  • EEG
  • LP if infection suspected
Result
  • CT = symmetrical diffuse white-matter edema
  • normal electrolytes and EEG → DDS
Management
  • control BP
  • change HD prescription (shorter sessions, lower blood flow, smaller target urea reduction)
  • correct metabolic issues (high PTH)
  • no antiepileptic (provoked seizure)
Start antiepileptic after first seizure if
  • abnormal neuro exam, MRI or EEG
  • Todd's paralysis
  • status epilepticus
  • high-risk job
  • family history of epilepsy
  • patient wish
Sodium disorders

13. Hypernatremia from extrarenal loss (75M, DM / HTN / HF on diuretics; confusion + vomiting 3 days; Na 160)
Clues
  • looks dehydrated
  • GCS 11/15
  • BP 110/70
  • HR 95
DDx
  • extrarenal loss (vomiting, diarrhea, insensible)
  • DI (central or nephrogenic)
  • osmotic diuresis (hyperglycemia, mannitol)
  • drugs (diuretics, lithium)
Workup
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca, Mg)
  • serum osmolality
  • urine (osmolality, spot Na, urinalysis, 24-h volume)
  • renal profile
  • glucose + HbA1c
  • liver profile
  • blood gas
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • cardiac (ECG, echo)
  • imaging (CXR, CT brain)
  • water deprivation / desmopressin test if polyuric (DI)
Results
  • Na 160 mmol/L (160 mEq/L)
  • urine Na 10 mmol/L
  • urine osmolality 800 mOsm/kg (concentrated — kidney conserving) → extrarenal water loss
  • glucose, renal profile, CXR and CT brain normal
Management
  • ABC
  • ICU / HDU
  • stop diuretics (with cardiology)
  • if hypovolemic NS until stable, then hypotonic fluid (D5W) per calculated water deficit
  • correct no faster than 10–12 mmol/L per day (0.5 mmol/L/h)
  • Na every 4–6 h
  • treat vomiting
  • VTE prophylaxis
  • nephrology
Examiner Qs
  • slow correction prevents cerebral edema, seizures, herniation
  • commonest cause in elderly = inadequate water intake (impaired thirst or access)
  • nephrogenic DI drugs = lithium, amphotericin B, demeclocycline
Secondary hypertension
14. Primary aldosteronism / Conn's (22F, BP 188/105 ×3, cramps and weakness)
Clues
  • HR 109
  • grade II hypertensive retinopathy
  • otherwise normal exam
Workup
  • electrolytes (Na, K, HCO3) + renal profile (urea, creatinine)
  • bone (Ca)
  • blood gas
  • endocrine screen (aldosterone / renin ratio — off interfering drugs; urinary / plasma metanephrines; 24-h urinary cortisol or 1 mg DST if Cushingoid; TSH; OGTT with GH if acromegalic)
  • cardiac (ECG, echo, CXR for coarctation)
  • imaging (renal US + Doppler or MRA; adrenal CT after biochemistry; adrenal vein sampling)
Results
  • Na 144 mmol/L
  • K 3.0 mmol/L (3.0 mEq/L)
  • urea 5.4 mmol/L (BUN 15 mg/dL)
  • HCO3 29 mmol/L (hypokalemic alkalosis)
  • creatinine 76 µmol/L (0.86 mg/dL)
  • Ca 2.3 mmol/L (9.2 mg/dL)
  • catecholamines normal
  • renal Doppler normal
  • ECG LVH
  • supine aldosterone 1,220 pmol/L (≈ 44 ng/dL; normal 100–500)
  • plasma renin activity 0.2 (normal 1.1–2.7) → suppressed renin
Next test
  • correct K
  • stop interfering drugs (ACEi, beta-blockers, spironolactone)
  • aldosterone and plasma renin activity → high aldosterone, suppressed renin
Imaging
  • adrenal MRI / CT → adenoma (surgery) vs bilateral hyperplasia (medical)
  • benign features = < 10 HU, washout > 50%, < 4 cm, smooth, homogeneous
Treatment
  • adrenalectomy for adenoma
  • tumors > 4 cm always removed
  • HTN cured in only about two-thirds
  • medical = spironolactone (gynecomastia, impotence, menstrual irregularity) or amiloride / triamterene

  • HCO3 25 mmol/L
  • Na and Ca normal
  • renin and aldosterone both mildly raised (secondary hyperaldosteronism)
  • ECG LVH + left axis deviation
  • Doppler high resistive index and peak systolic velocity on the left
Diagnosis
  • left RAS from FMD
  • confirm with renal angiography / CTA / MRA
Management
  • at least two antihypertensives including ACEi / ARB
  • nephrology and angioplasty referral
  • low salt
  • quit smoking
  • exercise
  • weight loss
Angioplasty indications in FMD
  • recent-onset HTN
  • resistant HTN
  • intolerance of medication
  • progressive kidney dysfunction
16. ADPKD (35M, BP 150/90, family history of kidney disease)
Clues
  • bilateral ballotable kidneys
DDx
  • ADPKD
  • RAS
  • endocrine (Cushing's, pheo, hyperthyroid)
  • essential HTN
  • other CKD
Workup
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR)
  • urine (urinalysis, 24-h protein)
  • liver profile
  • metabolic (lipids, HbA1c)
  • blood gas
  • cardiac (ECG, echo)
  • imaging (US abdomen / kidneys — cyst number and kidney size; MRI for total kidney volume; MRA brain if family history of aneurysm / SAH)
  • genetic testing (PKD1 / PKD2) if unclear
  • metanephrines, cortisol, TFTs if suspected
Results
  • creatinine 56 µmol/L (≈ 0.63 mg/dL)
  • hematuria without proteinuria
  • US multiple bilateral renal cysts
Management
  • education
  • low salt
  • stop smoking
  • weight control
  • exercise
  • high water intake
  • BP target < 110/75 with ACEi / ARB
  • tolvaptan for rapidly progressive disease
  • family screening and genetic counseling
  • nephrology
  • monitor BP, function, proteinuria
  • aneurysm screening in selected patients
Examiner Qs
  • extrarenal = liver cysts, intracranial aneurysms, valve disease (MVP, AR), diverticulosis
  • commonest cause of death = cardiovascular disease
Questions asked across several stations
Emergency dialysis (AEIOU)
  • refractory acidosis
  • refractory hyperkalemia (K > 6.5 or ECG changes)
  • toxins (methanol, lithium)
  • refractory overload / pulmonary edema
  • uremia (pericarditis, pleuritis, encephalopathy, seizures)
Hyperkalemia with ECG changes
  • IV calcium
  • insulin + dextrose
  • salbutamol
  • dialysis if refractory
  • stop K sources and offending drugs
  • low-K diet
Urine indices
  • pre-renal FeNa < 1%, urine Na < 20, urine osm > 500
  • ATN FeNa > 2%, urine Na > 40, osm < 350
Casts
  • RBC casts / dysmorphic RBCs = GN
  • WBC casts + eosinophils = AIN
  • muddy brown granular casts = ATN
Biopsy rule
  • proteinuria + hematuria or unexplained AKI with normal-size kidneys → renal biopsy confirms in GN
  • nephrotic and AIN stations
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

ECG — 22M, collapse after a charity run in the heat (case 3)
Findings
  • tall peaked T waves
  • widened QRS merging into the T waves
  • flattened / absent P waves
  • regular rhythm
Diagnosis
  • severe hyperkalemia from rhabdomyolysis → IV calcium gluconate, insulin–dextrose, salbutamol
  • urgent dialysis if refractory
Urine — same patient (case 3)
Findings
  • dark tea / cola-colored urine compared with normal straw-colored control
  • dipstick blood positive with no RBCs on microscopy
Diagnosis
  • myoglobinuria → rhabdomyolysis (CK very high) → aggressive IV fluids targeting urine output 200–300 mL/h
Urine microscopy — 26M, gross hematuria (case 4)
Findings
  • dysmorphic red cells (acanthocytes / "Mickey Mouse" cells) and red-cell casts
Diagnosis
  • glomerular hematuria (nephritic sediment) → IgA nephropathy in this station
Urine microscopy — 25M, red urine 2 days after URTI (case 4)
Findings
  • red blood cell casts
Diagnosis
  • glomerulonephritis
  • synpharyngitic timing (1–2 days after URTI) → IgA nephropathy (vs 2–3 weeks for post-strep GN)
Urine microscopy + CXR — 28F, joint pain, cough, fatigue (case 5)
Findings
  • urine: red-cell casts
  • CXR: bilateral patchy air-space consolidation, more in the left mid / lower zones
  • labs: WBC 19 ×10⁹/L, creatinine 470 µmol/L (≈ 5.3 mg/dL), K 5.2 mmol/L, ESR 92, ANA / ANCA / anti-GBM negative, C3 / C4 normal
Diagnosis
  • pneumonia with nephritic syndrome → infection-related glomerulonephritis (pulmonary-renal syndrome excluded by negative serology)
Renal biopsy (PAS) — 28F, protein 3+, RBC 15/hpf (case 6)
Findings
  • glomeruli with global endocapillary hypercellularity
  • thickened capillary walls ("wire loops") and mesangial expansion involving > 50% of glomeruli
Diagnosis
  • diffuse proliferative lupus nephritis
  • ISN/RPS class IV → steroids + MMF or cyclophosphamide
  • HCQ
Adrenal MRI — 22F, BP 188/105, cramps and weakness (case 14)
Findings
  • (a) T1 in-phase: 3.0 cm well-defined left adrenal nodule (arrow)
  • (b) out-of-phase: homogeneous signal drop = intracellular lipid
Diagnosis
  • lipid-rich adrenal adenoma → aldosterone-producing adenoma (Conn's) → adrenal vein sampling
  • then laparoscopic adrenalectomy
Hypertensive retinopathy grading (reference slide, case 14)
Findings
  • grade I arteriolar thickening
  • II AV nipping
  • III flame / dot-blot hemorrhages, cotton-wool spots, hard exudates
  • IV papilledema
Diagnosis
  • Keith-Wagener-Barker grading — grade III–IV = hypertensive emergency territory

Endocrinology

How to use this

The 19 endocrinology stations fall into five groups: diabetes and its emergencies, thyroid, adrenal, pituitary, and endocrine hypertension. Most stations score a structured history, a system-specific exam (the thyroid exam is examined on its own twice), then interpretation of a hormone panel.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • in ER cases secure ABC and ask for glucose and vitals first
Stations covered
  • diabetes (new T2DM with nephropathy, DKA ×2 incl. DKA + STEMI, HHS)
  • thyroid (Graves ×3, thyroid exam ×2, PTC ×2, MTC breaking bad news, post-thyroidectomy hypocalcemia)
  • adrenal (Addison / APS, Cushing disease, Conn's, pheochromocytoma)
  • pituitary (prolactinoma, Sheehan syndrome)
1. Core endocrine history (every case)
Presenting complaint
  • onset
  • course
  • duration
  • severity
  • impact on daily life
  • previous similar episodes
Weight and appetite
  • amount and duration of weight change
  • intentional or not
  • appetite up or down
Thyroid screen
  • heat or cold intolerance
  • sweating
  • tremor
  • palpitations
  • bowel habit
  • hair / skin change
  • neck swelling
  • eye symptoms
Adrenal / pituitary screen
  • postural dizziness
  • salt craving
  • pigmentation
  • bruising
  • striae
  • headache
  • visual field loss
  • galactorrhea
Glucose
  • polyuria
  • polydipsia
  • polyphagia
  • blurred vision
  • hypoglycemia episodes
Reproductive
  • menstrual cycle
  • amenorrhea
  • fertility
  • pregnancy / lactation
  • hirsutism
  • acne
  • libido
Bone and muscle
  • proximal weakness
  • bone pain
  • fractures
  • cramps
  • tetany
Past history
  • autoimmune disease (T1DM, thyroid, vitiligo, pernicious anemia)
  • neck / pituitary surgery or radiation
  • TB
  • malignancy
  • CKD
  • liver disease
  • PCOS
  • gestational DM
Drugs
  • steroids (oral, inhaled, topical, injection) and recent withdrawal
  • amiodarone
  • lithium
  • iodine contrast
  • thyroxine
  • antipsychotics
  • antidepressants
  • OCP
  • SGLT2i
  • checkpoint inhibitors
  • licorice
Family
  • DM
  • thyroid disease
  • autoimmune disease
  • MEN1 / MEN2
  • VHL
  • NF1
  • thyroid cancer
  • early HTN
Social
  • smoking (worsens Graves eye disease)
  • alcohol
  • diet
  • exercise
  • occupation
  • marital status

Mass

F. Cushing syndrome
Exogenous steroids first
  • oral

  • injected

3. Core endocrine exam (every case)
General
  • BMI
  • body habitus (central obesity, cachexia)
  • hydration
  • pigmentation
  • acanthosis nigricans
  • hirsutism
  • mood
Vitals
  • BP (both arms; lying and standing)
  • HR and rhythm (AF)
  • RR
  • temperature
  • glucose
Hands
  • tremor
  • sweating vs dry
  • palmar erythema
  • onycholysis
  • thyroid acropachy
  • palmar crease pigmentation
  • large hands
Face and eyes
  • moon face
  • exophthalmos
  • lid retraction
  • lid lag
  • chemosis
  • eye movements
  • outer eyebrow loss
  • coarse features
  • visual fields
  • fundoscopy
Neck
  • goiter
  • nodules
  • scars
  • lymph nodes
  • movement with swallowing and tongue protrusion
Skin
  • striae (wide purple)
  • bruising
  • thin skin
  • pigmentation (buccal, creases)
  • vitiligo
  • pretibial myxedema
  • candidiasis
Musculoskeletal and neuro
  • proximal myopathy (stand from squat, arms crossed)
  • reflexes (brisk vs slow-relaxing)
  • peripheral neuropathy
  • Chvostek / Trousseau
Feet (DM)
  • ulcers
  • pulses
  • monofilament
4. Thyroid examination (two stations)
Preparation
  • wash hands
  • introduce
  • confirm name and DOB
  • explain
  • consent
  • expose neck to upper sternum
  • ask about pain
Hands
  • pulse rate and rhythm
  • tremor
  • sweating or dryness
  • acropachy
  • palmar erythema
  • onycholysis
Face
  • dry skin
  • sweating
  • loss of outer third of eyebrows (Hertoghe sign)
Eyes
  • lid retraction
  • lid lag
  • chemosis
  • exophthalmos (look from above and side)
  • eye movements
  • RAPD
Neck inspection
  • midline swelling, scars
  • repeat while swallowing water
  • repeat with tongue protrusion (thyroglossal cyst moves)
Palpation from behind
  • size, symmetry, consistency, nodules, tenderness
  • swallow while palpating
  • tongue protrusion
  • cervical lymph nodes
Percussion and auscultation
  • retrosternal dullness from sternal notch
  • bruit over each lobe
  • Pemberton sign
Status
  • biceps reflex
  • pretibial myxedema
  • proximal myopathy (stand with arms crossed)
Close
  • thank patient
  • dispose PPE and wash hands
  • summarize
  • suggest TFT, ECG, thyroid US and screening for associated autoimmune disease (DM, Addison, pernicious anemia, RA, SLE)
Signs specific to Graves
  • exophthalmos
  • chemosis
  • ophthalmoplegia
  • thyroid acropachy
  • pretibial myxedema
Painful thyroid
  • de Quervain (subacute, post-viral)
  • infectious (suppurative) thyroiditis
  • radiation thyroiditis
  • hemorrhage into a nodule
5. Exam add-ons by diagnosis
New T2DM

DKA / HHS

  • DVT signs
Graves
  • anxious

  • warm moist skin

  • eye signs
  • brisk reflexes
  • pretibial myxedema
Hypocalcemia
  • Chvostek sign (less specific), Trousseau sign (more specific), stridor, neck scar
  • chronic = cataract, dry skin, brittle nails, papilledema
Addison
  • postural hypotension
  • buccal / palmar crease / scar pigmentation
  • vitiligo
  • oral candidiasis (APS type 1)
  • dehydration
Cushing
  • moon face
  • buffalo hump
  • supraclavicular fat pads
  • wide purple striae > 1 cm
  • thin skin
  • bruising
  • proximal myopathy
  • HTN
  • hirsutism
  • kyphosis / vertebral tenderness
  • edema
Prolactinoma
  • visual fields
  • galactorrhea (breast exam)
  • hypogonadism
  • thyroid
  • chest wall scar
  • cirrhosis signs
Hypopituitarism
  • postural hypotension
  • pale hairless skin
  • puffy face
  • outer eyebrow loss
  • slow-relaxing reflexes
  • visual fields
Endocrine HTN
  • BP both arms
  • radio-femoral delay
  • fundoscopy
  • renal bruit
  • Cushingoid or acromegalic features
  • thyroid signs
  • polycystic kidneys
  • proximal weakness
6. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

Diabetes and its emergencies
1. New type 2 DM with nephropathy (42F, polyuria, polydipsia, polyphagia)
Clues
  • BMI 31, acanthosis nigricans, BP 156/98
  • dipstick 2+ glucose, 2+ protein, no ketones
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • liver profile (LFT, albumin)
  • metabolic (fasting glucose, random glucose, HbA1c, lipid profile)
  • urine (dipstick, urine ACR or 24-h protein)
Results
  • WBC 6 ×10⁹/L (6,000/µL)
  • Hb 14 g/dL (140 g/L)
  • Na 139 mmol/L (139 mEq/L)
  • K 4 mmol/L (4 mEq/L)
  • HbA1c 9.8% (84 mmol/mol)
  • BUN 14 mg/dL (urea 5 mmol/L)
  • creatinine 1.2 mg/dL (106 µmol/L)
  • urine glucose 2+, protein 2+, no ketones
  • albuminuria 330 mg/day (macroalbuminuria, > 300)
Diagnostic criteria
  • HbA1c ≥ 6.5%
  • FPG ≥ 126 mg/dL
  • 2-h OGTT ≥ 200 mg/dL
  • random ≥ 200 mg/dL with symptoms
Management
  • endocrinology
  • diet, exercise, weight loss, education
  • metformin then add-ons (SU, others) or insulin
  • nephrology and ACEi / ARB for nephropathy
Complications
  • macrovascular (CAD, stroke, PVD)
  • microvascular (neuropathy, nephropathy, retinopathy)
  • foot ulcers
Retinopathy
  • non-proliferative = microaneurysms, dot-blot hemorrhages, hard exudates, cotton-wool spots
  • proliferative = neovascularization, vitreous hemorrhage, retinal detachment
  • maculopathy = macular edema
  • refer ophthalmology for laser / anti-VEGF
2. DKA (18F, T1DM, abdominal pain and vomiting)
Clues
  • missed insulin because of nausea
  • thirst, polyuria
  • dry mucosa, Kussmaul breathing, fruity breath
DDx
  • DKA
  • gastroenteritis
  • acute pancreatitis
  • other acute abdomen
Workup
  • bedside (capillary glucose, capillary ketones, urine dipstick)
  • blood gas (VBG / ABG — pH, PCO2, HCO3, anion gap)
  • serum ketones (β-hydroxybutyrate)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Cl, Mg, PO4)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • CBC (with differential)
  • pancreatic (amylase, lipase)
  • cardiac (ECG, troponin)
  • HbA1c
  • septic screen if febrile (cultures, CXR)
Results
  • glucose 450 mg/dL (25 mmol/L)
  • pH 7.10
  • PCO2 25 mmHg (3.3 kPa)
  • HCO3 8 mmol/L (8 mEq/L)
  • Na 132 mmol/L
  • K 5.5 mmol/L with peaked T
  • Cl 95 mmol/L
  • anion gap ≈ 29 (Na − Cl − HCO3)
  • β-hydroxybutyrate 5 mmol/L
  • urine glucose 4+, ketones 4+
  • WBC 12 ×10⁹/L (12,000/µL)
  • Hb 14 g/dL (140 g/L)
  • platelets 250 ×10⁹/L
  • BUN 30 mg/dL (urea 10.7 mmol/L)
  • creatinine 1.5 mg/dL (133 µmol/L)
  • amylase 80 U/L (normal)
Management
  • ABC, IV access, ICU
  • IV fluids by hydration
  • replace K (give insulin only once K ≥ 3.3)
  • fixed-rate insulin infusion 0.1 U/kg/h
  • hourly glucose, electrolytes / VBG every 2–4 h
  • cardiac monitor
  • DVT and GI prophylaxis
  • treat trigger
  • switch to SC insulin with overlap
  • education
Stop insulin infusion when
  • glucose < 250 mg/dL plus 2 of pH > 7.3, HCO3 > 18, normal anion gap
  • and eating
Precipitants
  • missed insulin
  • new-onset DM (20–25%)
  • infection (30–40%)
  • MI
  • stroke
  • pancreatitis
  • SGLT2i
  • clozapine / olanzapine
  • cocaine
  • steroids
Complication of treatment
  • cerebral edema
  • hypokalemia
  • hypoglycemia
3. DKA precipitated by anterior STEMI (49M, T1DM)
Clues
  • missed insulin
  • 2 h chest pressure
  • Kussmaul, HR 120, BP 95/60
DDx
  • DKA from MI
  • DKA from infection / non-adherence
  • HHS
  • lactic acidosis, toxins
Workup
  • bedside (capillary glucose, ketones)
  • blood gas (VBG — pH, HCO3, anion gap)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Cl)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • cardiac (12-lead ECG, serial troponin)
  • CBC
  • imaging (CXR, echo)
Management
  • DKA protocol (0.9% saline, insulin 0.1 U/kg/h, K replacement) together with STEMI protocol (DAPT, morphine, urgent PCI)
  • CCU / ICU
  • statin, beta blocker, ACEi once stable
  • sick-day rules
  • MDT
Results
  • glucose 480 mg/dL (26.6 mmol/L)
  • serum ketones 5 mmol/L
  • pH 7.15
  • HCO3 10 mmol/L (10 mEq/L)
  • anion gap 24
  • ECG anterior ST elevation
  • troponin raised
  • CXR mild congestion
Examiner Qs
  • MI raises catecholamines and cortisol → insulin resistance and ketosis
  • treat both at the same time (better survival than sequential)
  • STEMI ECG = ST elevation, reciprocal depression, new LBBB
4. HHS (70M, T2DM, bedridden, fever, reduced LOC)
Clues
  • poor compliance
  • drowsy and confused 1 day
  • low intake
  • fever, dysuria
  • on insulin, metformin, indapamide
Workup
  • bedside (capillary glucose, ketones)
  • blood gas (VBG — pH, HCO3, anion gap)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Cl, Mg, PO4, corrected Na)
  • serum osmolality (measured + calculated)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • CBC
  • lactate
  • septic screen (blood / urine cultures, urinalysis, CXR)
  • ECG
  • CT brain if focal signs
Results
  • glucose 117 mmol/L (≈ 2,100 mg/dL)
  • ketones 0.7 mmol/L (< 3)
  • Na 122 mmol/L (corrected for glucose ≈ 170)
  • K 5.3 mmol/L
  • HCO3 22 mmol/L
  • pH 7.3
  • PCO2 18 mmHg (2.4 kPa)
  • creatinine 226 µmol/L (2.6 mg/dL)
  • urea 16 mmol/L (BUN ≈ 45 mg/dL)
  • osmolality 361 mOsm/kg (> 320)
  • lactate 3.4 mmol/L (31 mg/dL)
  • urine glucose ++++, no ketones
Osmolality formula
  • 2 × Na + glucose / 18 + BUN / 2.8 (mg/dL units)
Management
  • ABC, two large-bore lines, ICU
  • 0.9% saline (+3–6 L in 12 h) watching osmolality
  • correct K before insulin
  • insulin 0.1 U/kg/h once fluids under way
  • LMWH prophylaxis (heparin 5000 U BID if renal impairment)
  • antibiotics for UTI
  • monitor neuro status, glucose, electrolytes, osmolality
  • switch to SC insulin when eating
  • stop metformin
Examiner Qs
  • LMWH because dehydration + immobility = high VTE risk
  • treatment complications = cerebral edema (too-fast correction), arrhythmia, hypoglycemia, seizures
  • other causes of low GCS in DM = stroke, sepsis, hypoglycemia, uremia, drugs
Thyroid
5. Graves disease with ophthalmopathy (36F, 30F, 35F — three stations)
Clues
  • weight loss with increased appetite, heat intolerance, sweating, palpitations, diarrhea, anxiety, oligomenorrhea
  • smoker
  • family history
  • fine tremor, warm skin, diffuse goiter, lid retraction, lid lag, proptosis, diplopia, hyperreflexia
DDx
  • Graves
  • toxic MNG
  • toxic adenoma
  • thyroiditis (painless, subacute, postpartum, drug / iodine / radiation)
  • exogenous thyroxine
  • TSH-secreting adenoma
Workup
  • thyroid function (TSH, FT4, FT3)
  • thyroid antibodies (TSH-receptor antibody / TSI, anti-TPO, anti-thyroglobulin)
  • CBC (baseline before antithyroid drugs)
  • liver profile (baseline)
  • renal profile
  • ECG (AF)
  • imaging (thyroid US with Doppler, radioiodine / technetium uptake scan if TRAb negative)
Results
  • station 1: TSH 0.002 mIU/L (0.5–5)
  • FT4 3 ng/dL (≈ 39 pmol/L; normal 0.7–1.8 ng/dL)
  • FT3 6 pg/mL (≈ 9.2 pmol/L; normal 2.3–4.1 pg/mL)
  • CBC, renal, LFT normal
  • Station 2: TSH < 0.01 mIU/L
  • FT4 41 pmol/L (≈ 3.2 ng/dL; normal 7.5–14.5 pmol/L)
  • FT3 13.5 pmol/L (≈ 8.8 pg/mL; normal 3.8–6)
  • TRAb > 40 IU/L (< 1.5)
  • scan: diffuse homogeneous increased uptake
Management
  • endocrinology
  • beta blocker for symptoms
  • methimazole / carbimazole
  • radioactive iodine
  • total thyroidectomy
Ophthalmopathy
  • stop smoking
  • lubricant drops, raise head of bed
  • IV steroids or teprotumumab for active moderate–severe disease
  • ophthalmology
  • RAI can worsen eye disease
Side effects
  • antithyroid drugs = rash, arthralgia, agranulocytosis, hepatitis, ANCA vasculitis, teratogenicity
  • RAI = worse eye disease, radiation thyroiditis, hypothyroidism
  • surgery = hypothyroidism, hypoparathyroidism, recurrent laryngeal nerve injury, anesthesia risk
Pregnancy
  • PTU in 1st trimester then methimazole
  • RAI contraindicated
  • surgery in 2nd trimester
  • if planning pregnancy, consider RAI or surgery 6–12 months before
RAI precautions
  • avoid close contact with young children and pregnant women for a period after treatment
6. Papillary thyroid cancer from a thyroid nodule (47M; 22M — two stations)
Clues
  • painless nodule moving with swallowing, euthyroid
  • ask radiation exposure and family history (mother had thyroidectomy)
DDx
  • benign (follicular adenoma, colloid nodule, cyst)
  • malignant (papillary, follicular, medullary, anaplastic, lymphoma, metastasis)
  • parathyroid lesion
  • thyroglossal or branchial cyst
Workup
  • thyroid function (TSH, FT4 — uptake scan only if TSH low)
  • imaging (neck US with TI-RADS, cervical nodes)
  • FNA (Bethesda)
  • bone panel (Ca, PTH) if MEN suspected
  • calcitonin if family history of MTC
Results
  • TSH 0.9 mIU/L (0.5–5)
  • FT4 1.2 ng/dL (≈ 15.4 pmol/L; normal 0.8–1.8)
  • US solid hypoechoic nodule with microcalcifications (TI-RADS 4–5) → FNA papillary carcinoma
Worrisome US features
  • solid
  • hypoechoic
  • irregular / infiltrative margin
  • taller than wide
  • microcalcifications
  • extrathyroidal extension
  • central vascularity
  • abnormal lymph nodes
Next step
  • FNA
Management
  • active surveillance acceptable for low-risk microcarcinoma < 1 cm
  • otherwise total thyroidectomy or lobectomy ± neck dissection
  • radioactive iodine
  • levothyroxine (TSH suppression)
  • follow-up TFT, thyroglobulin, anti-TG, neck US
7. Medullary thyroid cancer — breaking bad news (28F, MEN2A)
Clues
  • neck lump
  • weight loss
  • hypercalcemia
  • hypertension (pheo + hyperparathyroidism)
SPIKES
  • private setting
  • ask who she wants present
  • explore what she knows
  • warning shot
  • give result simply
  • silence and empathy
  • plan with MDT (surgery, oncology)
  • support
  • follow-up
  • check understanding
  • questions
  • thank her
Workup
  • tumor markers (calcitonin — > 100 pg/mL ≈ diagnostic; CEA)
  • MEN screen (bone panel — Ca, PTH; plasma metanephrines)
  • genetics (RET proto-oncogene)
  • imaging (neck and mediastinal US; CT chest / abdomen or PET if calcitonin > 400 pg/mL)
Diagnosis
  • MTC with likely MEN2A (MTC + pheochromocytoma + hyperparathyroidism)
Key point
  • exclude pheochromocytoma before thyroid surgery
8. Post-thyroidectomy hypocalcemia (28F, 2 weeks after total thyroidectomy)
Clues
  • muscle twitching
  • carpopedal spasm
  • dehydrated
Workup
  • bone panel (albumin-corrected Ca or ionized Ca, PO4, Mg, PTH, ALP, 25-OH vitamin D)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR)
  • liver profile
  • ECG (long QT)
  • malabsorption tests / amylase only if suspected
Results
  • corrected Ca 1.43 then 1.41 mmol/L (≈ 5.7 mg/dL; normal 2.1–2.6 mmol/L = 8.5–10.5 mg/dL)
  • PO4 1.77 mmol/L (≈ 5.5 mg/dL; normal 0.81–1.45)
  • albumin 43 g/L (4.3 g/dL)
  • ALP 57 U/L
  • Mg 0.56 mmol/L (≈ 1.4 mg/dL; low)
  • intact PTH 11 ng/L = pg/mL (≈ 1.2 pmol/L; normal 15–65)
  • vitamin D low
Diagnosis
  • post-surgical hypoparathyroidism + hypomagnesemia + vitamin D deficiency
DDx
  • hypoparathyroidism (autoimmune, DiGeorge, infiltrative, radiation)
  • PTH resistance (pseudohypoparathyroidism, low Mg)
  • low vitamin D (diet, malabsorption, CKD, cirrhosis)
  • IV bisphosphonate
  • pancreatitis
Management
  • symptomatic = 10–20 mL 10% calcium gluconate in 50–100 mL over 10 min with ECG monitoring, repeat until symptom-free
  • IV magnesium
  • then infusion 100 mL 10% calcium gluconate in 1 L at 50–100 mL/h
  • oral calcium + calcitriol / alfacalcidol early
Adrenal
9. Primary adrenal insufficiency / APS (27F, T1DM + Hashimoto)
Clues
  • 3 months fatigue, postural dizziness, cramps, salt craving, darker skin, irregular cycles
  • BP 90/60 with orthostatic drop
Workup
  • CBC
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • liver profile
  • metabolic / endocrine (glucose, HbA1c, TSH, FT4, 8 AM cortisol + ACTH, renin / aldosterone, 21-hydroxylase antibodies, pregnancy test)
  • urinalysis
  • short Synacthen test if cortisol equivocal
Results
  • WBC 6 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 13 g/dL (130 g/L)
  • platelets 240 ×10⁹/L
  • Na 127 mmol/L (127 mEq/L)
  • K 5.2 mmol/L
  • Ca 9.2 mg/dL (2.3 mmol/L)
  • HbA1c 6.5% (48 mmol/mol)
  • fasting glucose 95 mg/dL (5.3 mmol/L)
  • TSH 0.8 mIU/L
  • creatinine 0.7 mg/dL (62 µmol/L)
  • 8 AM cortisol 3.2 µg/dL (≈ 88 nmol/L; normal 5–25 µg/dL)
  • ACTH 97 pg/mL (≈ 21 pmol/L; normal 10–60) → primary adrenal insufficiency
Confirm
  • ACTH (cosyntropin) stimulation test
  • peak cortisol < 18 mcg/dL = adrenal insufficiency (here 8.1)
Cause
  • high ACTH + pigmentation + other autoimmune disease = Addison / autoimmune polyglandular syndrome
  • confirm with 21-hydroxylase antibodies
Management
  • hydrocortisone replacement (± fludrocortisone)
  • IV fluids
  • sick-day rules (double dose when ill, IM injection kit)
  • medical alert bracelet
  • endocrine follow-up
10. Cushing disease (28F, "PCOS", amenorrhea, vertebral fracture)
Clues
  • central weight gain, hirsutism, acne, easy bruising, polyuria, low mood
  • no exogenous steroids
  • moon face, buffalo hump, supraclavicular fat, wide red striae, proximal weakness
  • BP 145/85, BMI 36
First-line tests (2 of 3)
  • 24-h urinary free cortisol
  • late-night salivary cortisol
  • 1 mg overnight dexamethasone suppression test
Results
  • 24-h UFC 265 and 246 µg/24 h (≈ 731 and 679 nmol/24 h; high)
  • 1 mg DST morning cortisol 4 µg/dL (≈ 110 nmol/L; > 1.8 µg/dL = 50 nmol/L means not suppressed)
Next
  • ACTH on 2 days → 67 pg/mL (≈ 14.7 pmol/L) = ACTH-dependent (pituitary vs ectopic)
DDx
  • pseudo-Cushing
  • metabolic syndrome
  • PCOS
  • factitious
Localize
  • pituitary MRI
  • high-dose DST / CRH / desmopressin test
  • MRI normal → inferior petrosal sinus sampling confirms Cushing disease
Management
  • MDT
  • transsphenoidal surgery
  • then repeat surgery, radiotherapy, medical therapy, or bilateral adrenalectomy
Drugs
  • pituitary-directed (cabergoline, pasireotide)
  • steroidogenesis inhibitors (ketoconazole, metyrapone, osilodrostat, etomidate, mitotane)
  • receptor blocker (mifepristone)
11. Primary aldosteronism / Conn's (45M, HTN on HCTZ, weakness)
Clues
  • proximal weakness, polyuria, polydipsia
  • BP 160/95
  • power 4/5
DDx
  • essential HTN on diuretic
  • primary aldosteronism
  • Cushing
  • pheochromocytoma
  • hypothyroidism
  • hyperparathyroidism
  • renal artery stenosis
  • licorice
  • renin-secreting tumor
Workup
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Cl, HCO3, Mg)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • urine (ACR)
  • endocrine screen (aldosterone / renin ratio, TFT, 1 mg DST, plasma metanephrines)
  • cardiac (ECG, echo)
  • imaging (renal artery Doppler; adrenal CT / MRI after biochemical confirmation; adrenal vein sampling before surgery)
Results
  • Na 142 mmol/L
  • K 2.8 mmol/L (2.8 mEq/L; low)
  • Cl 102 mmol/L
  • HCO3 30 mmol/L (high — metabolic alkalosis)
  • BUN 18 mg/dL (urea 6.4 mmol/L)
  • creatinine 1.0 mg/dL (88 µmol/L)
  • aldosterone 25 ng/dL (≈ 690 pmol/L; normal 2–9)
  • plasma renin activity 0.2 ng/mL/h (suppressed)
  • ARR 125 (> 20–30 positive)
  • ECG flat T waves and U waves
  • CT left adrenal adenoma
Management
  • replace K
  • stop HCTZ
  • spironolactone
  • endocrinology
  • adrenal vein sampling then adrenalectomy for unilateral adenoma
  • monitor K, renal function, BP
12. Pheochromocytoma (25M, headache and high BP 9 months)
History
  • headache, palpitations, sweating, flushing, tremor, anxiety, hypertensive crises
  • screen Cushing, thyroid, acromegaly, renal disease
  • family history of MEN2, VHL, NF1
Exam
  • BP both arms and postural
  • radio-femoral delay
  • fundi
  • renal bruit
  • endocrine features
  • polycystic kidneys
DDx
  • renovascular disease
  • pheochromocytoma
  • primary aldosteronism
  • Cushing
  • coarctation
Workup
  • catecholamine screen (plasma free metanephrines, 24-h urine fractionated metanephrines, chromogranin A)
  • other secondary-HTN screen (aldosterone / renin, 1 mg DST or 24-h UFC, TFT)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR) + urinalysis
  • imaging (renal Doppler; CT / MRI adrenals after biochemistry; MIBG / PET if extra-adrenal or metastatic)
  • genetics if young / bilateral
Results
  • plasma free metanephrine 1,200 pg/mL (≈ 6.1 nmol/L; normal < 65 pg/mL)
  • urine metanephrines 1,000 (≈ 3× upper limit)
  • FT4 1.2 ng/dL (15.4 pmol/L)
  • aldosterone 12 ng/dL (333 pmol/L)
  • renin activity 1.1 ng/mL/h (normal)
  • CT 2 cm right adrenal mass
Management
  • endocrinology
  • alpha-blockade first (phenoxybenzamine / doxazosin) for 10–14 days
  • add beta blocker only after alpha-blockade
  • high-salt diet and fluids
  • laparoscopic adrenalectomy
  • genetic testing
  • metanephrines 2–6 weeks after surgery then yearly
Prognosis
  • sporadic tumors usually cured
  • malignant, extra-adrenal or SDHB tumors recur in 10–20% → lifelong surveillance
Pituitary
13. Prolactinoma (29F, prolactin 200 ng/mL)
Clues
  • irregular cycles and breast pain 6 months
  • no headache or visual symptoms
  • no drugs
  • not pregnant
  • no hypothyroid, CKD, CLD, PCOS
Workup
  • prolactin (repeat, macroprolactin if asymptomatic, dilute if large mass — hook effect)
  • exclude secondary causes (pregnancy test β-hCG, TSH / FT4, renal profile, liver profile, drug review)
  • other pituitary hormones (IGF-1, LH / FSH, estradiol, cortisol)
  • imaging (pituitary MRI)
  • visual fields if macroadenoma
Results
  • prolactin 200 ng/mL (≈ 4,240 mIU/L; > 200 suggests prolactinoma)
  • TSH 2.3 mIU/L, FT4 17 pmol/L (1.3 ng/dL) normal
  • pregnancy test negative
  • MRI 9 mm pituitary microadenoma
Causes of high prolactin
  • prolactinoma
  • physiological (pregnancy, nursing, stress, sleep, seizure)
  • hypothyroidism
  • CKD
  • liver disease
  • PCOS
  • chest wall trauma
  • drugs
  • stalk compression
  • idiopathic
Treat if
  • symptomatic microprolactinoma or any macroprolactinoma
  • no treatment for asymptomatic micro
Goals
  • normalize prolactin
  • shrink tumor
  • restore gonadal function / fertility
Drugs
  • cabergoline first-line (normalizes prolactin and shrinks tumor in ≈ 90%)
  • bromocriptine (preferred if pregnancy planned)
  • side effects = nausea, postural hypotension, nasal stuffiness, depression, Raynaud, constipation, valvulopathy at high cabergoline doses (echo if > 2 mg/week)
Follow-up
  • prolactin from 1 month after starting
  • MRI at 1 year (3 months if macroadenoma, rising prolactin or new symptoms)
  • visual fields for macroadenoma
14. Sheehan syndrome / hypopituitarism (40F, G3P3, fatigue and amenorrhea)
Clues
  • PPH needing transfusion 5 years ago
  • amenorrhea and failure to lactate since
  • fatigue, postural dizziness, cold intolerance, weight gain, low libido
  • BP 95/60 with postural drop, puffy face, loss of body hair, slow-relaxing reflexes
Workup
  • CBC
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • glucose (fasting)
  • pituitary axes (TSH + FT4; 8 AM cortisol + ACTH; prolactin; IGF-1; LH, FSH, estradiol; β-hCG)
  • bone (vitamin D, DXA)
  • imaging (pituitary MRI)
Results
  • Hb 11 g/dL (110 g/L; normocytic)
  • fasting glucose 75 mg/dL (4.2 mmol/L)
  • Na 130 mmol/L (130 mEq/L)
  • K 4.5 mmol/L
  • vitamin D 20 ng/mL (50 nmol/L; low)
  • TSH 0.3 mIU/L with FT4 6 pmol/L (≈ 0.47 ng/dL) = central hypothyroidism
  • cortisol 2 µg/dL (≈ 55 nmol/L) with ACTH 15 pg/mL (3.3 pmol/L) inappropriately normal
  • IGF-1 60 ng/mL (low)
  • prolactin 20 ng/mL (normal)
  • LH 3 IU/L, FSH 4 IU/L, estradiol 25 pg/mL (≈ 92 pmol/L) all low
  • MRI partially empty sella
  • DXA low bone mass
Diagnosis
  • panhypopituitarism from Sheehan syndrome (PPH history + hormone deficiencies + MRI)
Other causes
  • tumors (adenoma, craniopharyngioma, meningioma, metastases)
  • infiltrative / inflammatory (hypophysitis, sarcoid)
  • surgery
  • radiation
  • trauma
  • apoplexy
  • opiates
  • checkpoint inhibitors
Management
  • hydrocortisone first, then levothyroxine (1.6 mcg/kg) to avoid adrenal crisis
  • combined estrogen / progesterone
  • calcium and vitamin D
  • endocrinology, gynecology, fertility
Examiner Qs
  • axes lost first = GH > LH / FSH > TSH > ACTH
  • long-term = osteoporosis, infertility, CVD risk, adrenal crisis
Questions asked across several stations
Hydrocortisone before thyroxine
  • in any combined adrenal + thyroid deficiency
  • to prevent adrenal crisis
Sick-day rules
  • double glucocorticoid dose with illness
  • IM hydrocortisone if vomiting
  • medical alert bracelet
Pregnancy and Graves
  • PTU in 1st trimester
  • methimazole later
  • no RAI
Alpha before beta
  • in pheochromocytoma never start a beta blocker first
Thyroid nodule pathway
  • TSH → US → FNA by US risk features (scan only if TSH low)
DKA vs HHS
  • DKA = ketones > 3, pH < 7.3, HCO3 < 18, high AG
  • HHS = glucose > 600 mg/dL, osmolality > 320, little ketosis, older patient, days of onset
7. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

Eye photo — 36F, palpitations, heat intolerance, weight loss (case 5)
Findings
  • marked proptosis (exophthalmos) with upper-lid retraction
  • visible sclera
  • conjunctival injection / periorbital fullness
Diagnosis
  • Graves ophthalmopathy (thyroid eye disease) — Graves disease
Face photo — 35F, weight loss, palpitations, heat intolerance (case 5)
Findings
  • bilateral lid retraction with scleral show above the iris (right more than left)
  • staring look
  • proptosis
Diagnosis
  • Graves ophthalmopathy → stop smoking, lubricants, steroids or teprotumumab if active moderate–severe
  • avoid radioiodine in active eye disease
Thyroid ultrasound — 22M, right thyroid nodule (case 6)
Findings
  • solid hypoechoic nodule with irregular margins and multiple punctate echogenic foci (microcalcifications)
Diagnosis
  • suspicious nodule (TI-RADS 5) → FNA → papillary thyroid carcinoma
ECG — 28F, twitching and carpopedal spasm after thyroidectomy (case 8)
Findings
  • sinus rhythm
  • long ST segment with prolonged QT interval
  • normal T-wave morphology
Diagnosis
  • hypocalcemia (post-thyroidectomy hypoparathyroidism)
Photo — same patient (case 8)
Findings
  • carpal spasm (flexed wrist and MCPs, extended fingers, adducted thumb) after inflating the BP cuff above systolic for 3 minutes
Diagnosis
  • positive Trousseau sign → hypocalcemia
  • give IV calcium gluconate, then oral calcium + calcitriol
Photos — 28F, "PCOS", amenorrhea, vertebral fracture (case 10)
Findings
  • central obesity with thin limbs
  • wide purple abdominal striae
  • dorsocervical fat pad (buffalo hump)
  • round plethoric face
Diagnosis
  • Cushing syndrome (ACTH-dependent pituitary — Cushing disease in this station)

Hematology & Oncology

How to use this

The 17 hematology stations repeat a small set of patterns: low platelets (ITP vs TMA), hemolysis (AIHA vs G6PD), high counts (PV, CML), malignancy (AML, DLBCL), sickle cell, and transfusion / anticoagulation problems (often wrapped in an angry-patient station). Nearly every station asks for CBC, peripheral film, hemolysis panel and coagulation first.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • in bleeding or confused patients secure ABC and vitals first
Stations covered
  • thrombocytopenia (ITP ×2, TTP ×3, aHUS)
  • hemolysis (warm AIHA ×2 incl. CLL, G6PD)
  • myeloproliferative (PV, CML)
  • malignancy (AML, DLBCL)
  • sickle cell (VOC, TRALI + pregnancy counseling)
  • angry patient (missed DVT prophylaxis ×2, warfarin given in error)
1. Core hematology history (every case)
Anemia symptoms
  • fatigue
  • SOB
  • palpitations
  • chest pain
  • dizziness
  • syncope
Bleeding
  • site (skin, gums, nose, GI, urine, menses), onset, spontaneous vs after trauma, platelet-type (petechiae, mucosal) vs factor-type (joints, muscles, delayed)
  • previous bleeding with surgery / dental work
Hemolysis
  • jaundice
  • dark urine
  • previous episodes
  • gallstones
  • transfusions
Infection
  • fever
  • recurrent infections
  • sick contacts
  • travel
B symptoms
  • fever > 38 °C for > 2 weeks, drenching night sweats, weight loss > 10% in 6 months
  • pruritus (after hot bath = PV), bone pain
Lumps and organs
  • lymph nodes
  • abdominal fullness / early satiety (splenomegaly)
Thrombosis
  • DVT
  • PE
  • stroke
  • unusual sites (portal, hepatic, cerebral veins)
  • recurrent miscarriage
Neuro
  • headache
  • confusion
  • seizures
  • focal weakness
  • visual change
Renal
  • oliguria
  • hematuria
  • bloody diarrhea (HUS)
Autoimmune
  • joint pain
  • rash
  • mouth ulcers
  • hair loss (SLE / APS)
Women
  • menstrual loss
  • pregnancy
  • postpartum
  • preeclampsia
Drugs
  • anticoagulants, antiplatelets, NSAIDs, herbals
  • quinine, sulfa, penicillin, heparin (immune cytopenias)
  • clopidogrel (TTP)
  • oxidant drugs (G6PD)
  • chemo / radiation
  • recent vaccination or transfusion
Past history
  • liver disease
  • CKD
  • malignancy
  • transplant
  • bariatric surgery (copper, B12, iron deficiency)
Family and social
  • bleeding disorders, hemoglobinopathies, malignancy
  • smoking, alcohol, occupational chemicals / radiation
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Thrombocytopenia / petechiae
ITP
  • isolated bruising and gum bleeding
  • recent viral illness or vaccination
  • drugs (aspirin worsens bleeding)
  • no systemic features
TMA (TTP / HUS)
  • fever
  • confusion
  • headache
  • seizures
  • chest pain
  • oliguria
  • bloody diarrhea (STEC-HUS)
  • pregnancy / postpartum
  • HIV
  • drugs (quinine, clopidogrel, chemo)
  • SLE / APS
Other causes
  • HIV, HCV, H. pylori, EBV, malaria
  • liver disease (hypersplenism)
  • MDS
  • SLE
  • APS
  • heparin exposure (HIT)
  • post-transfusion purpura
  • DIC (sepsis)
B. Hemolytic anemia (jaundice + anemia)
Immune (AIHA)
  • autoimmune disease
  • lymphoma / CLL (nodes, B symptoms)
  • drugs (penicillin, quinine)
  • infections (EBV, CMV, mycoplasma)
G6PD
  • young male, previous similar episodes with transfusion, trigger drug (sulfa, dapsone, primaquine, rasburicase, nitrofurantoin) or fava beans or infection
  • family history
Others
  • sickle cell / thalassemia (ethnicity, family)
  • TMA
  • malaria
  • prosthetic valve
C. Polycythemia / thrombocytosis / splenomegaly
Hyperviscosity
  • headache
  • blurred / transient loss of vision
  • dizziness
  • paresthesia
MPN features
  • aquagenic pruritus
  • erythromelalgia (burning red extremities)
  • unusual-site thrombosis (portal vein)
  • gout
  • abdominal fullness
  • weight loss
  • sweats
Secondary causes
  • smoking / shisha
  • COPD
  • OSA
  • cyanotic heart disease
  • high altitude
  • CO exposure
  • testosterone / anabolic steroids / EPO
  • renal or liver tumors
D. Lymphadenopathy
Nodes
  • duration
  • painless vs painful
  • localized vs generalized
  • growth
Malignancy
  • B symptoms
  • pruritus
  • alcohol-induced node pain (Hodgkin)
Infection / exposure
  • TB
  • brucella (unpasteurized milk)
  • cat scratch
  • toxoplasma (undercooked meat)
  • HIV / EBV / CMV
  • travel
  • sexual history
Other
  • SLE, Still, sarcoid
  • drugs (phenytoin, allopurinol)
E. Pancytopenia / acute leukemia
Marrow failure triad
  • anemia symptoms
  • bleeding (epistaxis, gums)
  • recurrent infections
Infiltration
  • bone pain
  • gum hypertrophy
  • skin lesions
  • CNS symptoms
  • mediastinal mass symptoms
Risk factors
  • prior chemo / radiotherapy
  • benzene
  • MDS
  • bariatric surgery (copper / B12)
F. Sickle cell crisis
Pain
  • site
  • severity
  • usual crisis pattern
  • home analgesia and last dose
  • opioid tolerance
Triggers
  • cold
  • dehydration
  • infection
  • exertion
  • travel
  • alcohol
  • stress
Red flags
  • fever (infection / osteomyelitis)
  • chest pain / SOB (acute chest syndrome)
  • neuro symptoms (stroke)
  • priapism
  • abdominal pain / falling Hb (sequestration)
Baseline
  • hydroxyurea adherence
  • other drugs (L-glutamine, crizanlizumab, voxelotor)
  • transfusions and chelation
  • previous complications (ACS, stroke, AVN, leg ulcers)
  • vaccines
G. DVT / anticoagulation problems
DVT
  • immobility
  • missed prophylaxis
  • surgery
  • cancer
  • pregnancy / OCP
  • previous VTE
  • family history
Over-anticoagulation
  • bleeding sites
  • interacting drugs and diet
3. Core hematology exam (every case)
Vitals
  • temperature
  • HR
  • BP (high in aHUS / PV)
  • RR
  • SpO2
  • mental status
General
  • pallor
  • jaundice
  • plethora
  • cyanosis
  • cachexia
  • performance status (ECOG)
Skin and mucosa
  • petechiae
  • purpura
  • ecchymoses
  • gum bleeding / hypertrophy
  • mouth ulcers
  • oral thrush
  • rash
  • leg ulcers
  • erythromelalgia
Lymph nodes
  • cervical, supraclavicular, axillary, epitrochlear, inguinal
  • Waldeyer ring
  • size, consistency, tenderness, fixation
Abdomen
  • hepatomegaly
  • splenomegaly (size in cm)
  • CLD stigmata
  • masses
  • testicular mass
Cardiorespiratory
  • murmurs (flow, IE)
  • heart failure
  • crackles
  • effusion
  • SVC obstruction signs
Neuro
  • GCS
  • orientation
  • focal deficit
  • cranial nerves
  • meningism
  • fundi (hyperviscosity, papilledema)
Joints and limbs
  • arthritis (SLE)
  • hemarthrosis
  • leg swelling / DVT
  • limb ischemia
4. Exam add-ons by diagnosis
ITP
  • petechiae and bruising only
  • no nodes, no spleen, no systemic signs (if present, think again)
TTP / HUS
  • fever
  • confusion or focal deficit
  • petechiae
  • jaundice
  • pallor
  • high BP (aHUS)
  • oliguria
AIHA
  • pallor, jaundice, splenomegaly
  • look for nodes (CLL / lymphoma) and SLE signs
  • heart failure if severe
G6PD / hereditary hemolysis
  • jaundice
  • pallor
  • splenomegaly
  • gallstones
  • family pattern
PV
  • plethora
  • conjunctival / retinal vein engorgement
  • splenomegaly
  • HTN
  • erythromelalgia
  • scratch marks
  • thrombosis signs
CML / myelofibrosis
  • massive splenomegaly (> 20 cm)
  • pallor
  • bruising
Lymphoma
  • node map
  • Waldeyer ring
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • B-symptom fever
Acute leukemia
  • pallor
  • petechiae
  • infection sites
  • gum hypertrophy (AML M4 / M5)
  • nodes
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • testes
  • meningism
Sickle cell
  • pallor
  • jaundice
  • dehydration
  • chest signs (ACS)
  • spleen / liver (sequestration)
  • hip range of motion (AVN, septic arthritis, osteomyelitis)
  • neuro deficit
  • priapism
5. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

Thrombocytopenia
1. Primary immune thrombocytopenia (45F, petechiae and gum bleeding; two stations)
Clues
  • 3 days petechiae, bruising, gum bleeding
  • on amlodipine and aspirin
  • no systemic features
  • exam only petechiae
Workup
  • CBC (with differential) + peripheral film (exclude pseudothrombocytopenia — citrate tube, schistocytes, blasts)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT, fibrinogen)
  • hemolysis screen if anemic (retics, LDH, bilirubin, DAT)
  • secondary causes (HIV, HCV, HBV, H. pylori, ANA / APS, TSH, pregnancy test)
  • bone marrow only if atypical or > 60 years
Results
  • platelets 12 ×10⁹/L (12,000/µL) with normal Hb, WBC and film (large platelets, no schistocytes)
  • PT / PTT normal
DDx
  • ITP
  • drug-induced ITP
  • infection (HIV, HCV, H. pylori, EBV, malaria)
  • hypersplenism
  • MDS
  • SLE
  • APS
More tests
  • Coombs
  • HCV
  • HIV
  • pregnancy test
  • blood group
  • ANA
  • TFT
  • antiphospholipid antibodies
  • H. pylori
Bone marrow?
  • Not needed — no atypical features (blasts, dysplasia, other cytopenias, age > 60)
Management
  • admit and control bleeding
  • stop aspirin
  • steroids ± IVIG / anti-D
  • PPI, calcium, vitamin D with steroids
  • tranexamic acid for mucosal bleeding
  • treat iron deficiency
  • hematology
Second line
  • TPO receptor agonists (romiplostim, eltrombopag)
  • rituximab
  • splenectomy
2. Idiopathic TTP (30F, fever, fatigue, Hb 8, platelets 10)
Clues
  • fever, exertional SOB, chest pain, bruising, mild confusion
  • no focal deficit
Workup
  • CBC + peripheral film (schistocytes)
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, indirect bilirubin, DAT)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT, fibrinogen, D-dimer)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN)
  • liver profile
  • ADAMTS13 activity + inhibitor
  • PLASMIC score
  • other (pregnancy test, HIV / hepatitis, ANA, troponin, urinalysis)
  • imaging (CT brain if neurologic)
Results
  • Hb 8 g/dL (80 g/L)
  • WBC 6 ×10⁹/L
  • platelets 9–10 ×10⁹/L (10,000/µL)
  • MCV 85 fL
  • PT 11 s, APTT 30 s (normal)
  • creatinine 125–148 µmol/L (1.4–1.7 mg/dL)
  • AST 70, ALT 50 IU/L
  • bilirubin 40 µmol/L (2.3 mg/dL)
  • haptoglobin 0.1 g/L (low; 0.5–2.2)
  • retics 5%
  • LDH 1,300 U/L (140–280)
  • pregnancy test negative
  • film: schistocytes
DDx
  • TTP
  • STEC-HUS
  • aHUS
  • DIC
  • SLE
  • catastrophic APS
  • malignancy-associated TMA
More tests
  • ADAMTS13 activity and inhibitor
  • autoimmune screen
  • Coombs
  • ECG and troponin (cardiac involvement)
  • HBV / HCV / HIV
  • cultures
  • CT / MRI brain
Management
  • ICU, central line, hematology
  • urgent plasma exchange (1–1.5 plasma volumes daily until platelets and LDH normal for 2 days)
  • FFP only if TPE delayed
  • high-dose steroids (+ PPI, Ca, vitamin D)
  • rituximab
  • caplacizumab (anti-vWF)
  • platelets only for life-threatening bleeding or line insertion
  • aspirin after recovery
Prognosis
  • mortality fell from > 90% to ≈ 10% with TPE
3. Postpartum TTP (19F, 2 weeks after delivery)
Clues
  • confusion
  • petechial rash
  • jaundice
Workup
  • CBC + film
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, bilirubin, DAT)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT, fibrinogen)
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • pregnancy-related (urine protein, BP — HELLP / preeclampsia)
  • imaging (CT brain)
Results
  • WBC 10 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 7 g/dL (70 g/L)
  • platelets 9 ×10⁹/L
  • LFT and coagulation normal
  • creatinine 188 µmol/L (2.1 mg/dL)
  • CT brain normal
  • film schistocytes
DDx
  • TTP
  • HUS
  • SLE
  • catastrophic APS (also HELLP / preeclampsia in pregnancy)
More tests
  • ADAMTS13
  • ANA / dsDNA
  • Coombs
  • ECG
  • troponin
  • virology
  • MRI brain
  • cultures
  • 24-h urine protein
Result
  • ECG and troponin normal
  • Coombs negative
  • autoimmune and virology negative
  • MRI brain normal
  • ADAMTS13 0.1% (< 10%) → TTP
Scores
  • PLASMIC score
  • French score (platelets and creatinine)
Management
  • ICU
  • TPE until platelets normal, then 2–3 more days
  • pulse steroids
  • rituximab × 4
  • caplacizumab
Caplacizumab
  • humanized nanobody binding vWF A1 domain
  • blocking platelet GPIb-IX-V binding
4. TTP with spontaneous bruising and confusion (35F)
Clues
  • bruising for a week, intermittent disorientation, petechiae
  • only OTC analgesics
Workup
  • CBC + film
  • hemolysis panel (LDH, bilirubin, haptoglobin, retics, DAT)
  • coagulation (PT, PTT, fibrinogen — normal in TTP, abnormal in DIC)
  • renal profile
  • B12 (pseudo-TMA)
  • ADAMTS13
  • imaging (CT brain)
Results
  • Hb 9.5 g/dL (95 g/L)
  • platelets 18 ×10⁹/L
  • WBC 9 ×10⁹/L
  • creatinine 122 µmol/L (1.4 mg/dL; mildly high)
  • LDH and bilirubin high
  • film schistocytes
DDx
  • TTP
  • HUS
  • DIC
  • drug-induced TMA
  • SLE / APS
  • B12-deficiency pseudo-TMA
Before results
  • PLASMIC score to justify starting plasma exchange
Management
  • immediate plasma exchange in ICU
  • hematology
  • high-dose steroids
  • avoid platelets unless life-threatening bleeding or procedure
  • rituximab, caplacizumab
5. Atypical HUS (28F, petechiae, fatigue, dark scanty urine)
Clues
  • recent minor URTI, no diarrhea
  • BP 170/110, HR 110
  • petechiae
DDx
  • TTP
  • HUS / aHUS
  • APS
  • SLE
Workup
  • CBC + film
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, bilirubin, DAT)
  • coagulation (PT / INR, PTT, fibrinogen, D-dimer)
  • renal profile (creatinine, eGFR, BUN) + electrolytes (K)
  • urine (urinalysis with microscopy, UPCR)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • immunology (ANA, ENA, ANCA, anti-GBM, C3 / C4, APS screen)
  • ADAMTS13 activity
  • stool Shiga toxin / E. coli O157
  • viral screen (HBV, HCV, HIV)
  • liver profile
  • imaging (renal US, CXR)
Results
  • Hb 7 g/dL (70 g/L; normocytic)
  • platelets 21 ×10⁹/L
  • numerous schistocytes
  • creatinine 350–470 µmol/L (≈ 4.0–5.3 mg/dL) in the two stations
  • K 5.2 mmol/L
  • coagulation normal
  • urine RBC casts
  • ANA, ANCA negative
  • C3 low, C4 normal
  • ADAMTS13 > 10% (excludes TTP)
  • HBV, HCV, HIV negative
Diagnosis
  • atypical HUS (complement-mediated; ADAMTS13 not severely low)
Management
  • monitored bed
  • supportive care, transfusion
  • eculizumab (C5 inhibitor)
  • plasma exchange while awaiting ADAMTS13
  • BP control
  • dialysis if needed
  • nephrology, hematology, obstetrics
  • meningococcal vaccine before eculizumab
Complications
  • anemia
  • folate deficiency
  • VTE
  • renal failure
  • treatment complications
Hemolysis
6. Warm AIHA secondary to CLL (70F, SOB, jaundice, fatigue)
Clues
  • 1 week fatigue, jaundice, SOB
  • on ACEi
  • hepatosplenomegaly, raised JVP, crackles, edema
Workup
  • CBC (with differential) + film (spherocytes, smudge cells)
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, indirect bilirubin, urine urobilinogen)
  • DAT (IgG, C3) + indirect Coombs
  • blood bank (group and crossmatch)
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • coagulation
  • CLL work-up (flow cytometry, LDH, β2-microglobulin)
