  • imaging (US abdomen; CT CAP for staging)
Results
  • WBC 55 ×10⁹/L, mainly lymphocytes with smudge cells
  • Hb 6 g/dL (60 g/L)
  • platelets 500 ×10⁹/L
  • spherocytes
  • LDH 2,000 U/L
  • indirect bilirubin 120 µmol/L (≈ 7 mg/dL)
  • retics 9%
  • DAT 3+ (IgG + C3)
  • renal and coagulation normal
  • mild splenomegaly
  • urine urobilinogen 3+
DDx
  • warm AIHA
  • CTD / vasculitis
  • TMA
  • HUS
  • hepatitis
  • drug-induced AIHA
  • lymphoma
More tests
  • echo
  • ECG
  • cultures
  • iron / B12 / folate
  • ANA / dsDNA / C3 / C4
  • viral screen
  • CT CAP
  • flow cytometry
  • node or marrow biopsy
Diagnosis
  • warm AIHA secondary to CLL (flow cytometry, nodes above and below diaphragm)
Management
  • ICU
  • transfuse least-incompatible blood if symptomatic
  • methylprednisolone 1 g × 3 then prednisolone 1 mg/kg taper
  • rituximab 375 mg/m² weekly × 4
  • Ca, vitamin D, PPI
  • folic acid
  • VTE prophylaxis
  • plasmapheresis if persistent
  • splenectomy last (vaccinate 2 weeks before)
7. Primary warm AIHA (30M)
Workup
  • CBC + film
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, bilirubin)
  • DAT (IgG, C3) + cold agglutinins
  • secondary causes (ANA, HIV, HBV, HCV, SPEP, CLL flow cytometry)
  • imaging (CT chest / abdomen / pelvis for lymphoma)
Results
  • WBC 6 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 6 g/dL (60 g/L; microcytic hypochromic picture on the sheet) with spherocytes
  • platelets 500 ×10⁹/L
  • LDH 2,000 U/L
  • indirect bilirubin 120 µmol/L (≈ 7 mg/dL)
  • retics 9%
  • DAT 3+ IgG / C3
  • ANA, viral screen, CT CAP negative
Management
  • monitored bed, hydration
  • transfusion and folic acid
  • pulse then tapering steroids
  • rituximab weekly × 4
  • IVIG if no response
  • plasmapheresis and splenectomy if persistent
  • VTE prophylaxis (D-dimer and Doppler if raised)
Splenectomy
  • if steroids and rituximab fail or are not tolerated
  • vaccinate against pneumococcus, meningococcus and Hib ≥ 2–3 weeks before
  • thromboprophylaxis after
8. G6PD deficiency (19M, jaundice and dark urine after drug for dental infection)
Clues
  • previous similar episode needing transfusion
  • tachycardia, jaundice, pallor, spleen 2 fingers
Workup
  • CBC + film (bite cells) + supravital stain (Heinz bodies)
  • hemolysis panel (retics, LDH, haptoglobin, indirect bilirubin, urine hemoglobin)
  • DAT
  • renal profile (pigment nephropathy)
  • imaging (US gallbladder)
Results
  • WBC 11 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 6 g/dL (60 g/L)
  • platelets 500 ×10⁹/L
  • LDH 2,000 U/L
  • retics 9%
  • indirect bilirubin 120 µmol/L (≈ 7 mg/dL)
  • polychromasia, bite cells and Heinz bodies
  • DAT negative
  • gallstones on US
DDx
  • G6PD
  • hereditary pyropoikilocytosis
  • thalassemia
  • immune hemolysis
  • sickle cell
Tests
  • G6PD fluorescent spot test
  • quantitative enzyme assay after recovery (can be falsely normal during hemolysis due to young cells)
  • molecular testing
  • Hb electrophoresis
Diagnosis
  • G6PD deficiency (X-linked recessive)
Management
  • admit
  • transfuse
  • stop offending drugs
  • IV fluids
  • treat infection
  • folic acid 5 mg
  • monitor urine output (dialysis if AKI)
  • avoid oxidant drugs and fava beans
  • splenectomy rarely
Myeloproliferative and malignancy
9. JAK2-positive polycythemia vera (66M, headache, HTN, blurred vision)
Clues
  • 2 months pain and tingling in extremities, headache, new HTN
  • plethora
  • splenomegaly
Workup
  • CBC (with differential, Hct) + film
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • coagulation
  • urinalysis
  • imaging (abdominal US with Doppler — spleen, portal / hepatic veins)
Results
  • WBC 16 ×10⁹/L (normal differential)
  • Hb 19 g/dL (190 g/L)
  • Hct 59%
  • platelets 660 ×10⁹/L
  • spleen 20 cm with chronic portal vein thrombosis
DDx
  • PV
  • ET
  • myelofibrosis
  • CML
  • EPO-secreting tumor
  • secondary polycythemia
Tests
  • molecular (JAK2 V617F → exon 12; BCR-ABL to exclude CML)
  • serum EPO
  • B12, LDH, uric acid
  • imaging (CT CAP)
  • bone marrow biopsy
Results
  • JAK2 positive
  • low EPO
  • high B12 / LDH
  • BCR-ABL negative
High risk
  • age > 60 or prior thrombosis
Management
  • venesection to Hct < 45%
  • hydroxyurea
  • aspirin 81 mg
  • lifelong anticoagulation for portal vein thrombosis
  • hematology
10. Chronic myeloid leukemia (70M, abdominal fullness, weight loss)
Clues
  • fatigue, anorexia, SOB
  • splenomegaly without nodes
Workup
  • CBC (with differential) + film
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • coagulation
  • LDH, uric acid
  • imaging (abdominal US with Doppler)
Results
  • WBC 66 ×10⁹/L with neutrophilia and basophilia (whole myeloid spectrum on film)
  • Hb 15 g/dL (150 g/L)
  • platelets 660 ×10⁹/L
  • LFT, renal, coagulation normal
  • spleen 24 cm, no vein thrombosis
DDx
  • CML
  • ET
  • myelofibrosis
  • PV
  • solid tumor
Tests
  • molecular (BCR-ABL PCR / FISH, karyotype for Philadelphia chromosome; JAK2 if negative)
  • B12, LDH
  • imaging (CT CAP)
  • bone marrow (phase — blasts %) → BCR-ABL positive, JAK2 negative
Diagnosis
  • CML (BCR-ABL positive)
Management
  • tyrosine kinase inhibitors (imatinib, nilotinib, dasatinib)
  • hematology
Phases
  • chronic
  • accelerated
  • blast
11. Acute myeloid leukemia t(8;21) (50M, epistaxis and recurrent infections)
Clues
  • 2 months worsening epistaxis, 3 chest infections in 3 months, fatigue, fever
  • bariatric surgery 15 years ago
  • pale, tachycardic
Workup
  • CBC (with differential) + film (blasts, Auer rods)
  • hematinics (iron, B12, folate, copper — exclude reversible causes)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • infection (blood cultures, CXR)
  • hemolysis panel
  • coagulation (PT, PTT, fibrinogen, D-dimer — DIC)
  • tumor lysis labs (K, PO4, Ca, uric acid, LDH, creatinine)
  • liver profile
  • bone marrow with flow cytometry, cytogenetics and molecular (FLT3, NPM1, CEBPA, KIT)
  • HLA typing
  • echo before anthracycline
Results
  • WBC 17 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 9 g/dL (90 g/L)
  • platelets 50 ×10⁹/L
  • film 22% blasts with dysplastic neutrophils
  • marrow t(8;21) RUNX1-RUNX1T1 (favorable, core-binding factor AML)
DDx
  • AML
  • ALL
  • MDS
  • aplastic anemia
  • copper deficiency
  • reactive
Confirm
  • bone marrow with flow cytometry, cytogenetics, molecular tests
  • TLS labs (uric acid, LDH, K, PO4, Ca)
  • viral screen
  • ECG, echo, HLA typing
Result
  • MPO, CD13, CD33, CD34, CD117 positive
  • t(8;21) → AML (favorable risk)
Management
  • admit to hematology
  • transfuse
  • correct coagulopathy
  • febrile → cultures and broad-spectrum antibiotics
  • antimicrobial prophylaxis
  • fertility preservation
  • TLS prophylaxis (fluids, allopurinol / rasburicase)
  • induction 7 + 3 (cytarabine + anthracycline)
  • consolidation high-dose cytarabine
After induction
  • favorable risk = HiDAC only
  • intermediate / high risk = allogeneic SCT in first remission
Poor prognosis
  • adverse cytogenetics (monosomy 7, complex karyotype, FLT3-ITD)
  • older age
  • poor PS
  • therapy-related or secondary AML
  • no remission after induction
12. Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (40M, multiple neck masses)
Clues
  • 1 month painless enlarging right neck node
  • fever > 3 weeks, drenching sweats, > 10% weight loss
  • hard cervical and axillary nodes 3–4 cm
  • no spleen
DDx
  • lymphoma / leukemia / metastasis
  • infection (TB, brucella, HIV, EBV, CMV)
  • SLE / Still
  • sarcoid
  • HLH
Workup
  • CBC + film
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • LDH, uric acid
  • coagulation
  • infection screen (brucella, TB — QuantiFERON, HIV, EBV, CMV, HBV / HCV)
  • ANA
  • imaging (contrast CT neck / chest / abdomen / pelvis, then PET-CT)
  • tissue (excisional node biopsy with immunohistochemistry, FISH for MYC / BCL2 / BCL6)
  • bone marrow
Next
  • excisional lymph node biopsy (not FNA)
Result
  • large B cells CD19 / CD20 / CD22 / CD79a +
  • BCL2 +
  • MYC rearranged
  • Ki-67 > 80% → DLBCL
Staging
  • LDH, uric acid, HBV / HCV / HIV, PET-CT
  • nodes above diaphragm only + B symptoms = Ann Arbor stage IIB
Management
  • hematology-oncology
  • fertility preservation
  • R-CHOP (or DA-EPOCH-R for high-grade / double-hit)
  • CNS prophylaxis if high risk
  • HBV prophylaxis before rituximab
  • CAR-T for relapse
Sickle cell and transfusion
13. Sickle cell vaso-occlusive crisis with hip pain (23M)
Workup
  • CBC + retics (vs baseline)
  • hemolysis panel (LDH, bilirubin)
  • infection (CRP, procalcitonin, blood cultures)
  • renal profile
  • blood bank (extended-phenotype crossmatch)
  • imaging (hip X-ray / MRI for AVN; CXR if chest symptoms)
Results
  • WBC 3.2 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 5.9 g/dL (59 g/L; baseline 8–9)
  • platelets 322 ×10⁹/L
  • retics 2.5% (inappropriately low — consider aplastic crisis / parvovirus)
  • procalcitonin normal
  • CRP unchanged from baseline
DDx
  • acute VOC
  • avascular necrosis of femoral head
  • osteomyelitis / septic arthritis
  • drug-seeking
Management
  • admit
  • incentive spirometry
  • O2 if SpO2 < 94%
  • hematology
  • IV fluids
  • transfuse for symptomatic Hb drop
  • rapid analgesia ladder (acetaminophen, NSAIDs, opioids / PCA)
  • DVT prophylaxis
  • continue folic acid and hydroxyurea
  • septic screen
  • hip X-ray then MRI
Hydroxyurea benefits
  • raises HbF
  • lowers WBC and retics
  • improves red-cell deformability
Hydroxyurea side effects
  • myelosuppression, nausea, hair thinning, skin / mouth ulcers
  • teratogenic (stop 3 months before conception in women, 6 months in men)
Iron overload
  • suspect when ferritin > 2500 or by liver MRI
  • chelate (deferoxamine, deferasirox, deferiprone)
  • avoid unnecessary transfusion
Long-term care
  • yearly BP, urine protein, dilated eye exam
  • vaccines (influenza, pneumococcal, meningococcal, Hib)
  • transcranial Doppler to age 16
  • penicillin prophylaxis to age 5
14. TRALI during transfusion in sickle cell disease (30F)
Immediate
  • stop the transfusion
  • keep the bag and tubing
  • check vitals
  • recheck patient ID and product label, look at the bag for discoloration / bubbles
  • assess volume status
  • inform blood bank
Workup
  • imaging (STAT CXR)
  • transfusion reaction work-up (repeat group and crossmatch, DAT, hemolysis panel — LDH, bilirubin, haptoglobin, urine hemoglobin; blood cultures from patient and bag)
  • BNP (TACO vs TRALI)
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • blood gas (PaO2 / FiO2)
  • troponin
  • tryptase (anaphylaxis)
  • coagulation (DIC)
  • donor anti-HLA / anti-HNA antibodies
DDx
  • TRALI (hypotension, ± fever)
  • TACO (hypertension, overload, high BNP, responds to diuretic)
  • anaphylaxis (urticaria, angioedema, hypotension)
  • severe febrile non-hemolytic
  • septic reaction (fever + hypotension)
  • acute hemolytic reaction (fever, hypotension, DIC)
TRALI definition
  • new non-cardiogenic pulmonary edema during or within 6 h of transfusion
  • donor anti-HLA / anti-neutrophil antibodies activate recipient neutrophils in the lung
Management and prevention
  • supportive (O2, ventilation; no diuretics)
  • quarantine the donor
  • use plasma from male or never-pregnant donors
TACO risk factors
  • extremes of age
  • positive fluid balance
  • heart or kidney dysfunction
  • rapid plasma transfusion
  • emergency surgery
Pregnancy counseling in SCD
  • genetic counseling and partner's hemoglobinopathy status
  • update vaccines
  • folic acid 5 mg
  • stop hydroxyurea 3 months before, stop ACEi / ARB and chelators
  • echo for pulmonary HTN (maternal mortality up to 50%)
  • risks of preeclampsia, preterm labor, abruption, IUGR, VOC, VTE, acute chest syndrome
  • avoid triggers (dehydration, cold, stress)
Angry patient / anticoagulation stations
15. DVT after missed prophylaxis (70M, bedbound with pneumonia; two stations)
Task
  • angry patient framework (see General): ABC
  • safety
  • let him vent
  • empathize
  • apologize without blaming
  • explain plan and complaint process
  • follow-up
Leg swelling DDx
  • cellulitis / abscess
  • DVT, thrombophlebitis, venous / arterial insufficiency, post-thrombotic
  • lymphedema
  • myositis, rhabdomyolysis
  • compartment syndrome
  • Baker cyst
  • neuropathy
  • tumors
Thrombophilia testing if
  • recurrent VTE
  • VTE at young age
  • strong family history
  • unusual sites
Anticoagulate superficial vein thrombosis if
  • ≥ 5 cm long
  • within 5 cm of the deep system (saphenofemoral junction)
  • or positive D-dimer
Cancer-associated VTE (brain metastases)
  • LMWH (enoxaparin)
  • duration indefinite while cancer active
16. Warfarin given in error with gum bleeding (55M)
Task
  • angry patient framework
Gum bleeding DDx
  • gingivitis / periodontitis
  • hard brushing
  • infection
  • leukemia
  • hemophilia / coagulopathy
  • platelet disorders (ITP, TTP)
  • vitamin C or K deficiency
  • anticoagulants
  • pregnancy
Results
  • INR 4 with only gum bleeding after brushing
  • no other bleeding
Vitamin K or PCC?
  • No (minor bleeding only)
  • stop warfarin and recheck INR in 24–48 h
PCC complications
  • thrombosis (≈ 1.4%)
  • HIT (contains heparin)
PCC over FFP
  • faster reversal
  • small volume (less overload)
  • less TRALI
Increase warfarin effect
  • amiodarone
  • macrolides
  • fluoroquinolones
  • cotrimoxazole
  • metronidazole
  • fluconazole
Decrease warfarin effect
  • rifampin
  • carbamazepine
  • phenytoin
  • OCPs
  • vitamin K-rich food
Questions asked across several stations
First labs for any cytopenia
  • CBC with differential
  • peripheral film
  • retics
  • LDH
  • haptoglobin
  • bilirubin
  • DAT
  • coag
Schistocytes + low platelets = TMA
  • start plasma exchange for presumed TTP (PLASMIC ≥ 6) while ADAMTS13 is pending
Platelet transfusion
  • avoid in TTP / HIT unless life-threatening bleeding
Splenectomy prep
  • pneumococcal, meningococcal, Hib vaccines ≥ 2 weeks before
  • VTE prophylaxis after
Steroid cover
  • PPI
  • calcium
  • vitamin D
Before rituximab
  • hepatitis B screening
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

Peripheral blood film — 30F, fever, fatigue, Hb 8, platelets 10 (case 2)
Findings
  • many fragmented red cells (schistocytes / helmet cells), polychromatic cells, virtually no platelets
  • normal PT / aPTT on the lab sheet
Diagnosis
  • microangiopathic hemolytic anemia with thrombocytopenia → TTP until proven otherwise (ADAMTS13, PLASMIC score, urgent plasma exchange)
Peripheral blood film — 35F, spontaneous bruising and confusion (case 4)
Findings
  • schistocytes (fragmented, helmet and triangular cells) and markedly reduced platelets
Diagnosis
  • MAHA + thrombocytopenia + neurologic features → thrombotic thrombocytopenic purpura
Urine microscopy — 28F, petechiae, fatigue, dark scanty urine (case 5)
Findings
  • red blood cell casts
Diagnosis
  • glomerular injury (nephritic sediment) — with schistocytes
  • platelets 21
  • creatinine 470 and ADAMTS13 > 10% → atypical HUS (complement-mediated) → eculizumab / ravulizumab
Peripheral blood film + supravital stain — 19M, jaundice and dark urine after a drug for dental infection (case 8)
Findings
  • top and lower left: bite cells and blister cells (arrows)
  • lower right (supravital stain): Heinz bodies (precipitated hemoglobin) at the red-cell edges
Diagnosis
  • oxidative hemolysis from G6PD deficiency → stop the trigger, supportive care
  • measure G6PD level after recovery (falsely normal during acute hemolysis)

Infectious Diseases

How to use this

The 17 ID stations are dominated by four organisms that keep returning: TB (pleural, peritoneal, miliary / meningeal), Brucella (spine, sacroiliac, endocarditis), HIV (PCP, Kaposi, new diagnosis), and sepsis from a source. The marks reward an exposure history (travel, animals, raw milk, sex, sick contacts) more than in any other specialty.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • septic or confused patient = ABCDE, IV access, cultures before antibiotics
Stations covered
  • HIV (PCP ×3, Kaposi sarcoma, new HIV disclosure)
  • TB (pleural effusion ×2 incl. pregnancy, peritonitis, miliary + meningitis)
  • Brucella (spondylodiscitis, sacroiliitis, endocarditis with root abscess)
  • others (brain abscess from septic prosthetic knee, Salmonella + epididymitis, dengue, gonococcal urethritis, urosepsis → septic shock)
1. Core ID history (every case)
Fever
  • onset
  • duration
  • pattern (continuous, intermittent, nocturnal, relapsing)
  • Tmax
  • rigors
  • response to antipyretics / antibiotics already given
Constitutional
  • night sweats
  • weight loss (how much, how fast)
  • fatigue
  • anorexia
Source hunt by system
  • ENT / dental
  • chest (cough, sputum, hemoptysis, SOB, pleuritic pain)
  • heart (palpitations, murmur history)
  • GI (diarrhea, vomiting, pain, jaundice)
  • GU (dysuria, flank / testicular pain, discharge, ulcers)
  • CNS (headache, neck stiffness, photophobia, confusion, seizures, focal signs)
  • MSK (back pain, joint swelling)
  • skin (rash, ulcers, nodules)
Exposures
  • sick contacts (TB — family, housekeeper, prison, work)
  • travel (endemic areas, Hajj)
  • animals (sheep, camels, cats)
  • raw milk / undercooked meat
  • food eaten outside
Sexual history
  • partners (number, gender), protection, previous STDs
  • IV drugs
  • transfusions
  • tattoos
Immune status
  • HIV
  • DM
  • steroids / biologics / chemo
  • transplant
  • splenectomy
  • alcohol
Past history
  • prosthetic joints / valves
  • valve disease (rheumatic, AS)
  • recent hospital admission or procedures
  • previous TB / brucellosis
Drugs and allergies
  • recent antibiotics (C. diff, partially treated infection)
  • drug allergies
  • vaccines (BCG, HBV, pneumococcal, influenza)
Occupation and social
  • farmer, healthcare, prison officer
  • smoking, alcohol
  • living situation
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Fever + cough / dyspnea
TB
  • chronic cough > 2–3 weeks
  • hemoptysis
  • sweats
  • weight loss
  • contact (prison, housekeeper from endemic country)
PCP (HIV)
  • subacute dry cough
  • exertional dyspnea
  • desaturation on walking
  • thrush
  • weight loss
  • high-risk sex
  • stopped ART
Pleural effusion
  • pleuritic chest pain
  • progressive SOB
Other
  • CAP (acute, productive)
  • NTM
  • Kaposi (purple skin lesions)
  • malignancy (smoker)
B. Fever + back / joint pain
Brucellosis
  • raw milk (camel, sheep, goat)
  • animal contact
  • farmer
  • undulant fever
  • sweats
  • sacroiliac or lumbar pain
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • epididymo-orchitis
Spinal infection red flags
  • weakness
  • numbness
  • bladder / bowel incontinence
Endocarditis
  • known valve disease (AS)
  • poor dental hygiene
  • new murmur
  • emboli
TB spine
  • chronic back pain
  • gibbus
  • sweats
C. Fever + headache / confusion
Meningitis / encephalitis
  • neck stiffness
  • photophobia
  • seizures
  • rash (meningococcus)
  • Hajj travel
Brain abscess
  • headache
  • vomiting
  • blurred vision (papilledema)
  • focal deficit
  • a source (septic joint, dental, ear, endocarditis)
TB meningitis
  • weeks of headache with chronic cough
  • weight loss
  • cranial nerve palsies
Dengue / viral hemorrhagic fever
  • high fever
  • retro-orbital pain
  • bone pain ("break-bone")
  • rash
  • bleeding
  • travel (Najran, Jeddah, Makkah)
D. Fever + diarrhea
Gastroenteritis
  • food eaten outside
  • travel (Thailand)
  • sick contacts
  • blood or mucus
  • vomiting
C. diff
  • recent antibiotics or admission
Proctitis / STD
  • anal sex
  • tenesmus
  • genital symptoms (epididymitis)
E. Genital discharge / STD
Discharge
  • onset, color, amount
  • dysuria
  • ulcers, vesicles, warts
  • testicular or anal pain
Systemic
  • fever
  • joint pain and red eyes (reactive arthritis)
  • rash (secondary syphilis)
  • oral ulcers
Sexual history
  • partners
  • protection
  • previous STDs
  • partner symptoms
F. HIV-related
HIV course
  • date of diagnosis
  • ART regimen and adherence (reasons for stopping)
  • last CD4 and viral load
  • prophylaxis (TMP-SMX)
Opportunistic infection screen
  • thrush / odynophagia (Candida)
  • skin lesions (Kaposi, zoster)
  • vision loss (CMV retinitis, toxoplasma)
  • headache and confusion (Cryptococcus, toxoplasma)
  • diarrhea
  • cough (PCP, TB)
3. Core ID exam (every case)
Vitals
  • temperature
  • HR
  • BP (shock)
  • RR
  • SpO2 at rest and on walking
  • GCS
General
  • ill / toxic
  • cachexia
  • pallor
  • jaundice
  • hydration
  • lymph nodes (cervical, axillary, inguinal)
Mouth and ENT
  • thrush
  • ulcers
  • dental hygiene / abscess
  • tonsils
  • ear discharge
  • sinuses
Skin
  • rash type (petechial, macular on palms / soles, vesicular)
  • Kaposi lesions
  • IE stigmata (splinters, Osler, Janeway)
  • injection marks
  • pressure sores
Eyes
  • conjunctival hemorrhage
  • Roth spots
  • retinitis
  • papilledema
Heart
  • new murmur
  • rub
  • heart failure
Chest
  • consolidation
  • effusion (dullness, reduced breath sounds)
  • crackles
Abdomen
  • tenderness
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • ascites
  • renal angle
Genitals and anus
  • discharge
  • ulcers
  • testicular swelling / tenderness
  • warts
MSK
  • spine tenderness
  • sacroiliac tenderness
  • joint effusions
  • prosthetic joint
Neuro
  • meningism
  • focal deficit
  • cranial nerves
  • mental status
4. Exam add-ons by diagnosis
PCP / HIV
  • thrush
  • desaturation on exertion with clear chest
  • lymphadenopathy
  • Kaposi lesions
  • retinitis
TB effusion
  • reduced expansion
  • stony dullness
  • reduced breath sounds
  • no tracheal shift (small–moderate)
Brucellosis
  • fever
  • hepatosplenomegaly
  • sacroiliac / lumbar tenderness
  • epididymo-orchitis
  • new murmur (endocarditis)
Endocarditis
  • murmur
  • splinters
  • Osler / Janeway
  • Roth spots
  • splenomegaly
  • heart block
Brain abscess
  • papilledema
  • focal deficit
  • drowsiness
  • source (joint, teeth, ears)
Dengue / hemorrhagic fever
  • high fever
  • shock (BP 70/50)
  • petechial rash
  • tourniquet test
  • bleeding
  • hepatomegaly
Sepsis
  • hypotension, tachycardia, tachypnea, low GCS, mottled skin, low urine output
  • look for source (renal angle, chest, skin, lines)
STD
  • urethral discharge
  • genital ulcers
  • inguinal nodes
  • testicular tenderness
  • joints
  • eyes
  • rash
5. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

HIV
1. PCP as the first presentation of HIV (34M; two stations)
Clues
  • 3 weeks fever, dyspnea, dry cough, 5 kg loss
  • smoker
  • multiple male and female partners
  • T 38, SpO2 94%, oral thrush, clear chest
  • ± purple skin lesions (Kaposi)
DDx
  • pneumonia (viral, bacterial, atypical)
  • TB
  • NTM
  • PCP
  • Kaposi sarcoma
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • LDH
  • blood gas (PaO2, A-a gradient)
  • infection (blood cultures; HIV 4th-generation Ag / Ab → viral load + CD4; β-D-glucan)
  • induced sputum / BAL (bacterial culture, AFB smear + TB PCR, PCP immunofluorescence / GMS / PCR)
  • imaging (CXR → CT chest if CXR normal)
  • G6PD before dapsone / primaquine
Results
  • station 1 WBC 7 ×10⁹/L, Hb 11 g/dL (110 g/L), platelets 230 ×10⁹/L
  • station 2 WBC 3.4 ×10⁹/L, Hb 11 g/dL, platelets 150 ×10⁹/L
  • renal and LFT normal
  • PaO2 73 mmHg (9.7 kPa) on room air
  • LDH 460 U/L
  • blood culture no growth
  • HIV positive, viral load 100,000 copies/mL
  • sputum AFB negative, PCP immunofluorescence positive
  • CXR bilateral interstitial infiltrates
Management
  • admit
  • airborne isolation until TB excluded
  • TMP-SMX
  • fluconazole for thrush
  • CD4 and HIV genotype
  • start ART within 2 weeks
  • ID consult
  • counsel on diagnosis and adherence
2. PCP in HIV off ART for 3 months
Clues
  • ART 3 years, CD4 220 last year
  • cough, fatigue, mild fever
  • desaturation on walking, clear exam
Workup
  • blood gas (PaO2, A-a gradient)
  • LDH
  • β-D-glucan
  • CBC
  • renal and liver profile
  • HIV viral load + CD4 + resistance genotype
  • induced sputum / BAL (PCP stain / PCR, AFB, culture)
  • imaging (CXR, CT chest)
Expected results
  • low PaO2 (< 70 mmHg = 9.3 kPa) with wide A-a gradient (≥ 35 mmHg)
  • high LDH
  • raised β-D-glucan
CXR
  • may be normal
  • bilateral interstitial infiltrates
  • pneumothorax
Confirm
  • induced sputum or BAL with silver (GMS) stain / immunofluorescence / PCR
  • CT chest (ground glass)
Organism
  • Pneumocystis jirovecii (PJP)
Management
  • airborne isolation until TB excluded
  • TMP-SMX first line
  • alternatives = TMP + dapsone, clindamycin + primaquine, atovaquone, IV pentamidine
  • delay ART about 2 weeks (until TB / cryptococcus excluded)
Add steroids if
  • PaO2 < 70 mmHg on room air or A-a gradient ≥ 35 mmHg
Pregnancy
  • TMP-SMX is still preferred
  • TMP-dapsone is the alternative
3. AIDS-related Kaposi sarcoma (40M, fever and bleeding oral lesion)
Clues
  • 6 weeks fever, 15 kg weight loss
  • painless bleeding oral lesion
  • painless purple nodules on legs for 6 months
  • transfusion in India 5 years ago
  • cat at home
DDx
  • Kaposi sarcoma
  • mucormycosis
  • angiosarcoma
  • bacillary angiomatosis (Bartonella)
Workup
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • HIV test + CD4 + viral load
  • tissue (biopsy of the oral lesion ± HHV-8 PCR / LANA stain, tissue culture)
  • other infection (Bartonella serology — bacillary angiomatosis)
  • extent (ENT / sinus endoscopy, upper endoscopy, CT / MRI sinuses, US of leg lesions, CXR)
Results
  • Hb ≈ 10 g/dL (100 g/L; mild anemia)
  • WBC, platelets and coagulation normal
  • CD4 ≈ 150 cells/µL
  • biopsy spindle-cell proliferation with slit-like vascular channels (HHV-8 positive)
  • leg US subcutaneous hypoechoic vascular nodules
  • no nasal lesions
Management
  • symptom control (pain, bleeding, secondary infection)
  • local therapy (cryotherapy, radiotherapy, intralesional chemo) for limited disease
  • systemic chemotherapy for extensive / visceral disease
  • start or optimize ART
  • ID, oncology, dermatology, ENT
  • follow CD4 and viral load
ART classes
  • NRTI (tenofovir)
  • NNRTI (efavirenz)
  • PI (darunavir)
  • INSTI (dolutegravir)
High-risk groups for KS
  • HIV with low CD4
  • transplant recipients
  • elderly Mediterranean / Eastern European men
  • sub-Saharan Africa
Visceral sites
  • lungs
  • GI tract
  • lymph nodes
Mucormycosis
  • Mucorales fungi
  • surgical debridement + liposomal amphotericin B, step down to posaconazole / isavuconazole
Bacillary angiomatosis
  • Bartonella
  • doxycycline, azithromycin or rifampicin
4. New HIV diagnosis — disclosure (34M, married)
Clues
  • 6 kg loss over 4 months, night sweats, oral ulcers, flu-like illness 3 months ago, pneumonia 2 months ago
  • frequent traveler with unprotected sex
  • oral thrush
Workup
  • HIV (4th-generation Ag / Ab → confirmatory differentiation assay; CD4; viral load; genotype resistance)
  • baseline labs (CBC, renal profile, liver profile, lipid profile, glucose / HbA1c, urinalysis)
  • co-infections (HBV, HCV, syphilis, TB — IGRA, toxoplasma IgG, CMV)
  • imaging (CXR)
  • G6PD (before dapsone / primaquine)
  • pregnancy test for partner
Diagnosis
  • HIV with advanced disease (AIDS) — CD4 < 200
  • high viral load
  • opportunistic infection
Disclosure (SPIKES)
  • private setting
  • explore what he knows
  • warning shot
  • give result
  • silence
  • empathy
  • explain ART is effective
  • respect autonomy but stress duty to inform partner (and public health reporting)
  • safe sex
  • don't share razors / toothbrushes
  • MDT (ID, psychiatry, social work)
  • follow-up
  • contacts
  • check understanding
Tuberculosis
5. Tuberculous pleural effusion (39M, prison officer)
Clues
  • 3 weeks progressive SOB, dry cough, low-grade fever, right chest pain
  • sick contact at work
  • reduced expansion, stony dullness, absent breath sounds right base
DDx
  • pleural effusion (TB, parapneumonic, malignant)
  • pneumonia
  • lung cancer
  • bronchiectasis
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • serum LDH + total protein (for Light's criteria)
  • cardiac (troponin, pro-BNP)
  • TB tests (PPD / IGRA, sputum AFB × 3)
  • autoimmune profile
  • imaging (CXR, chest US, CT)
  • diagnostic thoracentesis (appearance, cell count and differential, pH, glucose, protein, LDH, ADA, Gram stain / culture, AFB smear / culture, cytology)
  • pleural biopsy if needed
CXR
  • right effusion with meniscus sign
Pleural fluid
  • cell count / differential
  • protein
  • LDH
  • glucose
  • pH
  • culture
  • cytology
  • ADA
  • AFB
Results
  • WBC 10.5 ×10⁹/L
  • ESR 95 mm/h
  • CRP 50 mg/L
  • serum LDH 185 U/L
  • serum protein 9 g/dL (90 g/L)
  • sputum AFB negative
  • PPD / QuantiFERON positive
  • autoimmune negative
  • fluid yellow, turbid, > 70% lymphocytes, pH 7.27, glucose 65 mg/dL (3.6 mmol/L), protein 6.5 g/dL (65 g/L; ratio 0.72 > 0.5), LDH 130 U/L (ratio 0.7 > 0.6), culture negative, cytology negative, ADA ≈ 80 U/L (> 40) → lymphocytic exudate = TB pleuritis
Diagnosis
  • tuberculous pleural effusion
Management
  • baseline LFT, renal, eye exam
  • RIPE (rifampicin, isoniazid, pyrazinamide, ethambutol) for 2 months then rifampicin + isoniazid for 4 months
  • chest drain if large or complicated
  • pulmonary and ID referral
6. TB pleuritis in pregnancy (27F, 12 weeks)
Clues
  • 4 weeks left pleuritic chest pain, mild SOB and dry cough
  • housekeeper from Uganda with chronic cough
DDx
  • TB
  • viral pleuritis
  • pneumonia with effusion / abscess
  • PE
  • malignancy
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, ESR)
  • PE screen (leg Doppler, D-dimer)
  • TB tests (IGRA, induced sputum AFB + TB PCR)
  • autoimmune and viral screen (ANA, RF, anticardiolipin, TSH, HBV, HCV, HIV, RPR)
  • imaging (shielded CXR, echo)
  • thoracentesis (cell count, protein, LDH, glucose, ADA, AFB smear / culture)
Results
  • WBC 11 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 11.2 g/dL (112 g/L)
  • Hct 33.9%
  • platelets 434 ×10⁹/L
  • renal and LFT normal
  • Doppler and D-dimer negative
  • small left effusion
  • CRP 87 mg/L
  • ESR 62 mm/h
  • IGRA positive
  • sputum AFB and PCR negative
  • screen negative
  • echo normal
  • fluid 650 mL, WBC 1,000 with 80% lymphocytes, LDH 400 U/L, protein 5.4 g/dL (54 g/L), glucose 66 mg/dL (3.7 mmol/L), ADA 50 U/L
  • fluid AFB / PCR negative
Management
  • admit, airborne isolation
  • treat as active TB with isoniazid, rifampicin, pyrazinamide, ethambutol for 6 months (pyridoxine with INH)
  • ID and obstetrics
7. Miliary TB with TB meningitis (44M)
Clues
  • 1 month headache with neck pain, photophobia, back pain
  • fever
  • > 20 kg weight loss
  • 4 months blood-tinged cough
  • heavy smoker
  • bronchial breathing, crackles, cervical and axillary nodes
DDx
  • TB
  • lung cancer
  • lymphoma
  • CTD
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile (baseline before RIPE)
  • electrolytes (Na — SIADH)
  • HIV
  • blood cultures (+ mycobacterial)
  • induced sputum / BAL (AFB smear, GeneXpert, culture)
  • imaging (CXR, MRI brain with contrast before LP)
  • LP (opening pressure, cell count, protein, glucose with paired serum glucose, AFB smear, GeneXpert, culture, ADA)
Results
  • WBC 12 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 11 g/dL (110 g/L)
  • platelets 200 ×10⁹/L
  • renal and LFT normal
  • CXR miliary pattern
  • blood culture no growth
  • AFB smear positive in induced sputum and CSF
Management
  • admit
  • airborne isolation
  • ID
  • HIV testing
  • RIPE then RI for 9–12 months for CNS TB
  • add dexamethasone for TB meningitis
8. Tuberculous peritonitis (35M)
Clues
  • 3 months abdominal distension, diffuse pain, nocturnal fever 38.5, sweats, 10 kg loss
  • pale
  • ascites without CLD
Workup
  • CBC
  • liver profile + albumin
  • renal profile
  • coagulation (INR)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • blood cultures
  • HIV
  • imaging (CT abdomen / pelvis, CXR)
  • paracentesis (cell count and differential, albumin for SAAG, total protein, amylase, cytology, Gram stain and culture, AFB smear / culture, ADA, TB PCR)
  • laparoscopic peritoneal biopsy if needed
Results
  • Hb 9 g/dL (90 g/L)
  • MCV 65 fL (microcytic)
  • CRP 32 mg/L
  • ESR 70 mm/h
  • LFT and renal normal
  • ascites straw-colored, WBC 500 cells/mm³ mostly lymphocytes, fluid albumin 5.5 g/dL vs serum 3.5 g/dL (SAAG < 1.1 g/dL), fluid protein 4 g/dL (40 g/L; > 2.5), cytology, Gram, AFB smear and culture negative → high ADA expected (> 39 U/L)
DDx
  • TB or bacterial peritonitis
  • peritoneal carcinomatosis / lymphoma
  • pancreatitis
  • nephrotic syndrome
Confirm
  • peritoneal biopsy (laparoscopy) → caseating granulomas
  • AFB
  • TB PCR positive
Management
  • ID
  • RIPE for 8 weeks then INH + RIF to at least 6 months
Counseling on TB drugs
  • adherence (resistance)
  • orange secretions (RIF)
  • hepatotoxicity (INH, RIF, PZA)
  • vision changes (EMB)
  • neuropathy (INH — give pyridoxine)
  • gout (PZA)
  • drug-induced lupus (INH)
  • rash and drug interactions incl. OCP (RIF)
Brucellosis
9. Brucella spondylodiscitis (65M, Sudan, camel milk)
Clues
  • 3 weeks intermittent nocturnal fever
  • worsening chronic back pain
  • warmth on medial thigh
  • DM, HTN, aortic stenosis
  • farmer drinking camel milk
  • poor dental hygiene
  • ejection murmur
  • lumbar tenderness
DDx
  • brucellosis
  • infective endocarditis
  • malaria
  • spinal infection (osteomyelitis / discitis, epidural abscess)
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • infection (≥ 2 blood cultures held 3–4 weeks, urine culture, Brucella SAT / Coombs titer, malaria film, TB tests)
  • imaging (CXR, TTE, MRI spine with contrast)
  • CT-guided biopsy (culture, TB PCR, histology) if cultures negative
Results
  • WBC 11 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 11.2 g/dL (112 g/L)
  • Hct 33.9%
  • platelets 400 ×10⁹/L
  • renal and LFT normal
  • ESR 45 mm/h
  • blood and urine cultures negative
  • Brucella titer 1:2560 (≥ 1:160 significant)
  • malaria film negative
  • CXR clear
  • TTE no vegetation
  • MRI L4–5 discitis without abscess
Management
  • admit
  • streptomycin or gentamicin + doxycycline ± rifampicin
  • at least 3 months for spondylitis
  • ID
10. Brucella sacroiliitis (40M, raw milk)
Clues
  • 3 weeks fever and back pain
  • raw milk
  • palpable liver
  • right sacroiliac tenderness
  • no neuro deficit
DDx
  • brucellosis
  • TB
  • lymphoma
  • ankylosing spondylitis
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • infection (blood cultures, Brucella titer, HIV, PPD / IGRA)
  • rheumatology (HLA-B27)
  • imaging (MRI sacroiliac joints, CXR, abdominal US, echo)
  • bone / joint biopsy (bacterial culture, TB PCR / culture) if titer negative
Results
  • Brucella titer 1:1280
  • mildly raised LFT
  • echo normal
  • MRI right sacroiliitis
Confirm
  • blood or tissue culture / PCR
Management
  • combination of 2–3 drugs (aminoglycoside / doxycycline / rifampicin; alternatives FQ, TMP-SMX, ceftriaxone) for 12 weeks for focal disease
  • ID
  • education (boil milk, cook meat, avoid animal contact)
Pregnancy < 36 weeks
  • non-focal = rifampicin + TMP-SMX
  • focal = ceftriaxone + rifampicin + TMP-SMX
Pregnancy ≥ 36 weeks
  • rifampicin alone (non-focal) or ceftriaxone + rifampicin (focal) until delivery (TMP-SMX risks kernicterus)
  • then full combination
  • delay breastfeeding until treatment ends
11. Brucella endocarditis with aortic root abscess (30M, farm)
Clues
  • 3 months fever, sweats, back and joint pain
  • exertional dyspnea and orthopnea
  • sheep contact and raw milk
  • macular rash on palms and soles
  • grade 3/6 diastolic murmur (AR)
  • spleen 2 cm
Workup
  • CBC
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • renal profile + urinalysis
  • liver profile
  • infection (≥ 3 blood cultures — hold 3–4 weeks, urine / sputum cultures, Brucella titer)
  • cardiac (troponin, ECG, CXR, TTE → TEE)
  • imaging (CT chest / abdomen / pelvis for emboli, splenic abscess)
Results
  • gram-negative coccobacilli after 3 weeks
  • first-degree AV block with LBBB
  • para-aortic collection
Diagnosis
  • Brucella endocarditis complicated by aortic root abscess (new conduction block = root abscess)
Management
  • ID
  • cardiothoracic surgery (valve replacement / drainage)
  • gentamicin or streptomycin + doxycycline 100 mg BID + rifampicin 600–900 mg daily for at least 6 months
Complications
  • heart block
  • arrhythmia
  • heart failure
  • emboli (stroke, limb)
  • splenic infarct / abscess
  • death
Other infections
12. Brain abscess from septic prosthetic knee (68M)
Clues
  • 7 days fever and frontal headache, vomiting, blurred vision
  • recent septic arthritis of left knee prosthesis, refused removal
  • drowsy, T 38.9, BP 102/60, papilledema, swollen tender knee
DDx
  • brain abscess
  • meningitis
  • septic arthritis / osteomyelitis
  • brain tumor
  • TB
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, ESR)
  • infection (3 sets of blood cultures, urine and stool culture, AFB, joint aspiration — cell count, Gram stain, culture)
  • cardiac (echo for endocarditis)
  • imaging (CXR, contrast CT / MRI brain — no LP with papilledema or mass)
Results
  • WBC 19 ×10⁹/L (19,000/µL)
  • renal and LFT normal
  • urine, stool and AFB negative
  • S. aureus in blood and joint cultures
  • joint fluid WBC 100,000 cells/µL
  • echo and CXR normal
  • CT left frontal ring-enhancing abscess
Management
  • stabilize, IV fluids
  • neurosurgery (aspiration / drainage)
  • orthopedics (washout / prosthesis removal)
  • IV vancomycin + ceftriaxone + metronidazole, narrowed to culture
  • 6–8 weeks
Complications
  • meningitis
  • ventriculitis / rupture into ventricle
  • herniation
  • venous sinus thrombosis
  • seizures
13. Salmonella gastroenteritis with epididymitis (20M, back from Thailand)
Clues
  • 5 days non-bloody diarrhea, fever 38.6, vomiting, cramps, headache
  • ate out
  • left testicular pain
  • unprotected sex 4 months ago
  • testicular swelling and tenderness
DDx
  • bacterial or viral gastroenteritis
  • C. diff
  • STD-related proctocolitis
  • liver abscess
  • GU infection / STD
  • IBD
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, ESR)
  • stool (culture, O&P, C. diff toxin)
  • blood and urine cultures
  • sexual health (NAAT for gonorrhea / chlamydia, syphilis, HIV)
  • imaging (abdominal US, testicular US with Doppler)
Results
  • WBC 12 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 11.2 g/dL
  • Hct 33.9%
  • platelets 400 ×10⁹/L
  • renal and LFT normal
  • CRP 35 mg/L
  • HIV and STD screen (gonorrhea, chlamydia, syphilis) negative
  • no ova / parasites
  • stool culture Salmonella
  • C. diff negative
  • blood and urine cultures negative
  • abdominal US normal
  • testicular US increased epididymal vascularity = epididymitis, no abscess
Management
  • admit
  • fluids (IV or ORS)
  • ceftriaxone (alternatives ciprofloxacin, TMP-SMX)
  • urology
  • ID / GI if no improvement
14. Dengue with shock (38F, from Najran, back from Hajj)
Clues
  • 2 days fever 40, severe headache with retro-orbital pain, bone pain
  • BP 70/50, HR 120, RR 26, SpO2 92%
  • petechial rash
  • no meningism
DDx
  • viral hemorrhagic fever (dengue, Alkhurma)
  • meningococcal disease
  • viral meningitis
  • measles
Workup
  • CBC with serial hematocrit and platelets
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, ESR)
  • infection (≥ 2 blood cultures, malaria film, dengue NS1 antigen / RT-PCR + IgM / IgG, other viral serology)
  • renal profile + electrolytes
  • liver profile
  • coagulation
  • blood gas + lactate
  • imaging (CXR, US for plasma leakage — effusions, ascites)
  • ECG
Results
  • WBC 14 ×10⁹/L
  • Hb 9 g/dL (90 g/L)
  • hematocrit rise > 20% (plasma leakage)
  • platelets 25 ×10⁹/L
  • AKI
  • acute liver injury (AST > ALT)
  • ESR 300 (as given)
  • blood culture negative
  • CXR clear
  • dengue RT-PCR positive
Management
  • critical care
  • ABC
  • careful fluid resuscitation with hematocrit monitoring
  • correct electrolytes
  • blood products for bleeding
  • avoid NSAIDs / aspirin
  • acetaminophen only
  • supportive care
15. Gonococcal urethritis (32M)
Clues
  • urethral discharge and itch
  • check ulcers, joints, eyes, rash, partners
Workup
  • urethral swab Gram stain (point of care)
  • NAAT for gonorrhea and chlamydia (first-catch urine)
  • urinalysis + urine culture
  • sexual health screen (HIV, syphilis, HBV, HCV)
  • CBC
Results
  • gram-negative intracellular diplococci
  • chlamydia NAAT unavailable
  • HIV negative
Management
  • ceftriaxone (gonorrhea) + doxycycline or azithromycin (chlamydia)
  • abstain / condoms
  • treat partners
  • notify public health
  • repeat HIV test in 6–12 weeks
If ceftriaxone allergy
  • gentamicin + azithromycin (alternatives high-dose azithromycin, FQ if susceptible)
Causes of urethritis
  • N. gonorrhoeae, C. trachomatis, M. genitalium
  • also Trichomonas and HSV
16. Urosepsis → septic shock (70M, DM, IHD, Alzheimer, HR 44)
Clues
  • 2 days fatigue and dizziness, bradycardia
  • then dysuria, right flank pain, fever, GCS drops to 11
DDx
  • BPPV / stroke
  • MI, arrhythmia, orthostasis
  • PE
  • anemia
  • infection (UTI, pneumonia, bed sore, meningitis)
  • hypo / hyperglycemia
  • drugs
ER approach
  • ABCDE, IV access, ECG monitor
  • IV fluids
  • empiric broad-spectrum antibiotics after cultures
Workup
  • blood gas + lactate
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • inflammatory markers (CRP, procalcitonin)
  • renal profile (creatinine, BUN)
  • electrolytes (Na, K)
  • liver profile
  • glucose
  • urine (urinalysis, culture)
  • blood cultures × 2 before antibiotics
  • cardiac (ECG, troponin — HR 44)
  • imaging (CXR, CT brain if GCS drop, renal US / CT for obstruction)
Results
  • leukocytosis, high procalcitonin and lactate
  • perinephric fat stranding → acute pyelonephritis with sepsis (SOFA ≥ 2)
Then
  • BP 80/50, anuric, raised LFT / RFT = septic shock → ICU
  • fluids
  • vasopressors (norepinephrine) to MAP ≥ 65
  • escalate antibiotics
  • ID
Culture
  • ESBL Klebsiella → continue meropenem 10–14 days
  • stop vancomycin
Response markers
  • MAP ≥ 65
  • falling lactate
  • urine output
Questions asked across several stations
Airborne isolation
  • any suspected pulmonary TB until 3 negative sputa
  • also before starting PCP treatment in HIV
RIPE regimen
  • 2 months RIPE + 4 months RI (pleural, peritoneal)
  • 9–12 months + steroids for CNS TB
  • pyridoxine with INH
Pleural / ascitic TB clue
  • lymphocytic exudate with high ADA (> 40)
Brucella regimen
  • doxycycline + rifampicin ± aminoglycoside
  • 6 weeks uncomplicated, ≥ 12 weeks spine, ≥ 6 months endocarditis
HIV and ART timing
  • start within 2 weeks for most OIs
  • delay for cryptococcal and TB meningitis
Sepsis bundle
  • cultures
  • lactate
  • broad-spectrum antibiotics within 1 h
  • 30 mL/kg crystalloid for hypotension
  • vasopressors for MAP < 65
6. Data interpretation

Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

CXR — 34M, fever, dyspnea and cough for 2–3 weeks (case 1)
Findings
  • bilateral, symmetric, perihilar reticular / ground-glass interstitial opacities
  • no effusion, no cavity, no lymphadenopathy
Diagnosis
  • Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (first presentation of HIV) → induced sputum / BAL immunofluorescence
  • LDH
  • ABG
CXR — 34M, same presentation, second station (case 1)
Findings
  • bilateral diffuse perihilar interstitial infiltrates sparing the apices
  • lab sheet: WBC 3.4, LDH 460, PaO2 73 mmHg, HIV positive (viral load 100,000), PCP immunofluorescence positive
Diagnosis
  • PCP in newly diagnosed HIV → TMP-SMX 21 days
  • steroids if PaO2 < 70 mmHg or A-a gradient ≥ 35
  • start ART within 2 weeks
CXR — 39M prison officer, dyspnea for 3 weeks (case 5)
Findings
  • large right pleural effusion — homogeneous opacity of the right mid and lower zones with a meniscus, blunted costophrenic angle
  • left lung clear
Diagnosis
  • pleural effusion → thoracentesis: lymphocytic exudate with high ADA = tuberculous pleuritis
CXR (shielded) — 27F, 12 weeks pregnant, left chest pain 4 weeks (case 6)
Findings
  • left-sided pleural effusion obscuring the left hemidiaphragm and costophrenic angle with a meniscus
  • right lung clear
Diagnosis
  • left pleural effusion → TB pleuritis in pregnancy (RIPE + pyridoxine is safe in pregnancy)
CXR — 44M, a month of headache and fever (case 7)
Findings
  • innumerable tiny (1–3 mm) nodules uniformly distributed through both lungs (arrows)
Diagnosis
  • miliary tuberculosis (with TB meningitis on LP / MRI)
Ziehl-Neelsen stain — same patient (case 7)
Findings
  • slender red (acid-fast) bacilli on a blue background
Diagnosis
  • acid-fast bacilli → Mycobacterium tuberculosis
Gram stain of blood culture — 40M, fever and back pain for 3 weeks (case 10)
Findings
  • small gram-negative coccobacilli
Diagnosis
  • Brucella species (raw milk) → doxycycline + rifampicin (or streptomycin / gentamicin) for spondylitis / sacroiliitis ≥ 12 weeks
Gram stain of blood culture — 30M on a farm, fever and night sweats 3 months, dyspnea (case 11)
Findings
  • numerous tiny gram-negative coccobacilli (culture flagged after prolonged incubation)
Diagnosis
  • Brucella bacteremia → Brucella endocarditis
ECG — same patient (case 11)
Findings
  • prolonged PR interval (first-degree AV block)
  • broad QRS with left bundle branch block pattern
Diagnosis
  • new conduction disease in endocarditis → aortic root (perivalvular) abscess → urgent TEE and cardiac surgery
Contrast CT brain — 68M, fever and headache for 7 days (case 12)
Findings
  • left frontal rounded lesion with a thin
  • smooth
  • complete ring of enhancement
  • low-density center and surrounding edema with mass effect
Diagnosis
  • brain abscess (seeded from a septic prosthetic knee) → neurosurgical aspiration + IV antibiotics
Skin photo — 38F from Najran, fever and headache 2 days (case 14)
Findings
  • diffuse confluent erythematous / petechial rash on the forearm with small islands of spared skin ("white islands in a sea of red")
Diagnosis
  • dengue fever rash → check platelets, hematocrit, NS1 antigen / IgM
  • watch for warning signs and plasma leakage
Gram stain of urethral discharge — 32M, urethral discharge and pruritus (case 15)
Findings
  • many neutrophils containing intracellular gram-negative diplococci
Diagnosis
  • gonococcal urethritis → ceftriaxone 500 mg IM single dose + doxycycline 7 days if chlamydia not excluded
  • treat partners, test for HIV / syphilis
Oral photo — 40M, fever and a bleeding oral lesion (case 3)
Findings
  • violaceous, raised nodular lesion on the hard palate, bleeds easily
  • candidiasis may coexist
Diagnosis
  • Kaposi sarcoma → test for HIV (AIDS-defining, HHV-8)
Legs — same patient (case 3)
Findings
  • multiple violaceous / purple-brown macules
  • plaques and nodules on both lower legs with lymphedema
Diagnosis
  • cutaneous Kaposi sarcoma (AIDS-related) → ART ± chemotherapy (liposomal doxorubicin / paclitaxel) for extensive disease

Neurology

How to use this

The 18 neurology stations split into examination stations (cranial nerves ×3, extrapyramidal ×1) and history stations built around a few syndromes: headache with seizure (CVT ×2, IIH), weakness / fatigability (MG ×2, GBS, stroke / TIA), visual loss (optic neuritis ×2), movement (Parkinson, NMS) and cranial neuropathies (Bell, diabetic 3rd nerve). Localize first, then list causes.

Open every station
  • introduce yourself
  • take permission
  • in acute weakness / seizure / respiratory distress do ABC, glucose, and (stroke) FAST and code stroke first
Stations covered
  • exam (cranial nerves ×3, EPS / Parkinson)
  • headache (CVT ×2, IIH)
  • NMJ (MG, myasthenic crisis)
  • neuropathy (GBS, copper-deficiency myelopathy)
  • vascular (acute stroke with thrombolysis, TIA)
  • eye (optic neuritis / MS ×2, diabetic 3rd nerve palsy)
  • other (Parkinson disease, Bell palsy, NMS)
  • foodborne botulism (extra case file)
1. Core neuro history (every case)
Main symptom
  • onset (sudden vs gradual)
  • duration
  • progression
  • side
  • distribution
  • fluctuation (end of day = MG)
  • previous episodes
Headache red flags (SNOOP)
  • systemic (fever, weight loss, cancer, HIV)
  • neurological deficit
  • onset sudden / thunderclap
  • older age > 50
  • pattern change
  • positional (worse lying / cough)
  • papilledema
  • pregnancy / postpartum
Seizure
  • aura
  • tongue bite
  • incontinence
  • eye rolling
  • duration
  • post-ictal state
  • Todd paralysis
  • first or recurrent
Vision
  • acuity loss
  • central vs peripheral
  • pain on eye movement
  • color desaturation
  • diplopia (monocular vs binocular, which gaze)
  • flashes
  • transient obscurations
Motor / sensory
  • weakness pattern (proximal vs distal, ascending vs descending)
  • numbness
  • sensory level
  • gait
  • falls
Bulbar and respiratory
  • dysarthria
  • dysphagia
  • nasal speech
  • choking
  • SOB
  • orthopnea
Autonomic
  • postural dizziness
  • constipation
  • urinary retention / incontinence
  • impotence
  • sweating
Cognition and mood
  • memory
  • hallucinations
  • sleep (REM behavior)
  • anosmia
Systemic
  • fever
  • rash
  • joint pain
  • oral / genital ulcers (CTD, Behçet)
  • weight loss
  • cough (TB, cancer)
Past history
  • stroke / TIA
  • DM
  • HTN
  • AF
  • IHD
  • malignancy
  • thrombosis
  • miscarriages (APS)
  • autoimmune disease
  • thyroid
  • recent surgery or infection
  • vaccination
Drugs
  • OCP
  • anticoagulants
  • antiplatelets
  • antipsychotics / metoclopramide (parkinsonism, NMS)
  • antibiotics and β-blockers (worsen MG)
  • retinoids / tetracycline / steroids (IIH)
  • chemo / isoniazid (neuropathy)
  • zinc supplements (copper deficiency)
Family and social
  • stroke, MS, epilepsy, thrombophilia, neuromuscular disease
  • smoking, alcohol, illicit drugs, occupation, driving
2. History add-ons by presentation
A. Headache ± seizure in a young woman
CVT clues
  • OCP, pregnancy / postpartum, previous DVT, miscarriages, dehydration, sinusitis / mastoiditis, IBD, cancer
  • headache worse lying and with cough
  • vomiting, blurred vision, seizure, 6th nerve palsy
IIH clues
  • obesity
  • transient visual obscurations
  • pulsatile tinnitus
  • diplopia
  • drugs (retinoids, tetracycline, steroids, vitamin A, lithium)
  • PCOS
  • hypothyroidism
SAH clues
  • thunderclap onset
  • neck stiffness
  • collapse
Infection clues
  • fever
  • neck stiffness
  • rash
  • travel
B. Diplopia / ptosis / fatigable weakness
MG
  • worse at end of day, better after rest
  • ptosis
  • proximal weakness
  • bulbar and respiratory symptoms
Triggers for crisis
  • infection
  • surgery
  • stress
  • drugs (aminoglycosides, quinolones, macrolides, β-blockers, procainamide, magnesium, phenytoin)
Lambert-Eaton
  • smoker
  • weight loss
  • proximal weakness improving with use
  • dry mouth
3rd nerve palsy
  • pain
  • pupil involvement
  • DM / HTN
  • headache (aneurysm)
  • jaw claudication (GCA)
C. Acute weakness
Stroke / TIA
  • time last seen well, FAST features, AF, prior stroke
  • thrombolysis contraindications (recent surgery, GI bleed in 21 days, prior ICH, head trauma, anticoagulants, AVM / tumor)
GBS
  • ascending, symmetric, preceded by diarrhea (Campylobacter), URTI or vaccination
  • back pain
  • facial / bulbar weakness
  • dyspnea
  • autonomic instability
Cord
  • back pain
  • sensory level
  • bladder / bowel involvement
Myopathy / NMJ / metabolic
  • low K
  • low PO4
  • botulism
  • snake bite
D. Visual loss
Optic neuritis
  • subacute, painful eye movement, color desaturation, central scotoma
  • Lhermitte sign
  • prior episodes of numbness / weakness
  • family history of MS
NMO
  • severe bilateral loss
  • transverse myelitis
  • intractable hiccups / vomiting
E. Tremor / parkinsonism
PD
  • asymmetric resting tremor, slowness, stiffness, falls, micrographia, soft voice
  • non-motor (anosmia, constipation, REM sleep disorder, depression, orthostasis)
Secondary / plus
  • antidopaminergic drugs
  • strokes
  • early falls (PSP)
  • early autonomic failure (MSA)
  • hallucinations / early dementia (DLB)
Essential tremor
  • action tremor
  • family history
  • better with alcohol
F. Fever + rigidity + confusion
NMS
  • recent antipsychotic start / dose increase
  • rigidity, hyperthermia, autonomic instability, confusion
Mimics
  • serotonin syndrome (SSRIs, clonus)
  • malignant hyperthermia (anesthesia)
  • CNS infection
  • thyroid storm
  • drugs (cocaine, amphetamines, lithium)
  • withdrawal
G. Ataxia / numbness
Sensory ataxia
  • falls in the dark
  • positive Romberg
  • numb feet
Causes
  • B12 / copper deficiency (bariatric surgery, celiac, zinc excess)
  • alcohol
  • DM
  • paraneoplastic (smoker, weight loss)
  • Sjögren
  • syphilis
  • HIV
  • cervical spondylosis
  • drugs
3. Cranial nerve examination (examined ×3)
CN I
  • ask about smell change
  • test each nostril with a non-irritant (coffee)
CN II
  • pupils (size, shape, direct and consensual reflex, RAPD)
  • acuity per eye with chart
  • red desaturation
  • visual fields in 4 quadrants per eye
  • mention fundoscopy
CN III, IV, VI
  • inspect for ptosis and squint
  • H-pattern pursuit asking about diplopia
  • comment on restriction and nystagmus
  • accommodation
CN V
  • sensation in V1, V2, V3
  • clench teeth (masseter, temporalis)
  • jaw opening against resistance (pterygoids)
  • jaw jerk
  • mention corneal reflex
CN VII
  • ask about taste and hyperacusis
  • inspect asymmetry and nasolabial folds
  • raise eyebrows, close eyes tight, puff cheeks, show teeth (forehead spared = UMN)
CN VIII
  • whisper test
  • Rinne and Weber
CN IX, X
  • palate and uvula on "ahh"
  • cough
  • swallow / gag
CN XI
  • inspect SCM and trapezius
  • shrug shoulders and turn head against resistance
CN XII
  • tongue at rest for wasting and fasciculation
  • protrusion (deviates to weak side)
  • push into cheek
Close
  • thank patient
  • summarize
  • suggest further tests
4. Extrapyramidal (Parkinson) examination
Inspection
  • hypomimia
  • reduced blinking
  • drooling
  • hypophonic monotone speech
  • stooped flexed posture
  • asymmetric resting pill-rolling tremor
Gait
  • start hesitation, shuffling short steps, reduced arm swing, festination, en-bloc turning
  • pull test for postural instability
Upper limbs
  • rigidity (cogwheel / lead-pipe, use reinforcement)
  • bradykinesia with decrement (finger tapping, fist opening, pronation-supination)
  • tremor re-emerging with distraction, settling with action
  • writing (micrographia)
Lower limbs
  • tone
  • heel / foot tapping
  • power and reflexes normal (no pyramidal signs)
Exclude Parkinson-plus
  • lying-standing BP (MSA)
  • cognition (DLB)
  • cerebellar signs (MSA-C)
  • vertical gaze palsy (PSP)
  • apraxia, asymmetric dystonia (CBD)
  • early falls
  • poor levodopa response
Cardinal features
  • tremor
  • rigidity
  • bradykinesia
  • postural instability
PD vs essential tremor
  • PD = resting, asymmetric, with rigidity and bradykinesia
  • ET = action / postural, bilateral, better with alcohol
5. Core neuro exam and add-ons
General
  • vitals (BP, temp, glucose)
  • GCS
  • orientation
  • signs of trauma
  • skin rash
  • joints
Higher function and speech
  • dysphasia
  • dysarthria
  • scanning speech
Meningism
  • neck stiffness
  • Kernig
  • Brudzinski
Fundi
  • papilledema (CVT, IIH)
  • optic disc swelling or pallor (optic neuritis)
  • retinopathy
Motor
  • bulk
  • fasciculation
  • tone
  • power (proximal vs distal)
  • reflexes
  • plantars
Sensory
  • light touch
  • pinprick
  • vibration
  • joint position
  • sensory level
Coordination and gait
  • finger-nose
  • heel-shin
  • dysdiadochokinesia
  • Romberg
  • tandem
MG add-ons
  • sustained upgaze for 60 s (fatigable ptosis)
  • repeated arm abduction
  • count to 50
  • ice-pack test
  • bedside FVC / NIF
GBS add-ons
  • areflexia
  • symmetric distal-to-proximal weakness
  • facial / bulbar weakness
  • FVC / NIF
  • HR and BP lability
Stroke add-ons
  • NIHSS
  • glucose
  • AF on pulse
  • carotid bruit
  • murmur
NMS add-ons
  • temperature
  • lead-pipe rigidity
  • autonomic lability
  • GCS
  • no clonus (vs serotonin syndrome)
6. Case scenarios

Each case: stem → clues that decide it → key results → diagnosis → management → examiner's extra questions.

Headache, seizure and raised pressure
1. Cerebral venous thrombosis with hemorrhage (24F, OCP, first seizure)
Clues
  • sudden severe right temporo-occipital headache 1 day, worse lying and coughing, vomiting, blurred vision
  • first GTC seizure
  • married 4 months on OCP
  • prior unprovoked DVT
  • right 6th nerve palsy
DDx
  • SAH
  • meningitis
  • CVT
  • RCVS
  • ICH
Workup
  • CBC
  • coagulation (PT / INR, aPTT)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca, Mg) + glucose
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • blood gas
  • pregnancy test
  • infection (cultures, ESR, CRP)
  • autoimmune (ANA, ANCA, C3 / C4)
  • thrombophilia (factor V Leiden, prothrombin G20210A, protein C / S, antithrombin — after the acute phase)
  • APS antibodies
  • imaging (CT + CT venogram, MRI + MR venogram)
  • cancer screen if indicated
Results
  • right transverse sinus thrombosis with small hemorrhage and edema
Management
  • ABCD
  • manage raised ICP (head up, mannitol)
  • analgesia and hydration
  • antiseizure drug
  • anticoagulate (LMWH / UFH, even with hemorrhage) then oral anticoagulation
  • stop OCP
  • neurology
Risk factors
  • inherited thrombophilia (factor V Leiden, prothrombin mutation, protein C / S, antithrombin deficiency)
  • acquired (OCP, pregnancy / puerperium, APS, cancer, infection, head injury, dehydration, IBD)
2. Superior sagittal sinus thrombosis postpartum (37F, known migraine)
Clues
  • 2 months postpartum
  • severe frontal throbbing headache different from usual migraine
  • vomiting, photophobia
  • normal exam and normal CT
DDx
  • CVT
  • migraine
  • meningitis
  • SAH / hemorrhagic infarct
  • mass
  • post-traumatic hemorrhage
Workup
  • imaging (CT → MRI with MR / CT venography)
  • fundoscopy
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • renal profile + electrolytes
  • thrombophilia and APS screen (after acute phase)
  • LP only if meningitis suspected and no raised ICP
Result
  • superior sagittal sinus thrombosis on CT venogram
Management
  • admit, ABC, analgesia
  • LMWH / UFH then warfarin / DOAC for 3–6 months (continue even with hemorrhagic infarct)
  • antiseizure drugs if seizures
  • no steroids
  • ICP measures
  • endovascular therapy if deteriorating
  • avoid estrogen contraception
Examiner Qs
  • commonest long-term problem = chronic headache
  • seizures 10–15%
  • mortality 5–10%, about 80% recover
  • CVT vs SAH = subacute headache with seizures / deficits and risk factors vs thunderclap with meningism and positive early CT
3. Idiopathic intracranial hypertension (30F, bilateral blurred vision + headache)
History
  • visual obscurations, pulsatile tinnitus, diplopia
  • headache worse lying / morning
  • drugs (retinoids, tetracycline, steroids, lithium, vitamin A)
  • thrombosis risk
  • hypothyroid symptoms
Workup
  • CBC + iron studies
  • renal profile
  • electrolytes
  • endocrine (TFT, cortisol, vitamin D, lipids)
  • ANA
  • drug review (tetracyclines, vitamin A, retinoids, OCP, steroid withdrawal)
  • imaging (CT → MRI brain + MRV to exclude CVT)
  • LP with opening pressure (> 25 cmH2O) and normal CSF
  • ophthalmology (fundoscopy, OCT, automated perimetry)
DDx
  • IIH
  • mass (tumor, abscess)
  • CVT
  • MS
  • SLE / vasculitis
  • hypothyroidism
  • PCOS
Results
  • slit ventricles, flattened posterior globes, distended optic nerve sheaths, tortuous optic nerves, empty sella
  • papilledema
  • enlarged blind spot
Management
  • education
  • weight loss
  • acetazolamide (± furosemide / topiramate)
  • serial LPs if needed
  • steroids short term only
If vision worsens
  • optic nerve sheath fenestration or CSF shunt (LP / VP)
  • neurosurgery
Neuromuscular junction
4. Myasthenia gravis → cholinergic crisis (65M, DM, hypothyroid)
Clues
  • 1 month binocular diplopia at end of day, right ptosis, difficulty climbing stairs, better after sleep
  • 40 pack-years
DDx
  • MG
  • Lambert-Eaton
  • botulism
  • diabetic cranial neuropathy
  • hypothyroid myopathy
Workup
  • CBC
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca, Mg) + glucose
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • metabolic / endocrine (TFT, HbA1c)
  • antibodies (AChR, MuSK, LRP4; VGCC if LEMS suspected)
  • neurophysiology (repetitive nerve stimulation, single-fiber EMG)
  • bedside (ice-pack test, FVC / NIF)
  • cardiac (ECG)
  • imaging (CXR, CT / MRI chest for thymoma)
  • cancer screen
Results
  • fatigable bilateral ptosis improved by ice pack
  • AChR antibody positive
  • > 10% decrement on RNS, jitter on SFEMG
  • no thymoma
Management
  • ABC
  • pyridostigmine for symptoms
  • prednisone then steroid-sparing (azathioprine, MMF, rituximab)
  • thymectomy if thymoma
  • crisis (dyspnea, dysphagia) = ICU, IVIG or plasma exchange
  • neurology
Cholinergic crisis
  • after extra pyridostigmine → weakness plus diarrhea, abdominal pain, salivation, bradycardia, miosis
  • give atropine and reduce / stop pyridostigmine
5. Myasthenic crisis after surgery (71M, prostate surgery 10 days ago)
Clues
  • recent diplopia investigated by GP
  • progressive dyspnea, pooled oral secretions, accessory muscle use, cannot maintain SpO2
  • proximal weakness 3/5
  • IHD
DDx
  • MG
  • GBS / Miller Fisher
  • MS
  • ALS
  • botulism
  • brainstem stroke / tumor
  • Lambert-Eaton
  • severe low K / PO4
  • snake envenomation
  • COPD / pneumonia
Workup
  • bedside respiratory (FVC, NIF, single-breath count)
  • blood gas
  • CBC
  • coagulation
  • renal profile
  • electrolytes (K, Mg, PO4, Ca)
  • liver profile
  • glucose
  • infection (ESR, CRP, procalcitonin, blood / urine cultures, urinalysis, CXR)
  • cardiac (ECG)
  • antibodies (AChR, MuSK)
  • NCS with RNS
  • imaging (CT / MRI head and chest)
Results
  • on face mask FiO2 0.4 — pH 7.32
  • PaO2 12.4 kPa (≈ 93 mmHg)
  • PaCO2 5.9 kPa (≈ 44 mmHg — normal value in a tiring patient = danger)
  • HCO3 28 mmol/L
  • lactate 1.2 mmol/L
  • BE +4.9
  • K 4.6 mmol/L
Management
  • ICU
  • ABC
  • VC and MIP every 2 h, intubate if VC < 20 mL/kg or MIP weaker than −30 cmH2O
  • hold pyridostigmine while ventilated
  • IVIG or plasma exchange
  • treat trigger
  • neurology
  • DVT and GI prophylaxis
Crisis definition
  • MG exacerbation causing neuromuscular respiratory failure
Triggers
  • infection
  • surgery
  • stress
  • drugs (aminoglycosides, FQ, macrolides, β-blockers, anesthetics)
Complications
  • respiratory failure
  • arrhythmia
  • aspiration pneumonia
  • stress ulcer
  • VTE
  • pressure sores
  • death
Weakness and neuropathy
6. Guillain-Barré syndrome (AIDP) after vaccination (27M)
Clues
  • 2 days ascending leg numbness and weakness
  • vaccination 1 month ago
  • absent ankle reflexes, foot drop
DDx
  • GBS
  • transverse myelitis
  • cord compression
  • infectious neuropathy (HIV, CMV, botulism)
  • vascular myelopathy
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • electrolytes (K, Mg, PO4)
  • metabolic / endocrine (B12, TFT, glucose / HbA1c, CK)
  • autoimmune and infection (ANA, HIV, hepatitis, stool Campylobacter, anti-ganglioside antibodies — GQ1b for Miller Fisher)
  • LP (protein, cells, glucose)
  • neurophysiology (NCS / EMG)
  • imaging (MRI spine)
  • respiratory (FVC, NIF)
Results
  • high CSF protein with normal cells
  • slowed conduction and prolonged F waves
  • normal MRI
Management
  • monitored bed
  • FVC, NIF, SpO2 every 4–6 h
  • intubate if FVC < 15–20 mL/kg or NIF weaker than −30
  • DVT prophylaxis, bladder / bowel / skin care
  • IVIG 0.4 g/kg/day × 5 days or plasma exchange × 5
  • no steroids
  • physio, OT, rehab
Causes
  • Campylobacter (commonest)
  • CMV
  • EBV
  • HIV
  • influenza
  • Mycoplasma
  • Zika
  • vaccination
  • surgery
ICU if
  • rapid progression
  • bulbar weakness
  • falling FVC / NIF
  • autonomic instability
Prognosis
  • about 80% recover fully
  • 5–10% residual deficit
  • mortality ≈ 5%
Variants
  • AIDP
  • AMAN
  • AMSAN
  • Miller Fisher (ophthalmoplegia, ataxia, areflexia; anti-GQ1b)
  • Bickerstaff encephalitis
7. Copper-deficiency myelopathy from celiac disease (55M, 80 pack-years)
Clues
  • 3 months progressive imbalance, falls, cane
  • numb feet and fingertips
  • 11 kg weight loss
  • cachexia
  • positive Romberg
  • absent vibration and joint position in legs
  • mild hip / knee flexion weakness
  • brisk arm reflexes
  • flexor plantars
Localization
  • dorsal columns ± lateral columns of the spinal cord (sensory ataxia with pyramidal signs)
DDx
  • paraneoplastic neuronopathy / myelopathy
  • CIDP / Sjögren / demyelination
  • neoplasm
  • B12 / copper deficiency
  • cervical spondylosis
  • toxins (pyridoxine excess, chemo)
  • infections (syphilis, HIV, CMV)
Workup
  • CBC (MCV)
  • vitamins and trace elements (B12 + methylmalonic acid if low-normal, folate, vitamin E, copper, ceruloplasmin, zinc)
  • celiac serology (TTG-IgA)
  • SPEP with immunofixation
  • infection (HIV, syphilis)
  • metabolic (HbA1c)
  • autoimmune (ANA, anti-DNA)
  • paraneoplastic antibodies + cancer screen (80 pack-years)
  • neurophysiology (NCS / EMG, SSEP)
  • imaging (MRI spine)
  • CSF analysis
Results
  • subtle dorsal column T2 signal
  • abnormal SSEPs
  • microcytic anemia
  • B12 normal 573 pg/mL (≈ 423 pmol/L; normal 211–946) → copper and zinc checked (copper low)
  • TTG very high
  • duodenal biopsy villous atrophy
Diagnosis
  • subacute combined degeneration-like myelopathy from copper deficiency due to celiac disease
Causes of copper deficiency
  • gastric bypass / gastrectomy
  • IBD / celiac / CF
  • zinc excess (supplements, denture cream)
  • chelators
  • long TPN
  • poor diet
Management
  • IV then oral copper
  • gluten-free diet
  • iron replacement
Vascular
8. Acute ischemic stroke in a young woman — right M1 occlusion (25F)
Approach
  • FAST → code stroke
  • ABC
  • BP monitor
  • glucose
  • ECG
  • two 18G lines
  • urgent CT to exclude hemorrhage
  • history and exam in parallel
History
  • last seen well (90 min)
  • seizure / syncope
  • head trauma, prior stroke or ICH, tumor / AVM / aneurysm, intracranial or spinal surgery, GI bleed in 21 days, recent major surgery
  • family history
  • autoimmune / hematological disease
Exam
  • GCS
  • cranial nerves
  • motor
  • sensory
  • gait
  • NIHSS
Workup
  • bedside glucose first
  • imaging (non-contrast CT → CT angiography ± perfusion)
  • CBC
  • coagulation (INR, aPTT)
  • renal profile (before contrast)
  • electrolytes
  • troponin
  • ECG
  • pregnancy test
  • young-stroke screen later (echo with bubble, Holter, thrombophilia — protein C / S, antithrombin, lupus anticoagulant, anticardiolipin; toxicology; autoimmune — ANA, dsDNA, ANCA; HbA1c, lipids)
Result
  • NIHSS 10
  • CTA right M1 MCA occlusion = large-vessel occlusion
Management
  • IV alteplase 0.9 mg/kg (max 90 mg; 10% bolus, rest over 1 h) + mechanical thrombectomy
First 24 h
  • ICU / stroke unit
  • BP < 180/105 after lysis (< 185/110 before)
  • neuro checks every 15 min × 2 h, 30 min × 6 h, hourly × 16 h
  • no antithrombotics for 24 h
  • avoid lines / Foley if possible
  • pneumatic compression
  • repeat CT if headache or deterioration
Young stroke workup
  • echo ± TEE (PFO)
  • carotid / vertebral Doppler
  • MRI / MRA brain and neck
  • thrombophilia and APS screen
  • vasculitis screen
9. TIA (52M, right hemiparesis for 20 minutes)
History
  • onset
  • duration
  • speech / facial / sensory symptoms
  • previous episodes
  • head trauma
  • HTN
  • DM
  • AF
  • IHD
  • stroke
  • anticoagulants
  • smoking
Exam
  • vitals
  • consciousness
  • cranial nerves
  • motor
  • sensory
  • cerebellar
  • NIHSS
Workup
  • imaging (MRI with DWI or CT; CTA / MRA head and neck; carotid Doppler)
  • CBC
  • renal profile + electrolytes
  • coagulation
  • glucose
  • metabolic (HbA1c, lipid profile, TSH)
  • cardiac (ECG, troponin, Holter / prolonged monitoring, echo)
Results
  • HbA1c 9% (75 mmol/mol)
  • LDL 100 mg/dL (2.6 mmol/L)
  • CT and MRI no infarct
ABCD2
  • Age ≥ 60 (1)
  • BP ≥ 140/90 (1)
  • Clinical — weakness 2, speech only 1
  • Duration ≥ 60 min 2, 10–59 min 1
  • Diabetes 1 → here 5 = moderate risk
Management
  • admit
  • DAPT (aspirin + clopidogrel) for 21 days then single agent
  • high-intensity statin (atorvastatin 80)
  • BP < 140/90
  • HbA1c < 7%
  • stop smoking, diet, exercise
Anticoagulate instead if
  • AF or other cardioembolic source
TIA vs minor stroke
  • TIA resolves (usually within minutes–hours) with no infarct on imaging
  • minor stroke persists or shows infarct
Carotid revascularization
  • symptomatic 70–99%
  • symptomatic 50–69% with high-risk features
  • asymptomatic 70–99% with life expectancy ≥ 5 years
Crescendo TIA
  • repeated TIAs with increasing frequency — unstable plaque or cardioembolic source
  • urgent workup
Eye and cranial nerves
10. Optic neuritis from multiple sclerosis (30M; 32F — two stations)
Clues
  • subacute monocular blurred vision ± pain on movement, flashes
  • tingling in right arm and leg over a month
  • fatigue
  • aunt with MS
Exam
  • acuity, color vision, RAPD, visual fields (central scotoma), fundoscopy
  • other cranial nerves, motor, sensory, cerebellar, gait
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile + electrolytes
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP)
  • vitamin D
  • mimics (ANA, ANCA, cryoglobulins, syphilis, B12)
  • antibodies (AQP4-IgG, MOG-IgG)
  • imaging (MRI brain + orbits + whole spine with gadolinium)
  • LP (cells, protein, oligoclonal bands with paired serum)
  • neurophysiology (visual evoked potentials)
  • ophthalmology (OCT, visual fields)
Results
  • CBC, renal, LFT normal
  • ESR 33 mm/h
  • vitamin D low
  • ANA, ANCA, cryoglobulins negative
  • MRI periventricular / juxtacortical white-matter lesions
  • CSF raised protein with oligoclonal bands (not in serum)
  • delayed P100 on VEP
  • AQP4 negative
DDx
  • MS optic neuritis
  • NMO
  • vasculitis
  • B12 deficiency
Typical MS MRI regions
  • periventricular
  • cortical / juxtacortical
  • infratentorial
  • spinal cord
Treat relapse if disabling
  • vision loss
  • diplopia
  • weakness
  • cerebellar symptoms
Management
  • neurology
  • exclude infection
  • IV methylprednisolone pulse
  • calcium / vitamin D
  • vaccinations
  • education
  • disease-modifying therapy (natalizumab, ocrelizumab, dimethyl fumarate, fingolimod; older interferon, glatiramer)
  • symptomatic care (fatigue, spasticity, pain, bladder, mood)
MS phenotypes
  • clinically isolated syndrome
  • relapsing-remitting (commonest)
  • secondary progressive
  • primary progressive
11. Bell palsy (34F, sudden right facial weakness)
Clues
  • 1 day right facial weakness incl. forehead, can't close eye, hyperacusis
  • URTI a week ago
  • on OCP
  • isolated right LMN 7th palsy
Red flags to ask
  • ear vesicles / pain (Ramsay Hunt)
  • other deficits
  • gradual onset
  • bilateral
  • recurrent
  • hearing loss / vertigo
DDx
  • Bell palsy
  • Ramsay Hunt
  • stroke (forehead spared)
  • MS
  • CP-angle tumor
  • basal meningitis
  • sarcoid
  • CTD
  • Lyme
Workup
  • clinical diagnosis
  • basic labs (CBC, renal profile, liver profile, electrolytes, glucose, HbA1c, ESR / CRP)
  • infection (Lyme serology if endemic, HIV, VZV if vesicles)
  • autoimmune if CTD suspected (ANA, ANCA, complement)
  • imaging (MRI with gadolinium only if red flags or no recovery by 3–4 months)
Management
  • prednisolone within 72 h (± antiviral)
  • eye care (lubricant, tape / patch at night)
  • analgesia
  • facial physiotherapy
  • neurology
Risk factors
  • DM
  • HTN
  • obesity
  • pregnancy (3rd trimester) and early postpartum
  • recent viral infection
Prognosis
  • 80–90% recover in 6 weeks to 3 months
  • ≈ 40% complete recovery if ≥ 60 years
Sequelae
  • exposure keratitis
  • synkinesis
  • taste loss
  • cosmetic asymmetry
12. Pupil-sparing diabetic 3rd nerve palsy (65M, DM, HTN, prior stroke)
Clues
  • acute painful diplopia with left ptosis
  • eye down and out
  • pupil spared
  • HbA1c 11%
  • BP 165/90
DDx
  • diabetic (microvascular) 3rd nerve palsy
  • PComm aneurysm
  • mass
  • MG
  • giant cell arteritis
Workup
  • CBC
  • renal profile
  • liver profile
  • electrolytes
  • metabolic (glucose, HbA1c, lipid profile)
  • inflammatory markers (ESR, CRP — giant cell arteritis if > 50)
  • imaging (CTA / MRA to exclude posterior communicating aneurysm, MRI brain / orbits)
  • visual fields
  • MG tests if fluctuating
Results
  • HbA1c 11% (97 mmol/mol)
  • CTA / MRA no aneurysm
Management
  • strict glucose (HbA1c < 7%), BP, lipids, secondary stroke prevention
  • analgesia
  • patch or prisms
  • reassure — recovers in 3–5 months
  • return if pupil dilates, pain worsens or new deficits
Mechanism
  • ischemia of central fibers
  • pupillary fibers run peripherally (pial supply) and are spared
Ischemic vs compressive
  • ischemic = pupil spared, recovers
  • compressive (PComm aneurysm, tumor) = dilated fixed pupil, progressive, emergency
Cavernous sinus nerves
  • III
  • IV
  • V1
  • V2
  • VI
Other causes of cranial neuropathies
  • DM
  • HTN
  • vasculitis
  • sarcoid
  • MS
  • TB
  • syphilis
Movement and toxicity
13. Idiopathic Parkinson disease (62F, hand tremor)
Clues
  • years of shoulder pain and anosmia
  • right resting tremor 2 years then left
  • slow gait, stiffness, recent falls
  • no antidopaminergic drugs
Exam
  • hypomimia, unintelligible speech, bilateral pill-rolling tremor, rigidity R > L, bradykinesia, flexed shuffling gait
  • normal eye movements
  • no ataxia
  • no orthostatic drop
DDx
  • idiopathic PD
  • essential tremor
  • PSP
  • MSA
  • secondary parkinsonism
Workup
  • clinical diagnosis
  • exclude secondary causes (CBC, electrolytes, glucose, renal profile, liver profile, TFT, B12; ceruloplasmin if < 40 years)
  • drug review (antipsychotics, metoclopramide)
  • imaging (MRI brain; DaT scan if unclear)
Management
  • neurology
  • levodopa-carbidopa
  • dopamine agonists
  • MAO-B inhibitors
  • COMT inhibitors
  • amantadine
  • anticholinergics (young, tremor)
Dopamine agonist side effects
  • impulse control disorders
  • sleep attacks
  • hallucinations / confusion
  • nausea
  • leg edema
Levodopa side effects
  • wearing-off
  • dyskinesia
  • hallucinations
  • nausea
  • orthostatic hypotension
Pathology
  • loss of dopaminergic neurons in substantia nigra pars compacta with Lewy bodies (α-synuclein)
14. Neuroleptic malignant syndrome (75M, schizophrenia, dose increased last week)
Clues
  • found febrile and immobile, not recognizing son, urine-soaked
  • T 40, HR 120, BP 160/90, RR 26
  • catatonic
  • lead-pipe rigidity
DDx
  • NMS
  • CNS infection
  • malignant hyperthermia
  • serotonin syndrome
  • malignant catatonia
  • CNS vasculitis
  • thyroid storm
  • drug toxicity (cocaine, amphetamines, ecstasy, lithium)
  • withdrawal
Workup
  • CBC (leukocytosis)
  • muscle injury (CK, LDH, AST, urine myoglobin)
  • renal profile (creatinine, BUN — AKI)
  • electrolytes (Na, K, Ca, PO4, Mg)
  • blood gas + lactate (metabolic acidosis)
  • septic screen (blood / urine cultures, CXR)
  • TFT
  • toxicology
  • drug levels (lithium)
  • imaging (CT brain)
  • LP if meningitis / encephalitis possible
Results
  • temperature 40 °C (104 °F)
  • BP 160/90 mmHg
  • HR 120/min
  • RR 26/min
  • rigid, mute
  • expected CK markedly raised with leukocytosis
Management
  • ICU
  • stop the antipsychotic
  • cardiorespiratory support
  • IV fluids and electrolytes
  • cooling
  • benzodiazepines
  • dantrolene, bromocriptine or amantadine
  • BP control (clonidine / nitroprusside)
  • DVT prophylaxis
Toxin-mediated weakness (extra case file)
15. Foodborne botulism (28M, sausage pasta at a restaurant)
Clues
  • 1 day of nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, diarrhea, then sudden bilateral symmetric descending weakness (cranial nerves → trunk and arms → legs)
  • blurred vision, diplopia, ptosis, dysphagia, dysarthria, facial weakness, fixed dilated pupils possible
  • urinary retention and constipation
  • alert with normal sensation
  • afebrile
History asks
  • food eaten (home-canned, sausage, fermented fish), others affected
  • wound or injected drug use
  • cosmetic botulinum toxin
  • dyspnea (diaphragm)
  • fever (usually absent)
  • vaccines
  • drugs
Exam
  • vitals and respiratory effort (single-breath count, FVC)
  • cranial nerves
  • tone and power (flaccid, descending)
  • reflexes normal or reduced
  • sensation normal
  • mental status normal
  • abdomen
DDx
  • myasthenia gravis
  • Guillain-Barré (Miller Fisher variant — ascending, areflexic, sensory, high CSF protein)
  • brainstem stroke
  • tick paralysis
  • heavy metal / organophosphate poisoning
  • poliomyelitis
  • Lambert-Eaton
Workup
  • CBC normal
  • electrolytes, glucose, CK
  • CSF normal (vs GBS)
  • EMG / NCS — reduced compound muscle action potential amplitude with increment on high-frequency stimulation
  • imaging (CT / MRI brain normal)
  • confirmation (toxin assay / culture of serum, stool, gastric fluid and the suspected food)
Management
  • ICU with serial FVC
  • intubation for respiratory failure
  • give heptavalent equine botulinum antitoxin (BAT, one vial IV) on clinical suspicion — do not wait for confirmation
  • infants get human botulism immune globulin
  • notify public health (outbreak)
  • supportive care
Questions asked across several stations
Intubation thresholds (MG / GBS)
  • FVC < 15–20 mL/kg
  • NIF weaker than −30 cmH2O
  • rising PaCO2
  • bulbar failure
IVIG vs plasma exchange
  • both work in MG crisis and GBS
  • steroids don't help GBS
Anticoagulate CVT even with hemorrhage.
    Thrombolysis window
    • alteplase within 4.5 h of last seen well
    • thrombectomy for large-vessel occlusion (up to 24 h with imaging selection)
    Drugs that worsen MG
    • aminoglycosides
    • fluoroquinolones
    • macrolides
    • β-blockers
    • magnesium
    • procainamide
    • neuromuscular blockers
    7. Data interpretation

    Images from the checklists, grouped by station, with the finding and the diagnosis.

    Non-contrast CT brain — 24F on OCP, headache and first seizure (case 1)
    Findings
    • rounded hyperdensity in the posterior interhemispheric / parasagittal occipital region at the superior sagittal sinus (hyperdense thrombosed sinus — "dense triangle" — with adjacent venous hemorrhage)
    Diagnosis
    • suspected cerebral venous thrombosis → confirm with MR / CT venography
    MR venogram — same patient (case 1)
    Findings
    • absent flow signal in the right transverse–sigmoid sinus (arrow)
    • left transverse sinus and superior sagittal sinus fill
    Diagnosis
    • cerebral venous sinus thrombosis → anticoagulate with LMWH even with hemorrhage
    • stop OCP
    • thrombophilia screen
    Fundoscopy — 30F, bilateral blurred vision and headache (case 3)
    Findings
    • bilateral swollen optic discs with blurred margins
    • obscured vessels
    • peripapillary flame hemorrhages and exudates
    Diagnosis
    • bilateral papilledema (raised intracranial pressure)
    MRI brain, sagittal T2 — same patient (case 3)
    Findings
    • flattened pituitary in a CSF-filled sella (partially empty sella)
    • no mass, no hydrocephalus
    Diagnosis
